- [s_setkill](../../core/shader/recompiler/RdnaDecoder/src/RdnaScalarOpDecoder.cpp) (core/shader/recompiler) - decoded as `s_endpgm` for any immediate: measured on RDNA2 hardware (gfx1035), a store before it is visible and nothing after it runs ([#771](https://github.com/boykopovar/AnyPS5/pull/771)); whether the KILL status bit it sets is observable differently from `s_endpgm` is unverified
- [sceRtcParseDateTime](../../core/libs/prx/libSceRtc/Export.cpp) (libSceRtc) - the console's zone name table is only known from the names measured on PS5 firmware 13.60 and 13.42; any other name is assumed to take the military letter offset of its first character, as every measured name outside the table does (`HST`, `MSK`, `XYZ`), so a name the console's table holds but nobody measured gets the wrong offset
- [sceAudio3dPortOpen](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudio3d/Export.cpp) (libSceAudio3d) - buffer modes 0 and 1 (also selected by the 0x10 and 0x18 parameter sizes) and 3 beds depend on an SDK version check in the module ([prosper](https://github.com/mattias800/prosper)) and throw. A repeated `sceAudio3dInitialize` (`NOT_READY` in shadPS4, 0 in prosper) and `sceAudio3dPortAdvance` on a full queue (`NOT_READY` in shadPS4, unhandled in KytyPS5) throw too
- [sceAudiodecDecode](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudiodec/src/Codecs.cpp) (libSceAudiodec) - a raw M4AAC stream carries no channel configuration, so the decoder tries the mono/stereo configuration and then configurations 3 to 7 within `ui_max_channels` until one decodes the first access unit, and keeps that one. No reference documents how the library picks the layout (shadPS4 maps the channel count straight to the configuration; SharpProspero only describes the maximum). With the stereo configuration a mono raw stream decodes as 2 channels, so 2 is reported, not 1
- [sceAudiodecDecode](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudiodec/src/Codecs.cpp) (libSceAudiodec) - the HE-AAC handling follows one console measurement of an HE-AAC v2 ADTS stream: SBR output at 48 kHz in one channel with `ui_enable_heaac` 1, the 24 kHz mono core with 0. Not measured: whether the console, like FFmpeg, decodes a stream without SBR data in its first access unit as AAC-LC with `ui_enable_heaac` 1, and what it reports in `ui_sampling_freq` and `ui_heaac` with `ui_enable_heaac` 0 (here the rate of the returned PCM and 0)
- [sceAudio3dObjectReserve](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudio3d/Export.cpp) (libSceAudio3d) - object ids, the `max_objects` limit and the checks of `sceAudio3dObjectReserve` and `sceAudio3dObjectUnreserve` follow [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4) (PS4), the only reference with a body: ids count up from 1 per port, skipping 0, 0xFFFFFFFF and ids in use, and a failed reserve writes 0xFFFFFFFF
- [s_sethalt](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/ScalarInstructions.cpp) (core/shader/recompiler) - with SIMM16[0] clear it is a no-op: the RDNA 2 ISA guide describes that value as resuming a halted wave, and a wave that executes it is not halted; measured on a gfx1035 (RDNA 2) wave: `s_sethalt 0` followed by a VALU op completes and gives the normal result. Only SIMM16[0] is read; with it set the instruction throws
- [AMPR WriteAddressFromCounterPairOnCompletion](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAmpr/Export.cpp) (libSceAmpr) - the pair of an even counter n is assumed to give counter n in the low 32 bits and n + 1 in the high 32 bits. Wait compare values, wait flush and counter index ranges follow the [ampr_emu](https://github.com/drakmor/ampr_emu) reimplementation; the flush is accepted and has no effect, as commands run in order
- [AMPR command sizes](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAmpr/Export.cpp) (libSceAmpr) - commands use the emulator's own encoding with an 8-byte header, so `Nop(n)` takes 8 + 4n bytes where the console record is n dwords including its header (`NopWithData(n)`: (n + 1) dwords). The measure functions agree with the appended sizes, but a title that pads to an exact offset or the end of the buffer with `Nop(remaining / 4)` gets `SCE_AMPR_ERROR_BUFFER_FULL` or a shifted offset. The at-start flag of the `_04_00` writes is ignored; commands run in order, each after the previous one completes
- [AMPR object layout and compact gathers](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Apr/include/AprCommandBuffer.hpp) - the 24-byte object and offsets (type 0, cursor 4, count 8, capacity 12, pointer 16) follow [ampr_emu assertions](https://github.com/drakmor/ampr_emu/blob/cfa85df379f6eeeb165d7badf9b648e266fe77b7/include/ampr_emu_command_buffer_types.h#L138-L143) and its [C declaration](https://github.com/drakmor/ampr_emu/blob/cfa85df379f6eeeb165d7badf9b648e266fe77b7/include/ampr.h#L41-L47). AnyPS5 represents the 32-bit type word as low 16-bit type and high 16-bit recording flags. Command encoding is AnyPS5 internal: opcode 29 and three 32-bit words encode the high offset byte, length minus one and low offset word. The 12-byte size reduces HFW's 1 MiB buffer pressure; it is not a claim about console opcode encoding. Existing 40-bit offsets and lengths 1 through 2^32 are retained; console behavior outside those limits is unverified.
- [APR resolve ForEach result](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Apr/src/Apr.cpp) (libkernel) - `sceKernelAprResolveFilepathsToIdsAndFileSizesForEach` returns the number of paths that resolved, with the per-path status in `results` and the failed ids/sizes set to the invalid id and 0. The [ampr_emu](https://github.com/drakmor/ampr_emu) bridge passes the count of its resolver through and does not show what is counted; counting successes is not confirmed on a title. The guest errno of the failing non-`ForEach` resolves is set through `__error_nid_postfix`
- [AMPR counter access](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Apr/src/Apr.cpp) (libSceAmpr) - signatures and checks of `WaitOnCounter_04_00`, `WriteCounter_04_00`, `ConstructNop` and `ConstructMarker` taken from the [ampr_emu](https://github.com/drakmor/ampr_emu) reimplementation, which hands counter commands to the console and does not show their effect. The counters are taken as 32 bits each: the 8-byte access of counter n spans n (low) and n + 1 (high), the 2- and 1-byte accesses select a half or a byte of the counter by its offset, waits compare at the access width (signed and wrapped compares included), the AND mask applies to both sides and the atomic writes wrap within the access. The `ConstructNop` type is not recorded
- [APR gather and scatter reads](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Apr/src/Apr.cpp) (libSceAmpr) - signatures, argument checks and the read cursor taken from the [ampr_emu](https://github.com/drakmor/ampr_emu) reimplementation, not confirmed on a title: each read leaves the file, the next file offset and the next destination; a gather reads that file at a new offset into the next destination, a scatter continues in the file into a new destination, a gather-scatter takes both. Recording one needs a read file since the last `ResetGatherScatterState`; like ampr_emu, `sceAmprCommandBufferReset` keeps that state, and a gather or scatter with no read before it in the submitted buffer throws instead of returning `SCE_AMPR_ERROR_APR_INVALIDGATHERSCATTERSTATE`
- [AMM map, map direct and unmap](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Apr/src/Apr.cpp) (libSceAmpr) - signatures and the protection mask (CPU, GPU, AMPR and ACP read and write) taken from the [ampr_emu](https://github.com/drakmor/ampr_emu) reimplementation, which hands the remaining checks and the mapping to the console kernel; the mapper model taken from [PS5PCEM](https://github.com/iStark/PS5PCEM): `sceAmprAmmGiveDirectMemory` usage 1 gives the allocated direct memory to the pool plain maps take pages from, usage 0 only allocates it. Not confirmed on a title. The AMM virtual address ranges are a 32 GiB range and a 32 GiB multimap range reserved on first use at the top of the guest arena, above the addresses that maps without `MAP_FIXED` are given lowest first and that titles derive their next hints from; the console's addresses are not known (ampr_emu takes them from `sceKernelMapperGetParam`), and a reservation that cannot be made there throws. Maps outside them throw. Addresses, sizes and direct offsets must be 16 KiB aligned. The memory type and the GPU mask are ignored, AMPR and ACP access maps as CPU access, unmapped pages go back to the pool and stay reserved. Submission runs the buffer before returning, the submit result is always 0 and a failing command throws; the AMM measures return errors sign-extended, as ampr_emu does. `sceAmprAmmGetVirtualAddressRanges` throws for a null output
- [AMM remap, multimap and protection changes](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Apr/src/Apr.cpp) (libSceAmpr) - signatures and the protection and mask checks taken from ampr_emu; the effects are inferred from the names, not confirmed on a title. A remap moves every page of a fully mapped range to the new address with the given protection and leaves the old range reserved; a multimap maps the same pages at a second address, anywhere in the AMM ranges, and a pool page goes back to the pool once its last mapping is unmapped; a multimap onto its own source range throws. A protection change sets the masked bits on every page of a fully mapped range; the memory type is recorded and ignored. A command on a range that is not fully mapped throws
- [sceVideodec2GetAvcPictureInfo](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVideodec2/Export.cpp) (libSceVideodec2) - runs the `sceVideodec2GetPictureInfo` body (KytyPS5 binds both NIDs to one function; SharpProspero gives both the same `outputInfo, first, second` signature); the second (bottom field) picture info is never written, since only progressive H.264 output is modelled. prosper calls the AVC layout unestablished (no title observed calling it)
- [AMM PRT ranges](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Apr/src/Apr.cpp) (libSceAmpr) - signatures, checks and their order taken from ampr_emu, where `MapAsPrt` is a map with the PRT flag and protection 0 and `AllocatePaForPrt` a memory type and protection change with mask 1019, both checking the buffer memory after its size; the effects are inferred from the names, not confirmed on a title. Unbacked PRT pages are zero-filled pages with GPU read access, so CPU reads see zeros instead of faulting and GPU writes fault instead of being dropped. `AllocatePaForPrt` backs each unbacked page with a pool page and sets the type and protection of backed ones; `RemapIntoPrt` moves a fully mapped range into a PRT range, its opcode argument (1011 when 0, as in ampr_emu) is recorded and ignored; `UnmapToPrt` returns the pages to the pool and zero-fills them again. A plain map, unmap or remap over a PRT range ends it; `AllocatePaForPrt`, `RemapIntoPrt` or `UnmapToPrt` outside a PRT range throws
- [AMM kernel write commands](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Apr/src/Apr.cpp) (libkernel) - signatures of `sceKernelWriteModifyMtypeProtectCommand` and `sceKernelWriteRemapIntoPrtCommand` taken from [ampr_emu's libkernel hook targets](https://github.com/drakmor/ampr_emu/blob/cfa85df379f6eeeb165d7badf9b648e266fe77b7/include/ampr_libkernel_hook_targets.h), which forward to the real kernel; the measure mode (`dst` null reports the size, `outSize` null skips the report) follows ampr_emu's measure-then-record calls, not confirmed on a title. The range, protection and mask checks mirror the `libSceAmpr` siblings, and the recorded bytes match them exactly so the submitted buffer executes on the existing paths
- [APR map begin and end](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAmpr/Export.cpp) (libSceAmpr) - as in ampr_emu, `MapBegin`/`MapDirectBegin` map when they run and `MapEnd` only closes the region; while a region is open a second begin and every completion write return `EPERM`, the at-start `_04_00` writes are accepted
- [AMPR markers](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAmpr/Export.cpp) (libSceAmpr) - signatures taken from the [ampr_emu](https://github.com/drakmor/ampr_emu) reimplementation, not confirmed on a title: `sceAmprCommandBufferSetMarkerWithColor` takes the color by pointer, the push and measure variants by value. Colors are not recorded
- [sceFontGetRenderScaledKerning](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFont/src/Render.cpp) (libSceFont) - arguments, the cleared output and the bound renderer check taken from `cellFontGetRenderScaledKerning` in [RPCS3](https://github.com/RPCS3/rpcs3/blob/master/rpcs3/Emu/Cell/Modules/cellFont.cpp) (PS3 predecessor of the library), not confirmed on a title. The kerning is the `sceFontGetKerning` one at the render scale
- [sceFontGetPixelResolution](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFont/src/Library.cpp) (libSceFont) - signature taken from the IDA type database of [Orbital](https://github.com/AlexAltea/orbital/blob/master/tools/ida/db_types.json), not confirmed on a title. The value is the one the library's driver reports (64 for FreeType); the argument checks and the cleared output follow `sceFontGetLibrary`
- [sceFontDettachDeviceCacheBuffer](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFont/src/Library.cpp) (libSceFont) - signature taken from the IDA type database of [Orbital](https://github.com/AlexAltea/orbital/blob/master/tools/ida/db_types.json), not confirmed on a title. It detaches the cache buffer attached by `sceFontAttachDeviceCacheBuffer`, returns the buffer and its size, and reports `SCE_FONT_ERROR_NOT_ATTACHED_CACHE_BUFFER` when no cache buffer is attached
- [sceFontGetFontGlyphsCount, sceFontGetCharGlyphCode](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFont/src/Open.cpp) (libSceFont) - signatures taken from the IDA type database of [Orbital](https://github.com/AlexAltea/orbital/blob/master/tools/ida/db_types.json) and matching the [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/main/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Font/SceFont.Engine.cs) bindings, not confirmed on a title. The count is the face's glyph count and the glyph code its glyph index, as the library's driver reports them; a code with no glyph returns `NO_SUPPORT_GLYPH` and code 0 `NO_SUPPORT_CODE`, as `sceFontRenderCharGlyphImage` does. The argument checks and the cleared output follow `sceFontGetPixelResolution`
- [sceFontGetFontResolution](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFont/src/Open.cpp) (libSceFont) - signature taken from the IDA type database of [Orbital](https://github.com/AlexAltea/orbital/blob/master/tools/ida/db_types.json), not confirmed on a title; the outputs are inferred from the names. The resolution is the face's units per EM and the scale pixel the driver's face scale (units per EM / 64 for FreeType), the pixel scale a font has when opened. Either output may be null, as in `sceFontGetResolutionDpi`
- [sceFontCreateWritingLine, sceFontWritingLineWritesOrder](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFont/src/WritingLine.cpp) (libSceFont) - signatures and the `SceFontWritingLineStep`/`SceFontCreateWritingLineDetail` layouts (detail id `0x0FD5`) taken from [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/main/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Font/SceFontStructs.cs), not confirmed on a title. The 64-bit writing attribute is read as the PS4 11.00 `libSceFont.sprx` reads it: low byte `0xFA`, embedding level in bits 8-15 (0 to 126), bit 30 and bit 31 marking space before and after the run; the PS5 library was not measured, and the names of the two bits are ours. PPSA01325 builds its attribute the same way (`0xFA | level << 8`, bits 30 and 31) and passes a null metrics pointer when its run has no metrics object. Attribute 0 is accepted and treated as `0xFA` with level 0 and no spacing bits, as before this reading; the PS4 library rejects it and what the PS5 library does with it is unknown. Any other attribute, a level above 126 and a null orderer throw: the PS4 library returns `0x80460002` for them, not confirmed for the PS5
- [sceFontWritingRefersRenderStepCharacter](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFont/src/Text.cpp) (libSceFont) - the second argument is an out pointer to the writing's letter step, as in [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/d2413fc2ebd91d1b7234197c2dd5bd48e1cb6a51/src/libs/libFont.cpp#L1882-L1896) and the [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/main/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Font/SceFont.Engine.cs) bindings. Measured on PS4 firmware 11.00 `libSceFont.sprx` (not on a PS5): exactly 8 bytes are written, the address of a step stored inside the writing, and a null writing writes null; shadPS4 and prosper treat the argument as a struct, which the measurement contradicts. The letter step lives in the writing, so the pointer stays valid as long as the writing does, and its contents are rebuilt by each call of this function. On the PS4 library repeated calls on one step walk the characters it covers and then return null; here every call returns the step's one character, so the second call differs. Assumed: each step covers one character and one glyph. PPSA06699 (Hot Shots Golf 2) dereferences the returned pointer for its character counts.
- [sceFontWritingLineGetOrderingSpace](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFont/src/WritingLine.cpp) (libSceFont) - the four outputs are written in the order the PS4 11.00 `libSceFont.sprx` writes them (line advance, leading space, space between spaced runs, trailing space); the parameter names in the [Orbital](https://github.com/AlexAltea/orbital/blob/master/tools/ida/db_types.json) IDA type database and the [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/main/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Font/SceFont.Engine.cs) bindings order them differently (head, inline, tail, advance), and the PS5 library was not measured. PPSA01325's only call uses them in the PS4 order: it computes first - second - fourth - 0.25 * third and compares that, divided by a scale, with its width limit. The PS4 library writes the trailing space through its pointer without a null check whenever the leading-space pointer is set; that call throws here
- [sceSaveDataTransferringMountPs4, sceSaveDataDirNameSearchPs4](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSaveData.native/Export.cpp) (libSceSaveData.native) - arguments assumed to be those of `sceSaveDataTransferringMount` and `sceSaveDataDirNameSearch`: KytyPS5 binds both NIDs to the PS5 functions, and [prosper](https://github.com/mattias800/prosper) captured the same result layout on a title for the search. There is no PS4 save area, so the mount returns `NOT_FOUND` and the search reports zero hits
- [sceCoredumpWriteUserString](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Coredump.cpp) and [sceSaveDataGetConvertProgress](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSaveData.native/Export.cpp) - imported by PPSA16064, not called during its start-up and added without an implementation; unknown signatures
- [sceSaveDataCancel](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSaveData.native/Export.cpp) (libSceSaveData.native) - imported by PPSA23126, which fails to load without it; added without an implementation because its signature is unknown. Every save data call here finishes before it returns, so nothing is ever in flight to cancel, but what the console returns then is unknown
- [sceAudioOutExOpen, sceAudioOutExConfigureOutput, sceAudioOutExClose](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) - the HDMI bitstream calls follow [PS5-Audio-Passthrough-Research](https://github.com/sainsaji/PS5-Audio-Passthrough-Research/blob/cf3c921d79bf00db4847f31b68a7bd95a8037cd7/docs/FINDINGS.md#L31-L80), which disassembles firmware 12.70's libSceAudioOut and logs the calls on a console. `sceAudioOutExOpen(user, mode)` returns `0x80260015` for a mode above 10 or outside mask `0x61F`; otherwise it opens a port of internal type 6 in S16 stereo, 256 frames at 48000 Hz for modes 0, 1, 2 and 9 and 1024 frames at 192000 Hz for modes 3, 4 and 10. `sceAudioOutExConfigureOutput(control, flags, mode, target, option)` returns `0x80268009` when `control` is not 0, takes modes 0 to 10 and 0xFF and targets 1 to 3 and 0xFF, and returned 0 on the console; `sceAudioOutExClose` returned 0. The argument registers match [Jelly5](https://github.com/02dnot/Jelly5/blob/552b866d89b2a58b27f7f7b0484dc743ce235526/app/src/jelly5_bitstream.cpp#L26-L28) and the sample app's [bitstream_out.cpp](https://github.com/sainsaji/PS5-Audio-Passthrough-Research/blob/cf3c921d79bf00db4847f31b68a7bd95a8037cd7/sample-app/src/platform/ps5/bitstream_out.cpp#L29-L32). Audio reaches the host through SDL as PCM and the host has no HDMI bitstream output, so an Ex port has no host device: `sceAudioOutOutput` paces it at its own rate and drops its IEC 61937 bursts, and `sceAudioOutExConfigureOutput` changes nothing. Nonzero `flags` or `option`, other modes or targets, `sceAudioOutExClose` of a handle that is not an Ex port, and `sceAudioOutGetPortState` on an Ex port throw, since the console's answers are unknown
- [sceOpusCeltDecCreateEx, sceOpusCeltDecDecode, sceOpusCeltDecDestroy, sceOpusCeltDecGetSize, sceOpusCeltDecInitialize, sceOpusCeltDecTerminate](../../core/libs/prx/libSceOpusCeltDec/Export.cpp) (libSceOpusCeltDec) - imported by a title. Their signatures are assumed to match libSceOpusDec's in [ProsperoRadio](https://github.com/blackbearreloaded/ProsperoRadio/blob/2c4029c3a8f43e9e1dfef98374d94d99b14d745d/src/opus_decoder.cpp), and the 640-byte state size, context handling and byte-count return are taken from [libSceOpusDec](../../core/libs/prx/libSceOpusDec/Export.cpp); none of them was measured on a console. a title opens this decoder only as a fallback when libSceOpusDec reports a retryable error for a CELT packet, which libSceOpusDec here never returns, so the fallback is not reached
- [sceVoiceResetPort](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVoice/Export.cpp) (libSceVoice) - the `(uint32_t portId)` signature is the PS Vita one in [VitaSDK](https://github.com/vitasdk/vita-headers/blob/ae5808f3105c42fa3373ba4ab64ab54e2e3ca848/include/psp2/voice.h#L503-L514) and the PS3 `cellVoiceResetPort` in [RPCS3](https://github.com/RPCS3/rpcs3/blob/be69c054eeac57e2e671697a463473d37fe5a7b2/rpcs3/Emu/Cell/Modules/cellVoice.cpp#L493-L510); that the PS5 library takes the same argument is assumed. A reset clears a port's buffered data, and ports here buffer nothing (`sceVoiceReadFromOPort` returns 0 bytes and `sceVoiceWriteToIPort` throws), so it only checks that the library is initialized and the port exists, as RPCS3 does, and keeps the port's connections, volume and mute flag. An uninitialized library or an unknown port throws like the other port calls; the console's error codes are not measured. a title imports it
- [sceNetCtlGetInfo](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNetCtl/Export.cpp) (libSceNetCtl) - answers from the host interface that holds an IPv4 address, preferring the one that carries the default route: its address, netmask, gateway, MTU and first two IPv4 DNS servers. The device is reported as wired, the link as connected, the IP configuration as DHCP and the HTTP proxy as off, whatever the host uses, and the Ethernet address is the one `sceNetGetMacAddress` reports. A gateway or DNS server the interface does not have is reported as `0.0.0.0`; what a console reports then is unknown. The Wi-Fi codes (5-10), the DHCP host name (12), the PPPoE user name (13) and the HTTP proxy server and port (20, 21) throw
- [libSceJpegDec](../../core/libs/prx/libSceJpegDec/Export.cpp) (libSceJpegDec) - structure layouts follow [OpenOrbis `jpeg.h`](https://github.com/OpenOrbis/OpenOrbis-PS4-Toolchain/blob/0a1aaf9dd4a92695538bdeb09fb056d06dd11725/include/orbis/_types/jpeg.h#L13-L72) and [SharpProspero `JpegDec.cs`](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/5dfdb4b0b166e83dc9ae275001afb695f12c7174/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Image/JpegDec.cs#L20-L117); attributes 1 and 2 are the values [SharpProspero's `JpegImage.cs`](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/5dfdb4b0b166e83dc9ae275001afb695f12c7174/src/SharpProspero/Graphics/JpegImage.cs#L64-L74) passes. The error codes are libScePngDec's: no reference lists the JpegDec ones, and SharpProspero raises 0x80650020 on its JpegDec path where it raises libScePngDec's 0x80690020 on its PngDec path. Image info always reports color space 3, suitable CSC attribute 1 and zero component ids and sampling factors; the console's values, including which images it reports attribute 2 for, are unknown. The coefficient memory size is 0 because the host decoder needs no coefficient buffer; the size the console reports for progressive images is unknown. `max_image_width` is not checked, and the packed `width << 16 | height` decode result is libScePngDec's
- [scePadSetVibrationMode](../../core/libs/prx/libScePad/Export.cpp) (libScePad) - modes 1 to 3 and 5 are accepted and stored. On a PS5 (firmware 13.60) the call returns `0x80920005` before `scePadInit`, then rejects a mode of 0 or below with `INVALID_ARG` before it checks the handle, and returns `INVALID_HANDLE` for a bad handle with any higher mode; whether an open handle accepts modes above 3 was not measured, since the payload could not open a pad; 5 is accepted because a title passes it on its open handle at start-up; the meanings of modes 3 and 5 are unknown (a title calls it with 3 right after `scePadOpen` and aborts on failure; [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/main/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Pad/Pad.cs) names only 1 and 2). The stored mode has no other effect

### Functional

- [sceOpusCeltDecDecode](../../core/libs/prx/libSceOpusCeltDec/Export.cpp) (libSceOpusCeltDec) decodes with the FFmpeg decoder of [libSceOpusDec](../../core/libs/prx/libSceOpusDec/include/OpusDecoder.hpp) and shares its limits. Packets whose TOC configuration is below 16 (SILK and hybrid) throw: whether the console decodes them or returns an error code is unknown. Contexts and decoder states are separate from libSceOpusDec's
- [sceContentDeleteById](../../core/libs/prx/libSceContentDelete/Export.cpp) (libSceContentDelete) and [sceContentSearchGetContentLastUpdateId, sceContentSearchOpenMetadataByContentId, sceContentSearchGetTotalContentSize, sceContentSearchGetNumOfContent](../../core/libs/prx/libSceContentSearch/Export.cpp) (libSceContentSearch) - imported by PPSA06465, which fails to load without them; added without an implementation because their signatures are unknown
- [sce::Json::Parser::parse with a read callback](../../core/libs/prx/libSceJson2/Export.cpp) (libSceJson2) - the callback's contract is taken from PPSA04540's: it returns 0 with the next character, and at the end of its data writes `'\0'` and returns -1. Characters are read until the callback returns non-zero or gives `'\0'`, and the text read is then parsed as by the `(text, size)` overload, so trailing characters after the value are rejected as there. Whether the console stops reading at the end of the top-level value, and what it returns when the callback fails before the end, are unknown
- [sce::Json::Object::Pair and sce::Json::Array::iterator members](../../core/libs/prx/libSceJson2/Export.cpp) (libSceJson2) imported by a title - the signatures follow the mangled names, with `Array::iterator::operator++(int)` returning its copy through a result pointer before `this` as `Array::begin` does, since the iterator has a non-trivial destructor. `Pair(const String&, const Value&)` deep-copies both into the layout `Object` iteration already hands out (key at 0, value at 0x10) and zeroes the 8 bytes between them; what the console keeps there is unknown. The default-constructed iterator is singular
- [sce::Json::MemAllocator::notifyError](../../core/libs/prx/libSceJson2/Export.cpp) (libSceJson2) - the base class's virtual does nothing. A title's allocator that does not override it calls it through its own vtable; the library never calls the title's allocator, so it never reports an allocation failure. Whether the console's base implementation logs anything is unknown
- [sce::Json::Object::insert](../../core/libs/prx/libSceJson2/Export.cpp) (libSceJson2) - imported by a title and declared without parameters, added without an implementation because the title fails to load without it; the return type, and so whether a result pointer precedes `this`, is unknown
- [sce::Json::Parser::parse](../../core/libs/prx/libSceJson2/Export.cpp) (libSceJson2) - a number that overflows a double (e.g. `1e309`) throws: whether the console rejects the document or stores an infinite real is unknown
- [sceUserServiceGetNpAccountId](../../core/libs/prx/libSceUserService/Export.cpp) (libSceUserService) - the account id for a user with no linked PSN account is assumed to be 0 with success; libSceNpManager's `sceNpGetAccountIdA` answers the same question with `SCE_NP_ERROR_SIGNED_OUT`
- [sceUserServiceInitialize, sceUserServiceTerminate](../../core/libs/prx/libSceUserService/Export.cpp) (libSceUserService) - measured on a PS5 (firmware 13.60): before `sceUserServiceInitialize`, and again after `sceUserServiceTerminate`, `sceUserServiceGetLoginUserIdList`, `sceUserServiceGetEvent` and `sceUserServiceTerminate` return `0x80960002`; a second initialization returns `0x80960003`, and a new initialization reports the login of the initial user again. The other calls were not measured before initialization and do not check it. `sceUserServiceInitialize2` was not measured: it initializes like `sceUserServiceInitialize` and returns success when already initialized
- [sceUserServiceGetUserColor](../../core/libs/prx/libSceUserService/Export.cpp) (libSceUserService) - the default profile colour is assumed to be blue (0)
- [libSceVdecsw](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVdecsw/Export.cpp) (libSceVdecsw) - structure layouts follow shadPS4's `vdecsw.h` and were checked against PPSA05684's movie decoder (`movie-decoder-vdecsw.cpp`), which asserts `pictureCount == 1` at offset 0xb of `OutputInfo` and reads `InputResult` offset 0x10 as the number of frames an access unit produced. The compute, decoder memory and decode input structures and the first 0x28 bytes of the 0x50-byte decoder config are those of libSceVideodec2, and decoding goes through it. The 0x78-byte AVC picture info is filled with Videodec2's leading fields and the cropping fields only. OUTPUT_PENDING (0x81510115), INPUT_QUEUE_EMPTY (0x81510116) and DECODE_PENDING (0x81510117) follow from the codes each call's assert accepts. At most four decoded pictures are queued and the last one is held until `sceVdecswFinalizeDecodeSequence`; the console's queue depth is unknown
- [sceGameLiveStreamingGetCurrentStatus2](../../core/libs/prx/libSceGameLiveStreaming/Export.cpp) (libSceGameLiveStreaming) - the status layout (user id, on-air flag, spectator, text and command message counts, broadcast resolution, 48 reserved bytes) is taken from shadPS4's `OrbisGameLiveStreamingStatus2`; the call reports that no stream is on air, with an invalid user id
- [Native multisampled depth sampling](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/DepthSurface.cpp) accepts numeric D16 or D32, two/four/eight samples, one Z_X slice and mip, and pipe-aligned ITERATE_256 HTILE without stencil or VRS. Sampling eligibility is recorded before the attachment's first host clear: every HTILE word must prove the same initial clear of zero or one, and the complete sample-aware depth and HTILE ranges must be accessible and write-watched. Unsupported initial state or device sampling support retains attachment rendering but sampling throws; eligibility is never retried. Sampling rejects later guest writes, queued external GPU writes, and HTILE clear/fill transitions. Guest publication, arbitrary guest depth import, stencil/raw-bit sampling, arrays, storage access and allocation retirement remain unimplemented. The block footprint follows main's AMD Addrlib thin-block rule; the accepted HTILE clear words and PS5 equivalence are not measured on console hardware. PPSA99902's historical resident eight-sample D32 reader is documented in [#419](https://github.com/boykopovar/AnyPS5/pull/419#issuecomment-6105597729); the subsequent completed HTILE transition needs separate support.
- [Depth clear draws](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (AGC driver) - a draw with `DB_RENDER_CONTROL.DEPTH_CLEAR_ENABLE` is taken to write `DB_DEPTH_CLEAR` to every pixel it covers whatever the depth test, depth bounds and depth bias say: it is drawn with an always-passing depth write and the viewport depth range pinned to the clear value. The draw is rasterized as submitted, so its coverage is the draw's own. Whether the DB still applies the depth test or HiZ to such a draw is not measured
- [Alpha-to-coverage](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (AGC driver) - only `DB_ALPHA_TO_MASK = 0xaa01` is accepted: the enable bit with the four quad offsets at 2 and no `OFFSET_ROUND`, the undithered setting [radeonsi](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/bffd91947efc1932da99aca69fc540d6d4201b0c/src/gallium/drivers/radeonsi/si_state.c#L371-L381) programs. Draws are single-sampled, so the pixel shader [writes](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvModuleEmitter.cpp) `SampleMask` 0 when its MRT0 alpha is below one half or NaN and all ones otherwise, and runs with late fragment tests. That rule was measured on a Radeon 660M (gfx1035, RADV with `RADV_DEBUG=noatocdithering`, which programs the same register) for 0, -0, 0.25, the two floats below one half, one half, the two above, 0.75, 1, 2, -1, both infinities and both NaNs, and is pinned in [AlphaToCoverage.cpp](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/tests/execution/AlphaToCoverage.cpp); it is not measured on gfx1013. Vulkan's `alphaToCoverageEnable` is not used: the NVIDIA driver 615.78.08 dithers single-sample coverage (an alpha of 0.25 writes half the pixels of an RTX 3050 Laptop target). Other offsets, `OFFSET_ROUND`, a sample-mask export, and an MRT0 that is missing, swizzled, packed or exported without a floating-point alpha throw. A draw without a color target on MRT0 keeps alpha-to-coverage from its MRT0 export, as [radeonsi exports MRT0 as `32_AR` for alpha-to-coverage without a color buffer](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/mesa-24.0.0/src/gallium/drivers/radeonsi/si_state_shaders.cpp#L2425-L2431). A draw that enables alpha-to-coverage with no MRT0 export still throws: what the console covers then is not measured.
- [MSAA_ENABLE on a single-sample target](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (AGC driver) - `PA_SC_MODE_CNTL_0.MSAA_ENABLE` with `PA_SC_AA_CONFIG.MSAA_NUM_SAMPLES = 0` is drawn like the same state without the bit when sample 0 of the four `PA_SC_AA_SAMPLE_LOCS_PIXEL_*_0` registers is at the pixel center and the draw rasterizes filled triangles with alpha-to-coverage and conservative rasterization off. [RADV](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/745709a000482a4a24c91faef5c5a40bd653a863/src/amd/vulkan/radv_cmd_buffer.c#L13606-13609) programs this state for one-sample custom sample locations on GFX10 and later, where the bit makes the rasterizer use the programmed location of sample 0 ([061bc615](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/commit/061bc6151a11597f720535f00e79a5752e24aff6)); that the bit changes nothing else for such a draw is inferred from that and is not measured on hardware. Other locations of sample 0, absent location registers, points, lines, rectangles, alpha-to-coverage and conservative rasterization throw
- [GPU flips](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVideoOut/src/VideoOutDriver.cpp) (libSceVideoOut) - a command-buffer flip of a buffer index with no registered buffer when the GPU reaches it presents black and completes like any flip (flip event, count, `flipPendingNum`). The console's behaviour is unknown; PPSA05684 requires that it completes: its renderer submits a flip of buffer 0 while another thread waits for `sceVideoOutIsFlipPending` to reach 0 before registering the buffers, and `flipPendingNum` counts that flip from its submission as in shadPS4
- [sceAgcDriverGetEqContextId](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Eq/src/Query.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - returns `ident`: an end-of-pipe interrupt is delivered only to the registration whose id equals the raising queue, so `ident` is that context's id (titles register 0 or 1 for graphics and `0x20 + 8 * pipe + queue` for compute). The console's value is not known; KytyPS5 returns the event's `data`, which it fills with the interrupt's context id, whereas `data` here is the EV_CLEAR trigger count. Other event filters throw
- [sceAgcSetSubmitMode](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Misc/src/Suspend.cpp) (libSceAgc) - mode values unknown; only 0 is accepted
- [User data registers](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Diagnostics.cpp) (AGC driver) - a `SPI_SHADER_USER_DATA_*` or `COMPUTE_USER_DATA_*` register the title never wrote reads as 0 when a stage's RSRC2 count covers it, as Kyty (zeroed `UserSgprInfo`) and SharpEmu (missing registers read 0) do, and as AMD PAL and Mesa initialize them; the console's value is not confirmed
- [SET_PREDICATION](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Pm4.cpp) (AGC driver) - boolean predication follows the AMD PM4 encoding [Mesa](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/main/src/amd/common/sid.h) uses: operation 3 reads 64 bits and 4 reads 32 bits, and bit 8 runs predicated packets when the value is non-zero (clear: when it is zero); the bit 12 hint is ignored. The address must have its low 4 bits clear, as `sceAgcDcbSetPredication` writes it. Query predication (Z-pass, primitive count), a predicated COND_EXEC, predicated command buffer chains and flips in predicated command buffers throw
- [sceAgcGetGsOversubscription](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Shader/src/GsOversubscription.cpp) (libSceAgc) - signature and occupancy formula (base and expanded vertex/export capacities, budget shift, `GE_PC_ALLOC` / `SPI_SHADER_PGM_RSRC4_GS` encoding) follow KytyPS5 only, not confirmed on hardware or a title; a negative or NaN factor that gives a negative target, a missing GS context register or a zero `GE_MAX_OUTPUT_PER_SUBGROUP` throws
- [Vertex entry merged wave info](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/InstructionTranslator.cpp) (recompiler) - `s3` of every vertex-path shader is set to `waveSize | waveSize << 8` (vertex and primitive count of a full wave, wave index 0), or to `32 | 32 << 8` for a wave64 program on a 32-lane host subgroup, which runs it at one lane per invocation and holds no lanes 32-63. On a device whose `subgroupSupportedStages` lacks the vertex stage (lavapipe, MoltenVK), vertex and local programs run as one-lane waves: `s3` is `1 | 1 << 8`, the lane id is 0, a ballot is the invocation's own bit, a shuffle or broadcast returns the invocation's value, an elect is true and a subgroup reduction is the value itself, so no `GroupNonUniform` capability is declared. Layout taken from the prologue Mesa's ACO emits for NGG vertex shaders (`s_bfe_u64 exec` from `s3`), not measured on hardware. The driver gives every graphics program user data from `s8`, so the condition does not tell NGG from legacy vertex shaders: they just never read `s3`. A draw whose last wave is partial still reports a full wave, and so does a host wave with idle invocations (an RTX 5070 ran every vertex warp of a non-indexed triangle list with 30 of its 32 invocations).
- [NGG vertex programs on the mesh path](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (AGC driver) - an unregistered vertex program with `PRIMGEN_EN` that uses LDS or `s_barrier` runs on the geometry (mesh) path. Each input primitive is assumed to yield at most one output primitive, the subgroup is bounded by `GE_MAX_OUTPUT_PER_SUBGROUP` as a vertex budget, and the GS/ES VGPR count checks are skipped, because the merged inputs keep their positions. Taken from the code Mesa's ACO emits for NGG culling and from PPSA59322 and PPSA99010 draws, not measured on hardware. Registered programs keep the vertex path
- [s_barrier in vertex-path programs](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvFlowEmitter.cpp) (recompiler) - emits no instruction when the program has no LDS or GDS access and no memory write or atomic, so no wave of the subgroup can observe another; the `GS_ALLOC_REQ` such a barrier orders on NGG passthrough programs is already dropped on the vertex path. The Vertex execution model has no workgroup barrier. Programs with LDS, GDS or memory writes throw. Not measured on hardware
- [s_barrier outside vertex-path and hull programs](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvFlowEmitter.cpp) (recompiler) - is a workgroup `OpControlBarrier` with acquire-release semantics on workgroup, uniform and image memory in every program, so accesses through a `Coherent` buffer array before it are ordered against those of the other waves of the work-group after it. On the console the barrier orders global memory only after an `s_waitcnt` for `vmcnt(0)` and `vscnt(0)` and, in WGP mode, with a `buffer_gl0_inv` after it (the work-group fence rows of the [LLVM AMDGPU memory model for GFX10](https://llvm.org/docs/AMDGPUUsage.html#memory-model-gfx10-gfx11)); the recompiler translates neither instruction and does not know the mode, so it also orders barriers the guest did not prepare that way. Not measured on hardware. Accesses that are neither `Coherent` nor `Volatile` in the SPIR-V (buffer accesses through the `buffers` array when it is not `Coherent`, BDA accesses without GLC or DLC, storage images) are private in the Vulkan memory model, which orders private accesses of different invocations only through API synchronization, not through this barrier
- [Strided buffer overhang](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderResources.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - a strided buffer descriptor that ends less than one stride past the end of the readable registered allocation holding its base is bound only up to that allocation's end, even when another readable allocation starts right there. This assumes the bytes past the end are the rounded-up `num_records` of the last record (RADV rounds it up), which no fetch inside the buffer reads; a guest that really keeps a vertex split across two adjacent allocations would read zeros for the cut tail. Taken from the RetroArch Dolphin core case in #2912, not measured on hardware.
- [Zero-stride vertex fetch range](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/include/VertexInput.hpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - for a stride-0 V# with OOB_SELECT 2 the whole fetch reads memory unless NUM_RECORDS is 0; with OOB_SELECT 3 it reads zero when NUM_RECORDS is 0 and throws when the fetch only partly fits. Rules taken from the GFX10 OOB_SELECT table in Mesa [ac_descriptors.c](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/main/src/amd/common/ac_descriptors.c), not measured on hardware. Whether OOB_SELECT 3 checks each dword of a multi-dword fetch separately is unknown. OOB_SELECT 0 and 1 keep the structured record checks.
- [Metal textures](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/Metal/MetalTexture.mm) (libSceAgcDriver) of guest formats 51 (10_10_10_2 SNorm) and 55 (10_10_10_2 SInt) are refused: Metal has no signed RGB10A2 pixel format, and the texels are not converted to another one.
- [sceAgcCbCondWrite](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Cb/src/Branch.cpp) (libSceAgc) - argument order and the 9-dword COND_WRITE layout follow the clean-room [OpenAGC](https://github.com/OpenAGC/OpenAGC/blob/main/src/game_compat.c) and [sharpemu](https://github.com/sharpemu/sharpemu/blob/main/src/SharpEmu.Libs/Agc/AgcExports.CommandPackets.cs), which agree on them; SharpProspero lists another order. The write space is accepted only as 1 (memory), the one value both encode the same way, and other values throw.
- [sceAgcGetIsTrinityMode](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Misc/src/Platform.cpp) (libSceAgc) - signature from the only PPSA26344 call (a pointer to a one-byte flag, result ignored); a null pointer throws because its error code is unknown
- [zARR5aCmkoY](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/DcbFlow/src/Control.cpp) (libSceAgc) - unknown name, signature
- [sceAgcDcbContextStateOpGetSize](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/DcbState/src/ContextState.cpp) (libSceAgc) - sizes from KytyPS5 only (5, 27, 27 and 32 dwords), equal to what `sceAgcDcbContextStateOp_0100` writes; KytyPS5 returns 0 for an operation above 3, here it throws
- [sceAgcBranchPatchSetThenTarget_0300, sceAgcBranchPatchSetElseTarget_0300](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Patch/src/Branch.cpp) (libSceAgc) - names from their NIDs (`7Wa3aeJgeVU`, `rP5xLdOf26k`); the `(packet, cachePolicy, target, sizeInDwords)` signature is inferred from PPSA28416's calls (a branch packet, 0, an aligned target, 4), with the policy stored where `sceAgcCbBranch` puts it
- [sceAgcDcbSet{Cx,Sh,Uc}RegistersIndirectGetSize](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/DcbState/src/Registers.cpp) (libSceAgc) - the count argument is ignored: PPSA28416 calls them with leftover register contents (0xf5f5039c), so the real functions likely take none; the size is 20 bytes either way
- [sceAgcUpdateInterpolantMapping_0100](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Misc/src/ShaderFusion.cpp) (libSceAgc) - name from its NID (`k0E7vkgqAuE`); the `(regs, vs, ps)` arguments are the declared ones, behaviour unknown
- [sceFontCharacterGetSyllableStringState](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFont/src/Text.cpp) (libSceFont) - the signature `(textCharacter, int* syllableStringState)` follows [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/main/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Font/SceFont.Engine.cs). Measured on PS4 firmware 11.00 `libSceFont.sprx` (not on a PS5): the state is 0 for Latin, CJK, Hangul, Arabic, Hebrew, Devanagari and the other scripts tried, and the library sets 1, 3 or 4 (never 2 on real text) for Thai while it creates the string; a null character or a null result returns `SCE_FONT_ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER`, and a null character writes 0 to a non-null result. This returns 0 for every character outside the Thai block (U+0E00 to U+0E7F), as [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/d2413fc2ebd91d1b7234197c2dd5bd48e1cb6a51/src/libs/libFont.cpp#L1805-L1815) does, and throws for Thai characters, whose cluster states are not modelled. SharpProspero's `SceFontSyllableString` names 0 NonDetection, 1 Starting, 2 Following, 3 Depending and 4 Connecting. PPSA06699 (Hot Shots Golf 2) calls it.
- [sceAgcWriteDataPatchSetDst, sceAgcWriteDataPatchSetCachePolicy and their Async forms](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Patch/src/WaitRegMem.cpp) (libSceAgc) - the signatures `(packet, uint8 value)` are inferred from the other patch functions, as no public declaration was found; the value is taken in the encoding of the `dst` and `cachePolicy` arguments of `sceAgcDcbWriteData` (graphics) and `sceAgcAcbWriteData` (Async, compute), and the patched packet is the one those calls would write. `sceAgcAsyncWriteDataPatchSetAddressOrOffset` is assumed to patch the address like `sceAgcWriteDataPatchSetAddressOrOffset`, as the compute WRITE_DATA packet keeps it in the same dwords
- [dbOlWdppb4o](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Shader/src/InterpolantMapping.cpp) (libSceAgc) - unknown name; same arguments as `sceAgcCreateInterpolantMapping_0100`; the `SPI_PS_INPUT_CNTL` bits for `is_f16` 2 inputs follow KytyPS5, not confirmed on hardware
- [sceAgcGetDataPacketPayloadRange](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Misc/src/PacketInfo.cpp) (libSceAgc) - signature, the `{base, size in bytes}` output and the type 0 range (header + 1, one dword longer than the type 1 payload) from KytyPS5 only, matching `sceAgcGetDataPacketPayloadAddress_0090`; prosper returns header + 2 for both types from that address function
- [sceAgcAsyncCondExecPatchSetCommandAddress, sceAgcAsyncCondExecPatchSetEnd](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Patch/src/CondExec.cpp) (libSceAgc) - no reference has a body for them; they are assumed to patch the COND_EXEC packet like `sceAgcCondExecPatchSetCommandAddress` and `sceAgcCondExecPatchSetEnd`, since `sceAgcAcbCondExec` writes the same packet as `sceAgcDcbCondExec`, as `sceAgcAsyncRewindPatchSetRewindState` does for REWIND
- [sceAgcAcbAtomicGds_0900](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Acb/src/Control.cpp) (libSceAgc) - name from its NID (`gQkqkLttcpw`), signature unknown
- [sceAgcDcbPrimeUtcl2](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/DcbState/src/Display.cpp) (libSceAgc) - assumed to write the packet `sceAgcAcbPrimeUtcl2` writes: PRIME_UTCL2 has the same 5 dwords on the graphics and compute rings. Not confirmed on a title. Both write a NOP of that size, as there is no TLB to prime
- [sceAgcDcbQueueEndOfShaderActionGetSize](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/DcbState/src/ContextState.cpp) (libSceAgc) - the builder is inline in the SDK and not exported; the size is assumed to be that of the RELEASE_MEM packet `sceAgcCbReleaseMem` writes (32 bytes), the same as `sceAgcAcbQueueEndOfShaderActionGetSize`, as in [OpenAGC](https://github.com/OpenAGC/OpenAGC/blob/main/src/game_compat.c) and [REmu](https://github.com/xnetcat/REmu/blob/main/crates/remu-runtime/src/agc_dcb.rs)
- [sceKernelInternalMemoryGetModuleSegmentInfo](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Module/src/Module.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown signature
- [sceKernelGetModuleInfoForUnwind](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Module/src/Module.cpp) (libkernel) - on Linux the tables come from `sceKernelGetModuleInfoFromAddr`, whose `eh_frame_size` includes the zero terminator, as measured on firmware 13.60 (reported in [#2419](https://github.com/boykopovar/AnyPS5/issues/2419), not reproduced here); on Windows it is assumed to stop before the terminator. LLVM libunwind's `CFI_Parser::findFDE` stops at the terminator or the length, whichever comes first, so it finds the same FDEs either way. Only the main executable and `.guest.prx` images report tables; other host images report none, as on Windows
- [sceKernelGetModuleInfoFromAddr](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Module/src/ModuleInfo.cpp) (libkernel) - the 0x1A8-byte info layout follows the [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4) reimplementation; a title's `libc.prx` reads only `id` (PPSA14632). The meaning of `flags` is unknown; only 2 is accepted. The input `st_size` is ignored, because PPSA21703's `libc.prx` passes an uninitialized 0x1A8-byte buffer; the full size is written back. The id is the `sceKernelLoadStartModule` handle of an image opened through the loader and otherwise a stable id from the same range. The name is the host file name without `.guest.prx`. An image with more than 4 load segments (the converted executable has 7) reports its first 4. `tls_offset` is 0 and `ref_count` is 1, as the host loader exposes neither. On Windows the module is the PE image holding the address: a relinked image reports its `.elf` sections as segments and the eh_frame_hdr recorded in `.ehmeta`, any other image its first 4 sections with memory access and the `.ehfram` section without an eh_frame_hdr; the TLS fields and `init_proc_addr`/`fini_proc_addr` are 0
- [sceKernelGetModuleList](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Module/src/ModuleInfo.cpp) (libkernel) - lists the main program and every loaded `.prx` image in load order; host runtime libraries are left out. The handles are the ids `sceKernelGetModuleInfoFromAddr` reports, not the console's. The `(int32_t* handles, size_t count, size_t* actual)` signature and returning `ENOMEM` without writing `actual` after filling the first `count` handles when more images are loaded follow [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/0fe263a4760dfbfa973366890061749b4af0de97/src/core/libraries/kernel/process.cpp#L244-L264); whether the console writes any handles in that case is unknown
- [sceKernelGetModuleInfo](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Module/src/ModuleInfo.cpp) (libkernel) - the 0x160-byte layout (`st_size`, `name[256]`, 4 segments, `segment_count`, `fingerprint[20]`) follows [fpPS4](https://github.com/red-prig/fpPS4/blob/04cefd43e6fddd1ab033e7980cd356d14c964905/sys/sys_types.pas#L319-L337) and [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/0fe263a4760dfbfa973366890061749b4af0de97/src/core/module.h#L15-L31). Only that `st_size` is accepted, as in shadPS4; other sizes return `EINVAL`, a null pointer `EFAULT` and a handle `sceKernelGetModuleList` does not list `ESRCH`. Name and segments are the ones `sceKernelGetModuleInfoFromAddr` reports, on Windows from the same PE sections, so `prot` uses the read/write/execute bits 1/2/4, while fpPS4 and shadPS4 store the ELF `p_flags`. The fingerprint is left zero, as the host image does not carry it
- [sceKernelGetModuleList2 and sceKernelGetModuleInfo2](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Module/src/ModuleInfo.cpp) (libkernel) - the title-module variants follow [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/main/src/core/libraries/kernel/process.cpp): `sceKernelGetModuleList2` lists what `sceKernelGetModuleList` lists without system libraries, and `sceKernelGetModuleInfo2` answers `EPERM` for a system library after the `EFAULT`, `EINVAL` and `ESRCH` checks of `sceKernelGetModuleInfo`. A system library here is a loaded `.prx` image that is not a relinked title module (`.guest.prx`); the main program and the title's own modules are listed. Not verified on the console. PPSA34063 imports both and fails to load without them
- [sceKernelReleaseFlexibleMemory](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/Export.cpp) (libkernel) - unmaps the range like sceKernelMunmap, as fpPS4 and prosper do; whether the console keeps the virtual range reserved after releasing its pages is unverified
- [Memory protection bits 0x100 and 0x200](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/DirectMemory.cpp) (libkernel) - taken as audio coprocessor (ACP) read and write, as in the [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5) and [etaHEN](https://github.com/etaHEN/etaHEN) headers; the coprocessor work runs on the host CPU, so they grant host read and read/write access like the GPU bits
- [_umtx_op](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/SyncOnAddress/src/SyncOnAddress.cpp) (libkernel) - `UMTX_OP_WAIT`, `UMTX_OP_WAKE`, `UMTX_OP_WAIT_UINT`, `UMTX_OP_WAIT_UINT_PRIVATE` and `UMTX_OP_WAKE_PRIVATE` follow FreeBSD 11's [kern_umtx.c](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/stable/11/sys/kern/kern_umtx.c): a timeout whose size argument is at most 16 is a relative `timespec`, a larger one an `_umtx_time`, and a wake of 0 or fewer wakes one waiter. Waiters share the queue of `sceKernelSyncOnAddressWait`. Operation 0 fails with `EOPNOTSUPP` and operations above 26 with `EINVAL`, as in FreeBSD 11; `UMTX_OP_WAIT_UINT` with a value that does not fit in 32 bits returns 0 at once, as FreeBSD's zero-extended compare does. Operation 1 throws, since PPSA12544's call_once uses it in a way FreeBSD rejects; the other operations throw, and so does a misaligned object. PPSA12544's `libc.prx` passes `UMTX_OP_WAIT` and `UMTX_OP_WAIT_UINT` a pointer to a 4-byte microsecond timeout with size argument 0, which FreeBSD reads as a `timespec`, and waits for a running call_once with operation 1; its `PS5Util.prx` passes a `timespec`. Whether the console reads a microsecond timeout there, and what operation 1 does on it, is unverified. A libc call cannot be told apart from a `timespec` call by the size argument alone, so a timeout with size argument 0 is read as a `timespec` and only throws when it is not a valid one (negative seconds, nanoseconds outside [0, 1e9)), which is where FreeBSD would return `EINVAL`; a microsecond value that happens to read as a valid `timespec` is waited on as one
- [sceKernelSyncOnAddressWait](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/SyncOnAddress/src/SyncOnAddress.cpp) (libkernel) - the only known caller passes a null timeout and a name string as the fourth argument; the timeout is assumed to point to microseconds like the other kernel waits, and the name is ignored
- [sceKernelSyncOnAddressWait8 / sceKernelSyncOnAddressWait16 / sceKernelSyncOnAddressWait32 / sceKernelSyncOnAddressWait64](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/SyncOnAddress/src/SyncOnAddress.cpp) (libkernel) - libc's `__std_atomic_wait_direct_8/16/32/64` pass a microsecond timeout or null and ignore the result; the by-value comparand, return values and the 2- and 8-byte alignment of Wait16 and Wait64 are assumed
- [scePthreadRwlockDestroy, pthread_rwlock_destroy](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/src/Rwlock.cpp) (libkernel) - measured on a PS5 (firmware 13.60): destroying a rwlock returns 0 whether it is free or held for reading or writing, and leaves 1 in the handle; every call on that handle then returns `EINVAL` until it is initialized again, and a zero handle is initialized on first use by the `scePthreadRwlock` calls as by the POSIX ones. A rwlock destroyed while held is not freed, so its memory leaks. Destroying a rwlock while another thread is acquiring it is not guarded
- [scePthreadCondWait / scePthreadCondTimedwait](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/src/Cond.cpp) (libkernel) - before waiting, FreeBSD libthr's [`cond_wait_common`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/main/lib/libthr/thread/thr_cond.c#L1992-L2029) checks ownership through [`_mutex_owned`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/main/lib/libthr/thread/thr_mutex.c#L1166-L1178): an unowned mutex returns `EPERM`, while its destroyed marker returns `EINVAL`. An untouched adaptive static initializer (slot value 1) therefore returns `EPERM`; the test covers this, an uninitialized slot, a mutex owned by another thread, and the destroyed marker for both timed and untimed sce and POSIX waits. A recursive mutex locked several times is released completely for the wait and relocked to the same depth afterwards, as FreeBSD libthr does (`_mutex_cv_unlock`, `_mutex_cv_lock`). These FreeBSD-derived behaviors remain unverified on console hardware
- [sceKernelAioSubmitReadCommandsMultiple / sceKernelAioSubmitWriteCommandsMultiple / sceKernelAioWaitRequests / sceKernelAioPollRequests / sceKernelAioCancelRequest / sceKernelAioCancelRequests / sceKernelAioDeleteRequests](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Aio/src/Aio.cpp) (libkernel) - behaviour from shadPS4 (one id per request for the Multiple submits, cancel turns any request aborted and id 0 reports processing, wait mode 2 returns once one request completed, poll copies every state without waiting); the 128 requests or ids per batch limit is from SharpProspero, with an unknown error, so larger batches throw; wait modes other than 1 (and) and 2 (or) throw; null pointers, negative counts and invalid ids return the codes the single-request siblings use, checked before any state is written
- [_WStoul](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Strings.cpp), [_Iswctype](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/RuneLocale.cpp), [swscanf_s and swscanf](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/ScanningWide.cpp) (libc) - `_WStoul` is `wcstoul` and throws for a base other than 0 or 2 to 36. `_Iswctype` takes the class numbers of Dinkumware's [xwcc.h](https://github.com/superna9999/ti-cgt6x/blob/cgt6x_7_3_4/include/xwcc.h) (1 `alnum` to 11 `xdigit`, 12 `blank`) and throws for any other value, including the FreeBSD masks `wctype` returns; the numbering is not confirmed on the console's `libc.prx`. `swscanf_s` follows C11 7.29.2.2 and K.3.9.1.5, but reads the size argument as `unsigned int` like `sscanf_s` where K.3.9.1.5 specifies `rsize_t`; the console's width is unverified. A runtime-constraint violation (null buffer, format or receiving pointer) only returns `EOF`, and a too small array gets a null first element as in `sscanf_s`. A literal mismatch is a matching failure and a field that is only a prefix, such as `0x` or `1e`, does not convert, as C11 requires; FreeBSD's `vfwscanf` reports an input failure for the first and converts the second. Integers out of range are truncated from `strtoll`/`strtoull`. It throws for `%p`, `%S`, `%C`, unknown length modifiers, scanset ranges, `%n` with suppression or width, and narrow `%s`, `%c` or `%[` input that the C-locale `wcrtomb` cannot convert. `swscanf` runs the same scanner without the size arguments of `%s`, `%c` and `%[`, as FreeBSD's [swscanf.c](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/d106002967eff712894ab8b5337bbbff106ce5e0/lib/libc/stdio/swscanf.c) passes its arguments to `vswscanf`, so everything above except the sizes applies to it; it also keeps the null buffer, format and receiving pointer checks returning `EOF`, where C leaves those undefined. Neither variant is confirmed on a PS5 title
- [scePthreadAttr setters and getters](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/src/Attr.cpp) (libkernel) - measured on a PS5 (firmware 13.60): a null or uninitialized attribute returns `EINVAL`, as does a null output of the stack size, detach state and scheduling parameter getters; a policy other than 1 and 3, an inheritance other than 0 and 4, or a priority outside 256 to 767 (or a null parameter) returns `ENOTSUP` (`0x8002002D`); a detach state other than 0 and 1 and a stack size below 16384 return `EINVAL`; any affinity mask is accepted. A null attribute pointer to `scePthreadAttrInit` and a null output of `scePthreadAttrGetaffinity` crashed the payload, so they still throw. A new attribute holds a 64 KiB stack size on the console and 1 MiB here, which the host code that runs on guest threads needs
- [sceKernelGetAvailableCpumask](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/src/Thread.cpp) (libkernel) - returns the default thread affinity, 0x1FFF (CPUs 0 to 12), as KytyPS5 does. Titles pin threads within it (PPSA02664 passes 0x1FFB, Hades II PPSA36082 0x3 and 0x3F); whether CPU 13 is available to a title is not confirmed
- [sceKernelGetCurrentCpu](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) - guest affinities are not applied to host threads, so the host CPU index is mapped into the calling thread's affinity clipped to `sceKernelGetAvailableCpumask` (the available mask when they do not overlap): host CPU n reports the (n modulo the CPU count)-th CPU of that mask, so a thread pinned to one CPU reports that CPU, as a FreeBSD `cpuset` guarantees on the console. Which CPU the console reports for a thread allowed on several is not known; shadPS4 returns 0
- [pthread_attr_setsolosched_np](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/Posix/Attr.cpp) (libkernel) takes `(attr, int)` and stores the value through `scePthreadAttrSetsolosched`, returning `EINVAL` (22) for a null or uninitialized attribute like the other POSIX attribute setters. Only the name is public (aerolib `2+pVfgiEd7A`); the signature and behaviour follow the SCE twin, which [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/947627d10d4ef4b68ba3c0189aab6c368e70a8d2/src/kernel/pthread.cpp#L2188-L2199) and [sharpemu](https://github.com/sharpemu/sharpemu/blob/a2983692930564815a77f81e74c151268f5b935f/src/SharpEmu.Libs/Kernel/KernelPthreadExtendedCompatExports.cs#L891-L913) store as an `int` without a range check, and [PS5PCEM](https://github.com/iStark/PS5PCEM/blob/71de7681f06c5db59a1120a3bb554c86780c3f5f/src/hle/libs/kernel_threading.zig#L1396-L1398) implements this alias over it. On a PS5 (firmware 13.60) only 0 and 16 are accepted; every other value returns `ENOTSUP` (45, `0x8002002D` from the SCE call) and keeps the stored one, and the SCE calls return `EINVAL` for a null or uninitialized attribute. PPSA15878 passes 16. Threads created from the attribute are not scheduled differently; what solo scheduling does on the console is unknown. Returnal (PPSA01285 v03.000.005) stopped on it with `what(): pthread_attr_setsolosched_np_nid_postfix not implemented` (#2872, #1155); LEGO Horizon Adventures (PPSA14632, #734) and Palworld (PPSA23126, #2598) import it
- [malloc, calloc, realloc, memalign, aligned_alloc and reallocalign](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/FileAndHeap.cpp) (libc) - follow FreeBSD 11.4 `contrib/jemalloc/src/jemalloc.c` for failures, not measured on the console: a size the default heap rejects or the guest arena cannot place (including a `calloc` product that overflows) returns null with `errno` `ENOMEM`, and `memalign` and `aligned_alloc` return null with `EINVAL` for an alignment that is zero or not a power of two. FreeBSD's libc has no `memalign`, so setting `errno` there is assumed. A host commit failure for a block that fits in the arena (Windows `ERROR_NOT_ENOUGH_MEMORY`, `ERROR_OUTOFMEMORY` or `ERROR_COMMITMENT_LIMIT`, Linux `ENOMEM`) resets and releases the reserved range and returns null with `ENOMEM`; any other commit failure releases the range and throws. `reallocalign` returns null with `EINVAL` for an invalid alignment and `ENOMEM` like the others, which is an assumption as it is not in FreeBSD. `realloc(ptr, 0)` frees the block and returns null, where FreeBSD 11.4's jemalloc 4.5 resizes it to one byte and returns a block
- [reallocf](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/FileAndHeap.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal, implemented in libc) - follows FreeBSD 11 `lib/libc/stdlib/reallocf.c` on top of this libc's `realloc`, not measured on the console: size 0 frees the block and returns null as `realloc` does here, so the block is not freed twice; when `realloc` fails because the application allocator returns null or the default heap rejects the size, the block is freed through the same allocator, `errno` is `ENOMEM` and null is returned. Other failures (a commit failure that is not out of memory, an unknown pointer) throw, as they do for `realloc`
- [_Dtest](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Math.cpp) (libc) follows Dinkumware double classification matching _FDtest: finite values return -1, subnormals -2, zeros 0, infinities 1, and NaNs 2.
- [sceKernelMapNamedFlexibleMemoryInternal](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/Export.cpp) (libkernel) - flag 0x8000 is accepted and ignored; its meaning is not established. KytyPS5 (`GUEST_MAP_UNKNOWN_8000`) and sharpemu accept it without reading it. Every known caller is the SDK 9 `libc.prx` heap (Astro Bot PPSA21564, PPSA22520), which passes it with read/write protection and uses the mapping as an older `libc.prx` uses `sceKernelMapNamedFlexibleMemory` with flags 0
- [sceKernelMtypeprotect](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/DirectMemory.cpp) (libkernel) - sets the memory type of the direct mappings in the page-rounded range and of the direct memory behind them, as shadPS4 and prosper do (KytyPS5 ignores the type); the type is not validated, flexible pages in the range only get the protection, and a later map of the retyped direct memory reports the type it was allocated with. `sceKernelBatchMap2` operation 4 takes the same path with the entry type
- [Map hints inside a reservation](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/DirectMemory.cpp) (libkernel) - `sceKernelMapDirectMemory` and the flexible and anonymous maps place a mapping without `MAP_FIXED` at its hint address when the whole range lies in a range still reserved by `sceKernelReserveVirtualRange`, as if `MAP_FIXED` were set. Taken from PPSA01474, which reserves 64 GiB and then maps its heaps inside it with hints and no `MAP_FIXED`, and uses the hint addresses afterwards; not measured on the console. Hints outside a reservation, or over a range that is partly mapped, stay hints
- [Mapping hints with no room above them](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/DirectMemory.cpp) (libkernel) - a mapping without `MAP_FIXED` whose hint lies above the guest address space, or has no free range at or above it, is placed from the bottom of the guest arena, as FreeBSD 11.4's [`vm_mmap_object`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/vm/vm_mmap.c#L1569) retries through [`vm_map_find_min`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/vm/vm_map.c#L1555-L1584) from its minimum address. FreeBSD's minimum is the end of the largest possible data segment rather than the start of the arena, and the console's address-space layout is not measured. A homebrew title gives `sceKernelReserveVirtualRange` a hint near its own code, which is a host address here because the relinked executable lies outside the guest arena, so its JIT gets memory more than 2 GiB from its code and does not start
- [Fixed anonymous mmap](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/PosixMemory.cpp) (libkernel) - `MAP_PRIVATE | MAP_ANON | MAP_FIXED` replaces the requested range through the existing guest mapping machinery and zeroes the new memory. `MAP_EXCL` is checked under the allocation-registry mutation lock, including virtual reservations, and returns `ENOMEM` without changing an occupied range; without `MAP_FIXED` it is `EINVAL`. Lengths are rounded to 16 KiB, while fixed addresses must be aligned and the complete rounded range must lie inside the guest arena. This follows FreeBSD 11.4 [argument validation and rounding](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/vm/vm_mmap.c#L226-L299), [fixed replacement](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/vm/vm_map.c#L1455-L1464), and [collision errno](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/vm/vm_mmap.c#L1604-L1613). The console behaviour has not been measured. AnyPS5 starts the guest arena at 0x200000000, so the fixed path rejects the low 0x400000 address requested by a title. The console address-space layout is unmeasured; executable placement outside the host arena is tracked in #5217. Static catalog disassembly finds `0x1012` in the audited titles and `0x5012` in the audited titles; these titles were not run.
- [tzname](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Time.cpp) (libc) points at the host's zone names, read at load and again by `tzset`: the abbreviations a `TZ` string gives (`EST5EDT` is `EST` and `EDT` on both hosts), but on Windows without `TZ` the CRT's full names such as `GMT Standard Time`, which ICU's [uprv_tzname](https://github.com/unicode-org/icu/blob/457157a92aa053e632cc7fcfd0e12f8a943b2d11/icu4c/source/common/putil.cpp#L1244-L1264) cannot map to a zone ID. A zone with no daylight-saving time has `tzname[1]` empty on Windows and equal to `tzname[0]` on Linux, where FreeBSD leaves three spaces. The console's names are not known
- [Guest arena](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/GuestArena.cpp) (libc) - the application map area is assumed to end at 0xFC_0000_0000, as `SCE_KERNEL_APP_MAP_AREA_END_ADDR` does on the PS4. On Windows, host allocations made in the arena before libc loads stay, and a fixed guest mapping over one throws
- [sceLibcInternalBacktraceForGame](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/HeapDiagnostics.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal, implemented in libc) - unknown signature
- [sceLibcInternalHeapErrorReportForGame](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/HeapDiagnostics.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal, implemented in libc) - unknown signature
- [malloc_usable_size](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/ApplicationHeap.cpp) (libc) - with the default allocator it returns the size the block was requested with (the range registered for it), not the rounded-up chunk size Sony's allocator reports; both satisfy "at least the requested size", and bytes past the request would not be write-watched. With an application allocator it calls entry 9 of the replacement table (`malloc_usable_size` in KytyPS5's `MallocReplace` order) and throws when the table leaves it null, like `malloc_stats_fast`
- [_sceLibcInternalThreadAtexit, _sceLibcInternalThreadDtors](../../core/libs/prx/libSceLibcInternal/Export.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal) - FreeBSD `__cxa_thread_atexit` semantics assumed; a null or non-image destructor and an allocation failure throw
- [_sceKernelRtldThreadAtexitIncrement, _sceKernelRtldThreadAtexitDecrement](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Rtld.cpp) (libkernel) - the argument is the `dso` address a title's `libc.prx` passes to `__cxa_thread_atexit` (PPSA14632), which fails when Increment returns nonzero. As in the [fpPS4](https://github.com/red-prig/fpPS4) reimplementation, Increment takes a reference on the loaded image containing the address and Decrement releases one; the variants without the leading underscore are assumed to match
- [sceImeGetPanelPositionAndForm](../../core/libs/prx/libSceIme/Export.cpp) (libSceIme) - the output is taken to be the `PositionAndForm` that `sceImeDialogGetPanelPositionAndForm` fills. While a panel is open it reports the type, position and alignments passed to `sceImeOpen` and the size `sceImeGetPanelSize` gives; the console's values for a panel moved by its own position adjustment are unknown. That a closed panel is reported before a null output is assumed by analogy with `sceImeSetCaret`, not confirmed on a PS5 title
- [_sceLibcInternalForceTlsDestructor](../../core/libs/prx/libSceLibcInternal/Export.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal) - behaviour follows `sceLibcForceTlsDestructor(int)` in a title's `libc.prx` (disassembly): the calling thread's destructors whose dso belongs to the module run newest first, only if the dso's module is loaded and the dso points to itself, in up to 4 passes. The return type is unknown (that function returns nothing), 0 is returned. The module of each dso is looked up when the destructors are forced, not when they are registered as `libc.prx` does
- [acosl](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Math.cpp) (libc) - computed by the host's math library, like the other libc math functions here, not by the console's (FreeBSD msun ld80 code in Sony's `libc.prx`): results can differ from the console's in the last bit, and on Linux glibc sets `errno` to `EDOM` for `acosl` outside [-1, 1] where msun only raises the floating-point exception. `long double` is the x87 80-bit format, as Sony's `frexpl` uses it (`fldt 0x8(%rsp)`, result in `st(0)`)
- [std_execute_once](../../core/libs/prx/libc/Export.cpp) (libc) - assumed to be the plain-named twin of `std::_Execute_once` (`_ZSt13_Execute_onceRSt9once_flagPFiPvS1_PS1_ES1_`, the callee of Dinkumware `call_once`): it delegates there and gets the [thread.once.callonce] semantics already verified for that symbol (one invocation per flag, concurrent callers block until it completes, and a failing or throwing invocation leaves the flag unready so the next caller runs it). The name equivalence is not confirmed on a title, and the callback convention (called with (null, arg, null), non-zero return meaning success) is the twin's, unverified for this symbol
- [Dinkumware mutexes and conditions](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Threads.cpp) (libc) use pointer-to-handle arguments, matching the console aerolib signatures reported in [#3079](https://github.com/boykopovar/AnyPS5/pull/3079). The title's `_Mtx_lock` maps `0` to `_Thrd_success` (0), `0x8002000b` to `_Thrd_busy` (3), and other failures to `_Thrd_error` (4); its internal mutex init recognizes recursive flag `0x100`, while every other type uses default attributes. The observed relock failure for a plain mutex suggests those defaults are error-checking, but that has not been confirmed on hardware. The title's `_Mtx_unlock` and `_Cnd_wait` return success regardless of their internal result. The host implementation treats invalid ownership preconditions as no-ops, and throws for invalid handles, host wait errors and destroying a locked mutex as debug guards; those cases are not confirmed against the console.
- [random, srandom, initstate, setstate](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Random.cpp) (libc) follow FreeBSD 11 [random.c](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/4ab2e064d7950be84256d671a7ae93f87cc6aa36/lib/libc/stdlib/random.c): the additive feedback generator on a default 128-byte `TYPE_3` table, `good_rand` seeding, state sizes rounded down to 8, 32, 64, 128 or 256 bytes, and `setstate` returning the previous state or null for a rear position past the degree. `initstate` below 8 bytes returns null without setting errno, as that file and its manual do (glibc sets `EINVAL`). FreeBSD does not lock the generator; here every call is serialised by one lock, so concurrent callers draw the serial sequence between them. A null state throws, where FreeBSD faults. That the console's `libSceLibcInternal` uses this FreeBSD code and not another generator is unverified
- [qsort](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/FileAndHeap.cpp) (libc) follows FreeBSD 11 [qsort.c](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/5ed7eb0d97ba4436218e810f61bd059acba984c2/lib/libc/stdlib/qsort.c): Bentley and McIlroy's quicksort, with insertion sort below 7 elements and after a partition pass that swaps nothing, so elements that compare equal end in FreeBSD's order and the comparator is called in the same sequence. That the console's libc uses this FreeBSD code is unverified
- [tcgetattr, tcsetattr](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/Terminal.cpp) (libkernel) - every file and pipe descriptor is taken as not a terminal (`ENOTTY`), and a socket gets `EADDRNOTAVAIL` for `AF_INET` and `EOPNOTSUPP` otherwise, as FreeBSD's `in_control` and `pru_control_notsupp` answer a termios ioctl; what descriptors 0 to 2 of a title are on the console, and whether any of them is a terminal there, is unknown
- [select](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Socket.cpp) (libkernel) - descriptor 0 is never reported readable and descriptors 1 and 2 are always writable, as for a console title with no input; reads from descriptor 0 still go to the host stdin. `/dev/random` and `/dev/urandom` are always readable and writable, as FreeBSD reports a seeded random device. Other descriptors that are not libSceNet sockets throw. Dungeons (PPSA16064) selects on descriptor 0
- [__progname](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) - unknown data export
- [environ](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) - data export pointing to a fixed empty environment so titles that import it load; it is not the environment libc's `getenv` reads, so it lists none of those variables and `setenv`/`putenv` changes do not appear in it
- [seteuid, setegid, setuid, setreuid, setgroups, getgroups](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) - the guest process is assumed to run as root, with real, effective and saved user and group ids 0. As in FreeBSD's `sys_seteuid`, `sys_setegid`, `sys_setuid` and `sys_setreuid`, id 0 (or -1 for an unchanged `setreuid` id) is then accepted without a privilege check and changes no credential, so 0 is returned; any other id would change an id, which is not modelled, and throws. `setgroups` keeps the supplementary group list as `kern_setgroups` does (`NGROUPS_MAX` 1023, an empty list leaves the effective group as the only entry, a first entry other than 0 would change the effective group and throws) and `getgroups` reports it. The ids and privileges a title has on a console are unverified
- [pthread_barrierattr_setpshared](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/Posix/Barrier.cpp) (libkernel) - PTHREAD_PROCESS_SHARED is accepted on the assumption that the console follows FreeBSD 11.0 libthr, which accepts it (9.0 rejects it with EINVAL); unverified on hardware. Guest code runs in one process, so a shared barrier behaves as a private one
- [pthread_getname_np](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/Posix/Thread.cpp) (libkernel) - a thread that has already been joined returns `ESRCH` (3) on a PS5 (firmware 13.60), but its object is freed after the join here, so the pointer cannot be told apart from a live thread and its name is read as for a live one
- [sceSslClose](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSsl/Export.cpp) (libSceSsl) - unknown signature
- [sceSslGetSerialNumber](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSsl/Export.cpp) (libSceSsl) - unknown signature
- [sceAudioInIsPadEmbeddedMic](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioIn/Export.cpp) (libSceAudioIn) - name from its NID (`X+4jdIS75P0`); signature `int32_t(int32_t handle)` from the Ghost of Yotei (PPSA26344) call site in #2355; the result's meaning is unconfirmed, so it still throws rather than invent one
- [sceAudioOut2Set3DLatency](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut2System.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) - assumed to take `(user_id, latency)`: KytyPS5 reads `(user_id, output, latency_us)`, but the known calls set just edi and esi; only `(0xFF, 2)` (PPSA26344) and `(0xFF, 1)` (PPSA14632) are accepted
