- [__isfinitel](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Math.cpp) - classifies the x87 80-bit exponent without narrowing to double or performing floating-point comparisons, following [FreeBSD msun](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/main/lib/msun/src/s_isfinite.c). The signature and classification follow FreeBSD; console behaviour is unmeasured. Requires little-endian x87 long double stored in 16 bytes, as the existing guest long-double ABI does.
- [getc_unlocked, putc_unlocked, getchar_unlocked, putchar_unlocked](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/StandardStreams.cpp) (libc) - byte I/O uses the host unlocked operations; guest EOF/error flags and macro counters follow `FileStream`. Linux status queries use `feof_unlocked`/`ferror_unlocked` because [glibc `feof`](https://github.com/bminor/glibc/blob/d2097651cc57834dbfcaa102ddfacae0d86cfb66/libio/feof.c) and [`ferror`](https://github.com/bminor/glibc/blob/d2097651cc57834dbfcaa102ddfacae0d86cfb66/libio/ferror.c) can acquire the stream lock. Windows uses [the CRT no-lock byte operations](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cpp/c-runtime-library/reference/fgetc-nolock-fgetwc-nolock) and `feof`/`ferror`, verified under a held stream lock on UCRT. Console behavior and other host CRTs remain unverified.
- [fgetws, getwc, getwchar, putwc, putwchar](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/FormattingWide.cpp) (libc) - wide stream I/O encodes and decodes wide characters as UTF-8 on the underlying byte stream; `fgetws` reads characters into the destination buffer until newline or EOF and null-terminates, returning null on stream error. Console wide stream buffering and encoding details remain unverified.

- [libSceOpusDec](../../core/libs/prx/libSceOpusDec/Export.cpp) uses FFmpeg for 48000 Hz mono/stereo signed-16 decoding. The six signatures, 640-byte opaque state allocation and byte-count return follow [public PS5 decoder research](https://github.com/blackbearreloaded/ps5-hardware-audio-decoding/blob/26be5a12f92b2cdc67f8a9e082fd69ae0e9a35a2/docs/DECODING.md#direct-opus-abi) and [ProsperoRadio](https://github.com/blackbearreloaded/ProsperoRadio/blob/2c4029c3a8f43e9e1dfef98374d94d99b14d745d/src/opus_decoder.cpp). Console PCM equivalence and error codes are unverified. Other sample rates, packet loss concealment, invalid inputs and termination with live decoders throw. Context identifiers and host decoder state are private registries; the firmware's opaque state layout and error recovery are unknown.
- [mkstemp and isatty](../../core/libs/prx/libScePosixForWebKit/Filesystem.cpp) (libScePosixForWebKit) follow FreeBSD's [temporary-file API](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/stable/9/lib/libc/stdio/mktemp.c) and [terminal check](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/stable/9/lib/libc/gen/isatty.c), without console verification. `mkstemp` replaces all trailing `X` characters, accepts a fixed name, and creates files through the guest path resolver with exclusive read/write access and mode 0600. Windows file permissions follow the existing kernel file implementation. `isatty` tests Windows console handles rather than all character devices; files, pipes and guest sockets return `ENOTTY`, closed descriptors return `EBADF`.
- [arc4random](../../core/libs/prx/libScePosixForWebKit/Export.cpp) (libScePosixForWebKit) returns four bytes from the same thread-local host `std::random_device` as `arc4random_buf`, uniform over the 32-bit range like FreeBSD 11.4's RC4 keystream in [arc4random.c](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/lib/libc/gen/arc4random.c). The console's generator and its reseeding are unknown; `arc4random_uniform`, `arc4random_stir` and `arc4random_addrandom` are not in the module's export set and are absent
- [Dinkumware thread exports](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAmpr/Export.cpp) (libSceAmpr) follow the Dinkumware threads library shipped with [Visual C++ 2013](https://github.com/adityaramesh/concurrency_runtime/tree/master/VS2013_SP2/crt/src/thr) (`cthread.c`, `xtime.c`) and its `atomic.c`, [microsoft/STL `cthread.cpp`](https://github.com/microsoft/STL/blob/main/stl/src/cthread.cpp) and C11 Annex K, without console verification. `_Thrd_t` is taken to be the guest `pthread_t`, as the `std::_Pad::_Launch(pthread**)` export suggests: `_Thrd_join` returns `_Thrd_error` (4) when `pthread_join` fails and stores the thread's return value truncated to `int`. `_Thrd_sleep` reads a 16-byte xtime (64-bit seconds and nanoseconds) on the guest `CLOCK_REALTIME`, normalizes the nanoseconds and sleeps through `nanosleep`, so it is a cancellation point; a null xtime or one whose normalization overflows throws. The console's `_Thrd_sleep` (disassembled from a title's `libc.prx`) also takes an optional `xtime*` of remaining time and returns `int`; here the sleep is never cut short, so it returns 0 and leaves the remaining time untouched. `_Thrd_join` (32-bit store of the result, 0 or 4) and the spin lock (byte exchange loop, no yield) match that disassembly. The shared_ptr spin lock is one process-wide flag that yields while it spins. `wcstombs_s` is `wcsrtombs_s` with a fresh conversion state, so `*retval` excludes the terminating null as in Annex K, not as in the Microsoft CRT, and `_TRUNCATE` has no meaning
- [_Cnd_signal, _Cnd_init_with_name](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAmpr/Export.cpp) (libSceAmpr) - take a pointer to the handle slot like the libc `_Cnd_*` exports from [#3079](https://github.com/boykopovar/AnyPS5/pull/3079) and act on the same conditions. `_Cnd_signal` wakes one waiter and returns `_Thrd_success` (0), as `_Cnd_broadcast` does; whether the console maps a failing internal signal to `_Thrd_error` is not known. The signature `_Cnd_init_with_name(_Cnd_t*, const char* name)` is inferred from [fpPS4 `ps4_mtx_internal.pas`](https://github.com/red-prig/fpPS4/blob/trunk/src/libcinternal/ps4_mtx_internal.pas), where the PS4 `_Mtx_init` is `_Mtx_init_with_name` with the name `SceLibcI_Mtx` and the name only reaches the pthread object; here it is `_Cnd_init` and the name, which may be null, is ignored. Neither is confirmed on a PS5 `libc.prx`. `_Thrd_id` still throws: microsoft/STL returns an `unsigned int` thread id, but whether the PS5 returns the `pthread_t` or a kernel thread id, and at what width, is not known
- [libSceZlib](../../core/libs/prx/libSceZlib/Export.cpp) completes RFC1950 decompression during submission and supports one outstanding request, null-timeout waits and one result retrieval. Timed waits, multiple outstanding requests, repeated retrieval and finalization with an unread result throw. The 64-bit initialization length and 64 KiB output limit with 2 KiB destination alignment follow [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/5dfdb4b0b166e83dc9ae275001afb695f12c7174/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Compression/Zlib.cs); the PS4 initialization length is 32 bits. shadPS4 also requires the output length to be a multiple of 2 KiB and returns `0x81120016` for invalid arguments; this implementation accepts any length up to 64 KiB (SharpProspero documents only the pointer alignment) and throws on invalid arguments. Scratch memory is not used, and its firmware size/alignment requirements are unknown. The initialization errors follow SharpProspero; malformed/checksum/truncated streams report `0x811200FF` and insufficient output `0x8112001C` as in [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/0fe263a4760dfbfa973366890061749b4af0de97/src/core/libraries/zlib/zlib.cpp). PS5 error precedence, scheduling, completion selection, result consumption and cancellation are unverified. The payload SDK's FreeBSD kernel `z_stream` and host errno are not this library's ABI.
- [Windows file timestamps](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/include/WindowsFileTime.hpp) use the host filesystem's timestamp range and resolution. `utimes` and `futimes` reject timestamps outside nonnegative signed 64-bit Windows file-time ticks with `EINVAL`, including exactly 1601-01-01 00:00:00 UTC, whose zero value means "leave unchanged" to `SetFileTime`. Guest directory descriptors still resolve through their recorded path. Filesystems with coarser timestamp resolution cannot retain every guest microsecond
- [fchdir](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel_sys/Export.cpp) (libkernel_sys) changes the guest working directory to the descriptor's directory: on Windows through the path recorded when the descriptor was opened, on Linux through the `/proc/self/fd` link target. It returns `EBADF` for a descriptor that is not open and `ENOTDIR` for one that is not a directory. FreeBSD `change_dir`'s search-permission check (`EACCES`) is not modelled, and a directory renamed after it was opened is still reached through the recorded path. Console behaviour is unverified
- [Windows file identity](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/NativeStat.cpp) (libkernel) - `stat` and `fstat` report the volume serial number as `st_dev` and the 64-bit file index folded into the guest's 32-bit `st_ino` (never 0), so two different files can in principle share an `st_ino`; the fold is not taken from a reference. `st_nlink` is clamped to 65535, the width of the guest field. The call keeps the `_wstat64` values when `GetFileInformationByHandle` fails
- On Linux, [direct memory](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/DirectMemory.cpp) lives in one sparse memfd at file offset = physical address, so the [GPU imports](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestBufferMemory.cpp) can share one udmabuf buffer object per 64 MiB of it. Released direct memory is not punched out of the file, because a live import of its chunk pins the pages and the GPU would keep reading the old ones: its pages stay committed (the process keeps the high-water mark of the direct memory it used) and are zeroed when the range is allocated again.
- [Per-face depth bias](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Draw.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver): Vulkan has one depth bias per draw, so a draw that rasterizes both faces with different front and back bias (PA_SU_SC_MODE_CNTL, PA_SU_POLY_OFFSET_FRONT/BACK_*) is recorded twice, front faces with the back faces culled and then back faces with the front faces culled. The vertex stages run twice, so a vertex shader that writes memory writes twice, and all front faces land before all back faces instead of in primitive order, which differs only for blending or stencil updates that depend on the order.
- [Separate alpha blend into 8_8 SWAP_ALT targets](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Pipeline.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver): the second channel of a two-channel target with `COMP_SWAP` 1 holds the alpha export, but Vulkan blends it with the color equation of the R8G8 attachment, so a draw whose alpha equation differs from its color equation is recorded twice, the first channel with the color equation and the second with the alpha factors and operation moved into the color equation. The vertex stages run twice, so a vertex shader that writes memory writes twice, and occlusion counts include both passes. Draws that write depth or stencil, pixel shaders that write memory and factors of the destination alpha throw. That the hardware blends the second channel with the alpha equation is taken from Mesa's `si_translate_colorswap` and radeonsi's use of it for luminance-alpha formats, not measured on hardware.
- [Compute LDS beyond the device's shared-memory limit](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) lives in a coherent storage buffer with one slice of `LDS bytes / 4 + 1` dwords per workgroup (the last dword is the atomic lock), shared by all such dispatches and grown in 1 MiB steps up to `maxStorageBufferRange`; a dispatch whose slices do not fit throws. It is slower than shared memory and its cost was not measured. An indirect dispatch of such a program always reads its group counts on the CPU. Its results are pinned against the in-limit semantics in `agc_driver_lds_beyond_device_limit`, not measured on hardware.
- [Graphics pipeline libraries](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/PipelineLibrary.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver): a pipeline is fast-linked at the draw and relinked with `VK_PIPELINE_CREATE_LINK_TIME_OPTIMIZATION_BIT_EXT` on a background thread, and binds the optimized one from the next draw on. Draws recorded before that use the fast link, whose vertex stage keeps the outputs the fragment stage does not read: on RADV (Radeon 660M, Mesa 26.2.4) 346 of 483 Astro's Playroom vertex shaders paired with a fragment stage that reads none of them used more VGPRs (median 8 more, up to 2.5 times), and on an RTX 4090 13 of 15 PPSA36082 vertex shaders up to 68 instead of 38 (#2242). The optimized link compiles to the same code as a whole pipeline
- [sceAvPlayerGetVideoData](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAvPlayer/src/Source.cpp) (libSceAvPlayer) in sync mode `SCE_AVPLAYER_AV_SYNC_MODE_NONE` skips queued frames that the audio clock has already passed while the title pulls audio, so a title that requests video slower than the film's frame rate keeps the picture with the sound. Whether the console hands out every frame in this mode is unknown. PPSA12544 requests 7-19 frames per second for a 30 fps cinematic
- [`APS5_THREAD_PRIORITY=rt`](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/ThreadPriority.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) lowers the process's hard `RLIMIT_RTTIME` when it is above `APS5_THREAD_RTTIME_US` or rtkit's `RTTimeUSecMax`, because [rtkit](https://github.com/heftig/rtkit/blob/c295fa849f52b487be6433e69e08b46251950399/rtkit-daemon.c#L592-L637) (`verify_process_rttime`) reads the hard limit from `/proc/<pid>/limits` and refuses real-time scheduling when it is higher. An unprivileged process cannot raise the hard limit again, so it stays lowered for the process and its threads even when rtkit then refuses the request (burst limit, user or start-time checks) and the thread throws. rtkit's `MaxRealtimePriority` and `RTTimeUSecMax` are read first, so an unreachable rtkit or a priority above its maximum leaves the limit untouched. The lowered limit only matters for threads that later run under a real-time policy
- AMD image descriptors with `TYPE=0` are treated as null for read-only image slots even when the base and other words are nonzero: `image_load` on a gfx1035 with such a T# returns 0,0,0,0, and the same descriptors were seen in direct reads in PPSA01341 on an RTX 4070. Writable and atomic image slots and entries of an image table keep faulting because there is no hardware measurement for them, and reserved `TYPE=1` also faults (gfx1035 reads it as zeros too); gfx1013 behavior is unverified. On Metal the image binding treats `TYPE=0` as null ([IsNullTextureResource](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestTextureResource.cpp)), so the image binds no texture and reads zero (`anyps5_metal_driver_semantics_imagetablerecord`). Upstream handles the Vulkan runtime binding in a separate PR (#4779).
- On a device without VK_EXT_robustness2's `nullDescriptor`, such as MoltenVK, a null image descriptor in a typed heap is a [placeholder image](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/PlaceholderImages.cpp) of that heap's type, 1×1 and cleared to zero, which every null descriptor of the heap shares. Reads give zeros, as `nullDescriptor` allows, but stores and atomics to it are kept, so a later read of a null storage image can return them, and a depth comparison compares against depth 0. A heap whose placeholder the device cannot create, for example 1D or 3D depth, 64-bit atomics without 64-bit image atomics, or multisampled storage without `shaderStorageImageMultisample`, throws when a null descriptor reaches it.
- [libSceHttp2 asynchronous requests](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHttp2/Export.cpp) (libSceHttp2) never reach the network: `sceHttp2SendRequestAsync` accepts the request, queues a completion whose result is the library's network error and triggers the user event named by the kqueue option (equeue at offset 0, event id at 8, user data at 16, layout inferred from a title); `sceHttp2WaitAsync` returns that completion. The completion's `event_type` value is unknown and left 0. a title completes its request object only after `sceHttp2SendRequestAsync` returns, and its handler for a synchronous failure calls `shared_from_this` before that, so a send must not fail synchronously. A title passes null kqueue and extra options, then calls `sceHttp2WaitAsync` with a 24-byte result and a 2,000,000 timeout, and checks the result at offset 8. A null kqueue option therefore retains the same offline completion for direct waiting without an event notification. The timeout is not used because the offline result is already complete. This path follows the original title call and caller control flow, not a console measurement. A supplied kqueue with a missing user event, an extra option, and a wait with no completed operation still throw
- [system](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) runs no command. It follows FreeBSD 11's `lib/libc/stdlib/system.c` for a sandbox without `/bin/sh`: a null command returns 1 unconditionally, as that version does, and any command returns the wait status of a child that could not exec the shell and called `_exit(127)` (`0x7f00`). Whether the console's application sandbox has no shell, and whether `vfork` succeeds there (FreeBSD returns -1 when it fails), is unverified
- [sceKernelWriteThrottlingStatus](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/Stdio.cpp) (libkernel) reports no write throttling: the host SSD has no write budget, so the first qword of the 32-byte status is set to `UINT32_MAX` and the rest is zeroed. PPSA17221 reads only that qword: it multiplies it by 64 KiB and compares the product with byte thresholds up to 0x4AF00000 to pick one of five tiers. When the call fails the title assumes 0x4B000001 bytes, which selects the same top tier as `UINT32_MAX`. What the value measures on hardware (a remaining budget or a rate) and the meaning of the other three qwords are unknown
- [/dev/random and /dev/urandom](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/RandomDevice.cpp) (libkernel) opened for reading return a descriptor backed by the host null device whose reads are filled from `std::random_device`; both paths behave the same, never block, and positioned reads ignore the offset (FreeBSD `random(4)`). Only the exact paths are recognised, a `dup`ed descriptor reads as an empty file, `fstat`, `lseek`, `mmap` and `select` see the null device, and opening them for writing throws
- [Wide-character conversions](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Multibyte.cpp) (libc) - The wide `wcsto*` functions parse the ASCII prefix of the guest string, as the C locale accepts only ASCII white space and digits. [`wcsrtombs`](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Multibyte.cpp) uses the guest's single-byte C locale through `wcrtomb`; other encodings and shift states are not supported
- [realpath](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/RealPath.cpp) (libc) follows FreeBSD 11's [`realpath.c`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/stable/11/lib/libc/stdlib/realpath.c) except for symlinks. The console's guest filesystem has none, so a host symlink under the guest root is followed for existence and type but not expanded, and `..` drops the previous component lexically, as `Resolve` does for every guest path. Through a host link `linkdir -> sub/deep`, `linkdir/../x` gives `x` beside the link, not beside its target.
- [strtod, strtof, strtold](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Strings.cpp) (libc) - `NAN(n-char-sequence)` converts to the quiet NaN that `nan` and `nanf` return, negated after a minus sign, and ignores the sequence as they ignore their tag. This is inferred from C17 7.12.11.2p2, which makes `nan("n")` equivalent to `strtod("NAN(n)", NULL)`, and not observed on the console. No console `nanl` was observed, so `strtold` returns the x87 default quiet NaN (exponent `0x7fff`, significand `0xc000000000000000`)
- [nan and nanf](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Math.cpp) (libc) return fixed quiet NaN values (`0x7fc00000` and `0x7ff8000000000000`), ignoring the tag argument as observed in the console's `libc.prx` (both exports load a global constant via `mov rax,[rip+..]; vmovsd xmm0,[rax]; ret`); VLC (PPSA85300) stops on nanf at start-up after strsep.
- [SPIRV-Tools optimization](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvOptimizer.cpp) skips CCP and Simplification for modules with storage-only 8- or 16-bit types because the pinned folder produces illegal narrow constants. This also disables unrelated constant folding; the restricted full pipeline mirrors the pinned performance recipe.
- [SRT signed comparison](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/SrtWalker/SrtEvaluator.cpp) evaluates `SLessThan32` as signed 32-bit less-than, matching the existing SPIR-V backend and [SPIR-V `OpSLessThan`](https://registry.khronos.org/SPIR-V/specs/unified1/SPIRV.html#OpSLessThan). Signed boundary descriptor expressions are tested on the host; native RDNA2/PS5 execution and title behavior remain unverified.
- On macOS the [write watch](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/GuestWriteWatch.cpp) write-protects watched pages and records the first write to each from a SIGBUS/SIGSEGV handler, because macOS has no counterpart to `userfaultfd` write-protect or `MEM_WRITE_WATCH`. The protection is real, so:
  - a system call that writes into a protected page fails with `EFAULT` instead of faulting. `GuestArena::HostWrite` lifts the protection for the calls it brackets (the file reads); a host call that writes guest memory without it (sockets, `clock_gettime` and the like into a watched page) fails;
  - code that changes the protection of watched pages, or maps over them, has to call `GuestWriteWatchProtectionChanged`, and code that asks the kernel for a page's protection has to ask `GuestWriteWatchProtection` first, since a watched page is read-only between a collection and the next write. On macOS, [DirectMemory](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/DirectMemory.cpp) reports its `mprotect` calls through `GuestWriteWatchProtectionChanged` and re-registers ranges it maps over; nothing asks `GuestWriteWatchProtection` yet;
  - the handler is installed when libc loads and passes other faults to the handler installed before it. A handler installed later has to do the same.
- [Pending storage-image results](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Texture.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) are stored when something reads their memory, not when the GPU work that wrote them completes. A title that waits for that work, frees the image and reuses its memory can have the new data overwritten by the late store (#1970). A title's own CPU reads are not seen either, so a title that reads the results after waiting for a label reads stale memory. `APS5_STORE_AT_FLIP=1` stores every pending result at each flip and `APS5_STORE_AT_LABEL=1` before each label the GPU writes; both store every pending image, not only the ones the waited-for work wrote, and neither is on by default.
- Windows host-pointer imports of the guest mapping itself are compared instead of write-watched: a driver can mark imported pages dirty after the synchronous import window. Shared memory imported through a separate writable alias retains protection-based tracking on the guest views; driver access to the import alias does not disarm those views. [GuestMemory](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/GuestMemory.cpp) excludes every overlapping 64 KiB tracker block while its backing is retained. A full block replaced by fresh private backing regains tracking and gets a new generation; protection changes, partial replacements and shared remappings do not restore it. This preserves CPU edits through byte comparisons but disables snapshot reuse and unit shadows for excluded blocks; restoring tracking on import retirement without replacing the backing and measuring the comparison cost remain unimplemented.
- [Storage-image units under a Linux import window](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Texture.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) that the window stamped as a possible CPU store (`BlockMaybeWritten`) are compared with the bytes the image was uploaded from or written back as, and are unchanged when those still match. A CPU store of the bytes already there within the window cannot be told apart from an import that resolves the write protection without storing, as the NVIDIA driver (610.57.04) does for every imported page, so such a store does not win over pending results. Images tracked by layer instead of by 64 KiB unit still read the window as a change and upload again.
- [shm_open](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/SharedMemory.cpp) (libkernel) creates only anonymous objects (`SHM_ANON`), each backed by a host anonymous file: a `memfd` on Linux, a delete-on-close temporary file on Windows. The flag checks follow FreeBSD 11's `kern_shm_open`; the mode is ignored. Named objects throw. The descriptor cannot be mapped yet: `mmap` accepts only private anonymous mappings and fails a descriptor mapping with `EOPNOTSUPP`, so the known caller (the bundled Mesa in PPSA59322, which truncates the object and maps it `MAP_SHARED`) gets that failure
- [sceKernelAddReadEvent and sceKernelAddWriteEvent](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Equeue/Equeue.cpp) (libkernel): a write event reports the socket's send buffer size (`SO_SNDBUF`) in `data`, not the free space left in it, as Windows has no query for that space. Only guest sockets are supported; other descriptors and a low watermark above 1 throw
- [sceNetSetsockopt](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNet/Export.cpp) (libSceNet) sets `SO_RCVTIMEO` and `SO_SNDTIMEO` as the host socket's timeouts. On Windows a receive or send on a stream socket that times out fails with `ETIMEDOUT`, where FreeBSD returns `EAGAIN` or the partial count: Winsock leaves the connection in an indeterminate state after a timeout and reports no partial count ([SOL_SOCKET options](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/winsock/sol-socket-socket-options)), so FreeBSD's behaviour needs the timeout waited for without the host option. A receive on a datagram socket that times out fails with `EAGAIN`
- [sceNetSendmsg and sceNetRecvmsg](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNet/Export.cpp) (libSceNet) copy the scatter/gather list through one host buffer and accept only the flags `sceNetSend`/`sceNetRecv` accept. Sending control data throws; a receive never returns control data (`msg_controllen` 0), since no socket option that queues it is supported
- [close](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/Stdio.cpp) and [setsockopt](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Udp.cpp) (libkernel) reach a [libSceNet](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNet/Export.cpp) socket through hooks libSceNet registers, as `select` polls them; the other libkernel socket calls do not. libSceNet numbers its sockets from 32 in its own table, so a CRT file descriptor and a socket can share a number; `close` then closes the socket. On the console both are one kernel descriptor space
- [close, sceKernelClose](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/HostStreams.cpp) (libkernel) - descriptors 0 to 2 that are open when libkernel loads belong to the host process: closing one fails with `EBADF` and leaves it open. The console's result is unknown. One title calls `sceKernelClose(0)` repeatedly; closing the host stream let the host C runtime give descriptor 0 to the next file opened, which the next call then closed
- [Additional content](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAppContent/Export.cpp) (libSceAppContent) is not installed or mounted: `sceAppContentAddcontMount` answers `NOT_FOUND` for every entitlement label and `sceAppContentAddcontUnmount` for every mount point, as no add-content mount is ever handed out; `sceAppContentGetAddcontInfo` answers `DRM_NO_ENTITLEMENT` for every label and `sceAppContentGetAddcontInfoList` reports 0 entries; `sceAppContentAddcontEnqueueDownload` answers `PARAMETER` for a null label and `NOT_FOUND` otherwise, since no download is ever queued; `sceAppContentAddcontDelete` answers `PARAMETER` for a null label and `NOT_FOUND` otherwise, since no additional content is ever installed. The signature follows [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/main/src/SharpProspero/Interop/AppContent/AppContent.cs); no reference gives the console's error codes, so both are unverified
- [Content export sessions](../../core/libs/prx/libSceContentExport/Export.cpp) (libSceContentExport): `sceContentExportStart` hands out positive session ids, at most 10 live, which `sceContentExportFinish` closes. The signatures follow [Orbital](https://github.com/AlexAltea/orbital/blob/cc408367da6a6ebbf5ac1ac559fefeadc73b2831/tools/ida/db_types.json) and the limit `MaxExecution` [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/5dfdb4b0b166e83dc9ae275001afb695f12c7174/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Content/ContentExport.cs); the parameter layouts follow [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/443f022450f6857256e780daa3d5a14029a748f7/src/core/libraries/content_export/content_export.h). The host has no media library, so `sceContentExportFromDataWithThumbnail` throws once the session is checked, before calling either data callback, and no export ever runs. `sceContentExportGetProgress` and `sceContentExportCancel` throw for a live session, as their result with no running export is unknown. An eleventh `sceContentExportStart` and `sceContentExportTerm` with live sessions throw: whether the console answers `EXECUTION_MAX` or `BUSY` there, or ends the sessions, is unverified. Session id values and error precedence are unverified
- [Guest import registries](../../core/relinker/relinker/src/guest/GuestModuleBuilder.cpp) - declared host imports of bundled modules are recorded with their module name and relocation target, even when another guest module exports the same stripped NID. No-op relocations are omitted. Registries describe import bindings, not implementation correctness or runtime reachability.
- [Executable guest imports](../../core/relinker/relinker/src/guest/GuestModuleBuilder.cpp) - on Linux and macOS, a relocated executable import resolved to one guest provider requires that module and its dependencies at startup, even when it was discovered through `--module-dir` without a DT_NEEDED entry. Unreferenced symbols and no-op relocations do not require an otherwise deferred module. Console module-start ordering is unmeasured.
- [Runtime-loaded modules](../../core/relinker/relinker/src/guest/GuestModuleBuilder.cpp) (`--module-dir`) that another module needs are initialized at startup with no start arguments, before the module that needs them is loaded; the console is assumed to start them when that module is loaded.
- [sceKernelPread, sceKernelPwrite, sceKernelPreadv, sceKernelPwritev](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/Stdio.cpp) (libkernel) on Windows transfer at the given offset with an overlapped `ReadFile`/`WriteFile` and restore the descriptor's file pointer afterwards. Guest scalar transfers, positioned transfers, vectored transfers and seeks share a position lock across duplicated descriptors; a vector holds it for the whole operation. Independently opened descriptors and other processes do not share that lock, so vector transfers over their overlapping file ranges are not coordinated. Handles that are not disk files return `ESPIPE`. A pipe or console `readv` can return a short read when no more input is available; each iovec is transferred separately, so a failure after the first transfer returns the bytes already done
- [sceKernelAioSubmitReadCommands, sceKernelAioSubmitWriteCommands and their Multiple variants](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Aio/src/Aio.cpp) (libkernel) on Windows transfer through the same positioned path as `sceKernelPread`/`sceKernelPwrite` and restore the descriptor's file pointer afterwards, so a concurrent `read`/`write` on the same descriptor can see the moved pointer in between, unlike on the console
- [sceKernelGetFsSandboxRandomWord](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Sandbox.cpp) (libkernel) returns a fixed directory name, `gkbHtXc3Wq`. On the console the name is the process's randomized path (syscall 602, `randomized_path`), and the sandbox exposes system files under it, such as `common/lib` and `common/cert`. Nothing is mounted under the name here, so `/gkbHtXc3Wq/common/...` resolves under the guest root like any other path and does not exist
- [sceKernelSync](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/General.cpp) (libkernel) flushes only the host files and directories recorded through `RecordWrittenPath_nid_no_patch` (the libc, libkernel, save data and app content file functions) and their changed parent directories. A host write in a library that does not record its path is not flushed by `sceKernelSync`
- [sceKernelSync](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/General.cpp) (libkernel) on Windows skips a recorded file it cannot open for writing (read-only, or held open by a handle that denies write sharing), since `FlushFileBuffers` needs a writable handle. On Linux, it skips a recorded file it cannot open for reading. Such a file's data may not reach the disk before a host crash; the guest reads the same data either way
- [getcontext and setcontext](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/UserContext.cpp) (libkernel) and [makecontext](../../core/libs/prx/libSceLibcInternal/UserContext.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal) use FreeBSD 11's amd64 `ucontext_t` (`mcontext` at 0x10, `mc_len` 800, `uc_link` at 0x330, `uc_stack` at 0x338, 0x370 bytes), the layout homebrew built against FreeBSD's headers passes: PPSA99039's OpenSSL `ASYNC` (`crypto/async/arch/async_posix.c`) captures into a 0x370-byte stack buffer. `getcontext` writes the 0x330 bytes before `uc_link`, as FreeBSD's `UC_COPY_SIZE`. The console's own layout for these functions is not verified; the exception handler's context is a different one (see `sceKernelRaiseException`)
- [makecontext](../../core/libs/prx/libSceLibcInternal/UserContext.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal) follows FreeBSD 11's `lib/libc/amd64/gen/makecontext.c`: at most six arguments, a stack of at least `MINSIGSTKSZ` (2048) bytes, and `exit(0)` when the function returns with no `uc_link`. The console's version is not verified
- [getcontext and setcontext](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/UserContext.cpp) (libkernel) keep the 512-byte FXSAVE image in `mc_fpstate` (x87, MXCSR, XMM0-15), so the upper halves of YMM0-15 are neither saved nor restored, and `getcontext` leaves `mc_xfpustate` and `mc_xfpustate_len` 0. `getcontext` sets `mc_fpformat` and `mc_ownedfp` to FreeBSD's `_MC_FPFMT_XMM` and `_MC_FPOWNED_FPU`, `mc_cs` and `mc_ss` to the host selectors, and `mc_flags`, `mc_fsbase` and `mc_gsbase` to 0. `setcontext` loads `mc_fpstate` whatever `mc_fpformat` and `mc_ownedfp` say, and restores neither the segment selectors, `mc_fsbase` and `mc_gsbase` nor the extended state, which FreeBSD restores when `mc_flags` asks for them
- [makecontext](../../core/libs/prx/libSceLibcInternal/UserContext.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal) on Windows pins its stack writable until a later `makecontext` stack overlaps it, since nothing tells the library when the guest frees the stack
- [setcontext](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/UserContext.cpp) (libkernel) and [longjmp](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/JumpBuffer.cpp) (libc) on Windows set the thread's stack bounds in the TEB to the stack they switch to: a `makecontext` stack, or else the committed region around the target stack pointer. A switch to any other guest stack, such as a context built by hand, gets the bounds of that region only
- [sceKernelInstallExceptionHandler](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Exception.cpp) records the handler, but a host fault still ends the process: only signals raised with `sceKernelRaiseException` are delivered. Unity's PS5Util module installs a SIGUSR1 handler that reads `uc_mcontext.mc_rsp` (offset 0xf8 of the context) and raises SIGUSR1 on other threads to stop them for garbage collection
- [sceKernelRaiseException](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Exception.cpp) on Linux sends the host SIGUSR1, which the library takes over, to the target thread, so the handler runs wherever the thread was interrupted, emulator code included. On the console kernel objects are system calls and libthr defers signals while a thread holds its internal locks; here a handler that needs a host lock the interrupted code holds deadlocks, and a thread that the handler keeps waiting keeps its host locks. Host waits resume after the handler. The host calls Linux does not restart end early: sleeps that do not retry return before their time, and the poll of `sceNetEpollWait` and socket calls with a send or receive timeout return `EINTR` to the guest
- [sceKernelRaiseException](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Exception.cpp) builds the handler's `ucontext` with FreeBSD's amd64 layout (16-byte sigmask, `mcontext` at 0x40, `mc_rsp` at 0xf8); the console's layout is not verified beyond the fields PS5Util reads
- [sceKernelRaiseException](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Exception.cpp) does not consult the target thread's signal mask
- [pthread_sigmask](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Signals.cpp) (libkernel) shares the process-wide mask of `sigprocmask` and `_sigprocmask`, which keeps only the first 32 signals; FreeBSD keeps a mask per thread
- [sceKernelRaiseException](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Exception.cpp) reaches a thread that waits in an alertable `TimedWait` wait through an APC. A thread blocked anywhere else, including all waits under `APS5_COARSE_TIMED_WAITS` and host waits inside the emulator, is redirected and runs the handler only when that wait returns
- [sceKernelRaiseException](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Exception.cpp) on Windows runs the handler wherever the target thread was interrupted. It waits only while the thread is inside winpthreads or restoring a context through `NtContinue`, and throws if that lasts 1 s, so a handler that calls into other host code the thread was interrupted in, such as the C runtime's heap or a lock of the emulator, can deadlock on it or reenter it
- [sceAgcDebugRaiseException](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Misc/src/Debug.cpp) (libSceAgc) - logs the exception id and returns 0, as retail hardware does nothing per [OpenAGC](https://github.com/OpenAGC/OpenAGC/blob/3c7e8072f0d7684e930454794fd5c02056946d73/src/game_compat.c); the trap (`ud2`) OpenAGC describes for development hardware is not reproduced. Neither is verified on a console. The `uint32` exception id is [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/6f0cddd4404ec5203e71fea77e75fc373299ba3f/src/libs/agc.cpp)'s claim; OpenAGC declares no arguments, so the logged id may be leftover register contents
- [sigaltstack](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Signals.cpp) (libkernel) records each thread's alternate signal stack and reports it back, following FreeBSD's `kern_sigaltstack` (`EINVAL` for flags other than `SS_DISABLE`, `ENOMEM` below `MINSIGSTKSZ` 2048), but handlers never run on it, so `SS_ONSTACK` and `EPERM` are never reported
- [libSceUlt queues](../../core/libs/prx/libSceUlt/Export.cpp) use preallocated host storage instead of the supplied guest work area; its existing size formula and the 512-byte object layouts remain unverified. Waiting-queue pool thread limits and waiter priority ordering are not implemented. Finalization wakes blocked queue callers with `ULT_ERROR_STATE`.
- [sceUltWaitingQueueResourcePoolDestroy](../../core/libs/prx/libSceUlt/Export.cpp) refuses with `ULT_ERROR_BUSY` only when a thread is currently waiting on a pool-backed mutex, semaphore or queue, matching the PS5 libSceUlt disassembly (0x473a in or55417wcDk); bound but idle objects do not block pool destruction. Waiting-queue pool guest work area node accounting is not implemented.
- [sceUltMutexDestroy](../../core/libs/prx/libSceUlt/Export.cpp) returns `ULT_ERROR_BUSY` when the mutex is held or has waiting threads, tracked via owner and waiter counts in `UltMutexState`. Prosper (`hle_ult.cpp`) logs a destroy of a held mutex and returns `ULT_OK`; AnyPS5 returns `ULT_ERROR_BUSY` to prevent destroying active mutexes.
- [scePthreadJoin, scePthreadCreate](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/src/Thread.cpp) (libkernel) - on a PS5 (firmware 13.60) joining a thread a second time returns `ESRCH` (`0x80020003`); here a joined thread is released, so a second join reads freed memory. A null entry crashed the payload and still throws. Joining the calling thread returns `EDEADLK` and a zeroed attribute creates a default thread, as on the console
- [Thread cancellation](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/src/Thread.cpp) (libkernel) follows FreeBSD libthr (states and types 0/1 and 0/2, `PTHREAD_CANCELED` 1, the `_pthread_cleanup_info` node layout), not measured on the console. A request is acted on only in `scePthreadTestcancel`, the condition variable, `sem` and `scePthreadSem` waits, `scePthreadJoin` and the sleeps, and their POSIX names; file and socket calls are not cancellation points. A sleep is not interrupted: the thread exits when it ends. The asynchronous type acts like the deferred one, except at the two set calls.
- [cpuset_setaffinity](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/Posix/Thread.cpp) (libkernel) - only the calling thread or the calling process is handled. Bits outside `sceKernelGetAvailableCpumask` are dropped, and a mask with none of those bits fails with `EDEADLK`, as FreeBSD does when the new set has no CPU in common with the parent set
- [dup and dup2](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/Stdio.cpp) (libkernel) throw for the combinations they do not model: `dup2` between a guest socket and a host descriptor in either direction, and on Windows duplicating a directory descriptor. Guest sockets share one socket object between the duplicates and the descriptor numbers come from the guest socket counter, not the lowest free number as in FreeBSD `kern_dup`. The errno values for descriptor exhaustion are not verified against the console.
- [Cancellation coverage](ARCHITECTURE.md#thread-cancellation) checks deferred request handling and recovery, not full asynchronous support or console ABI compatibility. Cancellation during file/socket I/O, sleep interruption, cancellation versus exit/detach, condition signal conservation under cancellation, and safe rejection of non-null invalid or reclaimed thread handles remain unverified. No console title or homebrew establishes the cleanup NIDs or buffer layout. Accepted asynchronous mode currently has no diagnostic for its deferred behavior.
- [MIMG `tfe` and `lwe`](../../core/shader/recompiler/RdnaDecoder/src/RdnaImageOpDecoder.cpp) never write the status VGPR after the data. On hardware it is written only when a texel fetch hits an unmapped page of a partially resident texture, or, for `lwe` on sample/gather, when the LOD is below the T# `MIN_LOD_WARN`; texture memory is always resident here, the LOD warning (texture-streaming feedback, like the T# `MIN_LOD_WARN` itself) is never reported, and `lwe` on `image_get_lod` throws.
- [Packed horizontal gathers](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) (`image_gather4h_pck`, MIMG opcode 98): the four window texels are read raw and concatenated into a little-endian bitstream, with DMASK bit i writing stream word i to the next destination register; measured on RDNA2 (gfx1031) at every edge: the anchor is floor(x-0.5) with elements at -1, 0, +1 and +2 clamped horizontally into the width, the row is floor(y), negative rows clamp to row 0, rows at or past the height return zeros, sampler addressing modes do not affect the window, and formats wider than 32 bits per texel write zeros in every register. The emitter fetches each window texel at LOD 0 and packs its bits like `image_load_pck`, so the sampler's filter, addressing and LOD state are not used; formats whose bits are not recoverable from the view and D16 data throw. The anchor and row multiply the normalized coordinate by the image size without contraction. The stream rule was measured for R8_UNORM, RG8_UNORM and RGBA8; only RGBA8 has an execution test. `image_gather8h_pck` (opcode 99) never returns texel data on this hardware - across dmasks 0x1 to 0xFF including the upper nibble, RGBA8, RGBA16 and RGBA32 formats, 1D, 2D and 3D descriptors and normalized and unnormalized sampling it only zero-fills popcount(dmask) registers (sentinel-prefilled destinations) - so it decodes but throws at translation; its eight-texel semantics remain unmeasured.
- [waitpid](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) returns -1 with `ECHILD` (`EINVAL` for unknown options): nothing here creates a child process; child processes would need a real `waitpid`.
- [execvp](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Process.cpp) (libkernel) throws: replacing the process image is not supported. PPSA85300 imports it and fails to load without it; its `fork` and `vfork` fail with `ENOSYS` ([libc_missing.c](https://github.com/ZiZc3/VLC/blob/f3253020a64368153233ede29183014c0b6c8979/ps5/compat/libc_missing.c#L290-L292)), so the child process that would call it is never created.
- [fork](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel_sys/Export.cpp) (libkernel_sys) throws: creating a child process is not supported. PPSA19111 imports it and fails to load without it. Its engine calls `fork` in `Posix_Daemonize`, only with `-daemonize`, and in `Platform_ShellExecute` before `execvp("xdg-open")`, which the main menu's Previews button reaches, so pressing Previews ends in this throw. [setsid](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel_sys/Export.cpp) fails with `EPERM`, as FreeBSD's does for a process group leader, since the guest is taken to lead its own process group (see `kill`). [umask](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel_sys/Export.cpp) keeps the guest's mask (initially 022, masked to 07777) and returns the previous one, but files are still created with the host process's mask
- [MIMG BY2/BY4 loads](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) (`image_load[_mip]_by2/by4`, opcodes 0x42, 0x43, 0x4a and 0x4b) were measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) with the [hardware oracle](HW_ORACLE.md) for 2D images with identity swizzle. They read N texels (BY2: 2, BY4: 4) from `x & ~(N - 1)` on row y, texel by texel and component by component within each texel, with each texel converted like `image_load`. Every register is zero when the mip level is past the last level, `x + N - 1` is outside the level's width (unaligned x, unsigned), or y is outside its height. Data only comes back when the N texels fit in one dword: R8 and R16 (DMASK 0x3) or RG8 (0xf) for BY2, and R8 (0xf) for BY4. With 32-bit texels (R32, RG32, RG16, RGBA8) the hardware returns zeros. Other formats, masks, dimensions, swizzles, D16/A16 and indirect descriptors throw. The stores (0x52, 0x53, 0x5a and 0x5b) decode but throw at translation. Measured on hardware, they write from the unaligned x up to the end of its dword, which is not implemented.
- [2D stores to 1D storage images](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp): an `image_store` with DIM 2D through a 1D T# writes texel x and ignores y, as measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) for y from 0 to 4 and 0xffffffff, single- and four-component formats and a 1x1 8_8_8_8_UNORM T#: the T# type decides the addressing, DIM only how many address VGPRs are read. Storage loads, atomics and `image_store_mip` through such a pair still throw
- [MIMG PCK2/PCK4 loads](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) (`image_load[_mip]_pck2/pck4`, opcodes 0x70, 0x71, 0x73 and 0x74) were measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) with the [hardware oracle](HW_ORACLE.md) for 2D images with identity swizzle. With data mask 0x1, they write one dword: the raw bits of the N texels (PCK2: 2, PCK4: 4) from `x & ~(N - 1)` on row y, concatenated (element k at bit `k * texel bits`) and zero-extended. The dword is zero when the mip level is past the last level, `x + N - 1` is outside the level's width (unaligned x, unsigned), or y is outside its height. Data only comes back when N texels fit in 32 bits: R8, R16 or RG8 for PCK2, R8 for PCK4. Wider texels (R32, RG16, RGBA8) and masks other than 0x1 write zeros on hardware. Those masks throw at decode, and formats outside UNORM/UINT/SINT R8, R16 and RG8 throw. #1314's earlier measurement saw only zeros because it used masks 0x3 and 0xf. The stores (0x76, 0x77, 0x79 and 0x7a) decode but throw at translation. They are not measured, and opcode 0x76 on 32_32_32_32_FLOAT hung the GPU (#1314).
- [3D image accesses bound to a 2D or 2D-array T#](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp) follow CouchRiv's [hardware oracle](HW_ORACLE.md) measurement on RDNA2 (gfx1036, #2023) and adriadam10's (gfx1035, #2140), not the PS5's gfx1013. On a 2D T#, `image_sample_lz` ignores r and reads the (u,v) texel, `image_load` and `image_load_mip` read the (x,y) texel when z is 0 and return zero otherwise, and `image_get_resinfo` returns (width, height, 1, levels). On a 2D-array T#, these accesses behave like dim 2D-array: r or z is the layer, and `image_get_resinfo` returns the layer count as its third component. Every other access of such an image, including samples with an LOD, bias, derivatives, offset or comparison, gathers, `image_get_lod` and stores, keeps throwing because it is not measured.
- [2D accesses through a cube T#](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp) (#4139) read the T#'s `BASE_ARRAY` face as a cube access with face coordinate 0 does, measured on RDNA1 (gfx1010, RX 5700 XT) with the [hardware oracle](../HW_ORACLE.md) template run through the Windows driver's comgr and HIP runtime on 8x8 and 32x32 32_FLOAT cubes (`DEPTH` 5 and 11, `BASE_ARRAY` 0 and 6): `image_sample_lz`, plain `image_sample` (one level, and six levels in 2x2 compute quads) and `image_sample_l` (the third address VGPR is the LOD, integer and fractional) with DIM 2D equal DIM CUBE with face 0 on every row, with point and bilinear filters, wrap, clamp and mirror addressing and `DISABLE_CUBE_WRAP` 0 and 1, and the VGPR after the two coordinates is not read; `image_load` with DIM 2D reads texel (x, y) of the `BASE_ARRAY` face. So the face coordinates are cube coordinates in [1, 2]: the texel is `floor((u - 1) * width)`. They are translated through the cube mode DIM CUBE accesses use, which samples the face of the 2D-array view at (u - 1, v - 1) with the S# addressing. That matches the hardware inside the face, except within half a texel of its edges with a bilinear filter and `DISABLE_CUBE_WRAP` 0, where the hardware blends texels of the neighbouring faces (also measured on RDNA2, gfx1035, 8x8 cubes with `BASE_ARRAY` 0 and 6). Outside it the hardware clamps a coordinate below 1 to the first texel under every filter, wrap mode and `DISABLE_CUBE_WRAP` setting measured, clamps a coordinate of 2 or more to the last texel when `DISABLE_CUBE_WRAP` is 0 (point filter) and applies the S# addressing to it when it is 1; the translation does none of this, as for DIM CUBE. Only `image_sample_lz`, plain `image_sample`, `image_sample_l` and mip-less `image_load` take this mode; other DIM 2D accesses through a cube T# (gathers, bias, comparison, offsets, derivatives, storage, mip loads) still throw.
- [2D biased sampling through a one-slice, single-level 3D T#](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp) supplies a zero third coordinate and retains the bias operation, with normalized point minification and magnification and no linear mip filtering. Measured on RDNA2 (AMD integrated gfx1036, driver 32.0.21036.18, Windows 11) by running an OpenCL-loaded native kernel with `image_sample_b` DIM 2D against 16x16x1 R32_UINT and RGBA8_UNORM 3D images: biases -16, -0.5, 0, 0.5 and 16 read the (u,v) texel in slice zero, including wrapped coordinates, point min/max reduction and the title's sampler with Z border addressing. Wave32, IEEE off, DX10 clamp on, denormals preserved, round to nearest even, FP16 overflow off; wave64 and the PS5's gfx1013 are not measured. Pixel-stage, direct, non-depth, unpacked samples whose only use is the plain 32-bit bias form are accepted; extra slices, any nonzero mip range, linear or anisotropic filtering and unnormalized coordinates throw. Vulkan readback is checked on NVIDIA RTX 5070 and AMD integrated graphics for positive and negative bias and point weighted-average/min/max reduction.
- [FORCE_DEGAMMA on RGBA8_UINT](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp) materializes a direct image sampled only by plain biased pixel accesses as RGBA8_SRGB when every paired sampler forces degamma. Measured on AMD integrated RDNA2 gfx1036 (driver 32.0.21036.18, Windows 11, wave32, IEEE off, DX10 clamp on, denormals preserved, round to nearest even, FP16 overflow off): a native `image_sample_b` DIM 2D on a 16x16x1 RGBA8_UINT 3D T# returns integer channels without the bit, but the same floating-point decoded values as RGBA8_UNORM with it, including point min/max reduction and the title's exact sampler. The metadata and image view must both become floating-point sRGB; ignoring the sampler bit or retaining a UINT heap is incorrect. The accepted case requires one slice, mip zero, normalized point sampling and uniform FORCE_DEGAMMA across its samplers. Other image accesses, mixed sampler states, indirect descriptors and other integer formats remain refused. Wave64, other filtering modes and the PS5's gfx1013 are not measured.
- [Unnormalized S# coordinates](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestSamplerResource.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - `FORCE_UNNORMALIZED` uses a Vulkan `unnormalizedCoordinates` sampler, so the S# LOD range, LOD bias and mip filter are not applied, which is exact only on a one-level view.
- [Unnormalized S#s](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/DescriptorBindingBuilder.cpp) are accepted only when direct and used only by explicit-LOD `image_sample` without comparison, derivatives or `_a`, on a single-level, single-layer 1D or 2D view of mip 0, even when the image is also sampled through a normalized S#.
- An [unnormalized S#](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestSamplerResource.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) maps clamp mode 0 (repeat) on X and Y to clamp-to-edge as required by Vulkan unnormalized coordinates, and assigns the magnification filter when minification and magnification filters differ (since unnormalized coordinates sample at LOD 0). Anisotropy, TRUNC_COORD, MC_COORD_TRUNC and other clamp modes still throw. Secondary LOD bias (LOD_BIAS_SEC, bits [19:14]) is accepted and ignored, matching RDNA2 hardware measurements across explicit and implicit samples. A title (#4736) uses secondary LOD bias and unnormalized coordinates with clamp mode 0 and differing min/mag filters.
- [Unnormalized texel offsets](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) are added to the f32 coordinates: exact on RDNA2 (gfx1035), which applies the offset before the clamp ([measured by adriadam10](https://github.com/boykopovar/AnyPS5/pull/822#issuecomment-6033192725)).
- [Image atomics](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp) on 32_SINT and 32_FLOAT surfaces act on the raw dword through an R32_UINT storage view; their float results are assumed to match those measured on 32_UINT surfaces. Other formats throw.
- [`s_memtime`](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvModuleEmitter.cpp) on RADV with a GFX10.3 to GFX11.7 GPU (RDNA 2, RDNA 3, RDNA 3.5) reads the Vulkan device clock instead of the subgroup clock, which [ACO](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/main/src/amd/compiler/instruction_selection/aco_select_nir_intrinsics.cpp) reads from the 20-bit `SHADER_CYCLES` register there and which wraps every 2^20 cycles. The device clock is a monotonic 64-bit counter, but it counts the 100 MHz reference clock (the same one as `s_memrealtime`), not shader cycles, so `s_memtime` deltas are about 20 times smaller than on hardware. The families are matched by the name RADV reports ([`NarrowSubgroupClock`](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/SubgroupClock.cpp)); a family added to Mesa later reads the subgroup clock until it is listed. AMD's proprietary Windows driver also exposes a 20-bit subgroup clock on device 0x1114 (Radeon 860M, measured in #1615), device 0x744c (Radeon RX 7900 XT, measured in #1632), and device 0x747e (Radeon RX 7800 XT, measured in #1632); other AMD proprietary devices remain unverified.
- [Background link-time-optimized relink of library pipelines](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/include/PipelineLinkOptimization.hpp) (libSceAgcDriver) is skipped on every GPU under NVIDIA's proprietary Vulkan driver (`VK_DRIVER_ID_NVIDIA_PROPRIETARY`), so those pipelines keep their fast-linked form. The optimized link loses the device when bound on an RTX 3080 (Ampere, #2866) and an RTX 4060 (Ada, #3434); an RTX 5060 and an RTX 5080 (Blackwell) on the same driver do not, so the scope is wider than the evidence. No standard Vulkan query tells the architecture apart, and the driver version that fixes it is unknown. `APS5_NO_PIPELINE_LTO` disables the relink on every driver.
- [`s_memtime` and `s_memrealtime`](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvModuleEmitter.cpp) on llvmpipe read only the low 32 bits of the host's nanosecond clock at either scope: Mesa's [`emit_clock`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/main/src/gallium/auxiliary/gallivm/lp_bld_nir_soa.c) builds the high dword with a left shift by 32, so it is always 0 and both counters wrap every 2^32 ns (about 4.3 s). The [shader clock test](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/tests/execution/ShaderClock.cpp) compares them modulo 2^32 there
- [`ds_wrxchg2_rtn_b32`, `ds_wrxchg2st64_rtn_b32`, `ds_wrxchg2_rtn_b64` and `ds_wrxchg2st64_rtn_b64`](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/MemoryInstructions/SharedMemoryInstructions.cpp) are translated as two separate 32-bit or 64-bit atomic swaps per invocation. RDNA2 hardware (measured on gfx1035) performs every lane's first exchange before any lane's second one, so when lanes of the same wave or of different waves overlap addresses, the result can differ from hardware. `offset0 == offset1` throws, although on hardware it is two exchanges in order.
- [`ds_condxchg32_rtn_b64`](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/MemoryInstructions/SharedMemoryInstructions.cpp) uses two 32-bit atomic conditional exchanges. RDNA2 (gfx1035) measurements confirmed aligned return values, conditional bit clearing and 16-bit offsets; out-of-range accesses return zero without modifying LDS. Misaligned accesses fault the wave on gfx1035; the recompiler does not model this fault. Radeon 780M (gfx1103, RDNA3) measurements confirmed aligned return values, conditional bit clearing, EXEC masking and operand overlap in wave32 and wave64; contended cases passed per-dword conservation checks. Contended lane ordering and gfx1013 remain unmeasured; pair-wide indivisibility is not assumed, and concurrent 64-bit LDS atomics use a separate lock. GDS throws. VGPR pairs extending beyond v255 throw; allocated-VGPR EXEC nullification is not emulated.
- [64-bit image atomics](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/MemoryInstructions/ImageInstructions.cpp) (`image_atomic_*` with a two-dword data mask) need `VK_EXT_shader_image_atomic_int64` and act on 32_32 UINT, SINT and FLOAT surfaces through an R64_UINT storage view. 64-bit `image_atomic_inc`, `image_atomic_dec` and the float atomics throw, as does any other access in the same shader to an image it updates with 64-bit atomics.
- [8_SRGB and 8_8_SRGB loads](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) on a device that cannot sample R8_SRGB or R8G8_SRGB return the sRGB EOTF rounded to nearest even at bf16 precision, as adriadam10 measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) in #866; the PS5's gfx1013 is not measured.
- [8_SRGB and 8_8_SRGB textures](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/TextureFormat.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) that the device samples as R8_SRGB or R8G8_SRGB use its own decode, which need not match gfx1035: NVIDIA's R8_SRGB is one bf16 step off in 29 of 256 codes.
- [Samples and gathers](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) of 8_SRGB and 8_8_SRGB textures throw on a device that cannot sample R8_SRGB or R8G8_SRGB.
- [Bindless image table](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp) entries of 8_SRGB and 8_8_SRGB textures read zeros, like converted formats, on a device that cannot sample R8_SRGB or R8G8_SRGB.
- [Shader MODE register access](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/RoundingMode.cpp) starts from the rounding fields of `GuestContext::floatMode` and follows immediate rounding writes (`s_round_mode`, `s_setreg_imm32_b32` on MODE bits 0-3) through the control flow graph; `s_getreg_b32` of those bits returns the tracked value. Only `v_cvt_f32_i32`, `v_cvt_f32_u32`, `v_ldexp_f32`, the f16 conversions and `v_ldexp_f16` (with f16 denormals kept) and the instructions that already read `FLOAT_MODE` follow a rounding mode set at run time; any other float instruction that runs while a directed rounding mode differs from the initial one throws at that instruction. Rounding bits written from an SGPR (`s_setreg_b32`) or that differ between the paths reaching an instruction throw where they are read or used. A shader without `FLOAT_MODE` metadata throws on any rounding change. Non-rounding register reads still throw; `s_setreg_b32` writes to other hardware registers are still ignored. Tests follow the public RDNA2/LLVM contract, not console measurements.
- A [flat SRT read](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/SrtWalker/SrtDescriptorEvaluation.cpp) through a null or unmapped pointer loaded from memory raises a [BDA fault](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/BdaResources.cpp) only if the program executes it, then stops or reads zero like a BDA read.
- Repeated `s_load`s of one [flat SRT](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/SrtWalker/SrtDescriptorEvaluation.cpp) dword through a null or unmapped pointer report one of their pcs, and an `s_load` whose inaccessible dwords are all unused, or only address repeated loads, reports no fault of its own.
- A value read through a null or unmapped pointer still throws at record time if a [branch condition, uniform fill or table base](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/SrtWalker/SrtDescriptorEvaluation.cpp) needs it, or if the pointer comes from user data and is not null, even if the console skips the read.
- A [V# or flat SRT word](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/SrtWalker/SrtEvaluator.cpp) read through a null pointer taken from user data is zero and counts as a [poisoned SRT read](../../core/shader/recompiler/Recompiler.cpp), so a dispatch that reads it is not cached. The console faults if the program executes the read; PPSA28416 branches around it with `s_cbranch_execz`. The fault is not modeled.
- A [V#, T# or S#](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/SrtWalker/SrtDescriptorEvaluation.cpp) loaded through a null or unmapped pointer read from memory is zero when the work is recorded, and the capture throws if no flat SRT slot records that read.
- [2D accesses through a 1D T#](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp) read the texel the 1D addressing selects and ignore the second coordinate, as simonbbby measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) in #2237 for `image_sample_lz` and mip-less `image_load` with point filtering and wrap, clamp and border addressing; `image_sample_l` was measured the same way on gfx1036 against `image_sample_l` with DIM 1D through the same T#, for u inside, on and past the edges, v of 0, 0.5, 1, -0.25 and 3, LOD -1 to 6 on a 5-level R32_FLOAT texture, point, linear and linear-mip filtering, wrap, clamp and border addressing and HEIGHT or DEPTH 3: all 4725 rows matched. The PS5's gfx1013 is not measured. A plain `image_sample` (implicit LOD) through such a pair takes its LOD from the derivatives of the first coordinate alone, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) with the [hardware oracle](../HW_ORACLE.md) on the quads of a compute kernel (wave32 and wave64, IEEE off, DX10 clamp on, denormals preserved, round to nearest even): on a 16-texel, 5-mip 32_UINT T# with point filters it picks the same mip and texel as DIM 1D for first-coordinate steps of 0, 1, 2, 4 and 8 texels per lane along x or along y, whatever the second coordinate does (normalised steps up to 16 per lane), while the same rows on a 16x16 2D T# move the mip with the second coordinate. Outside the pixel stage a plain `image_sample` is emitted with LOD 0 for every image type, so this pair reads mip 0 there, where the kernel measurement shows the quad's derivatives being used. `image_sample_c_lz` and plain `image_sample_c` through such a pair compare against the texel the DIM 1D comparison with the same reference and first coordinate reads, measured on RDNA1 (gfx1010, RX 5700 XT) with the [hardware oracle](../HW_ORACLE.md) template run through the Windows driver's comgr and HIP runtime (wave32 and wave64, IEEE off, DX10 clamp on, denormals preserved, round to nearest even): 8-texel 32_FLOAT and 16_UNORM T#s, also with HEIGHT 3, point and bilinear filters, wrap, clamp and opaque-white border addressing, less and gequal functions, second coordinates 0, 0.5, 1, 1.5, -0.25 and 3; on a 64-texel, 7-mip 32_FLOAT T# `image_sample_c` picks its LOD from the first coordinate's steps alone (1, 4 and 16 texels per lane give mips 0, 2 and 4; second-coordinate steps up to 64 texels leave it unchanged). Only comparisons the runtime does natively (32_FLOAT, 16_UNORM) reach the 1D view; comparisons the runtime emulates for other color formats still throw through such a pair. 2D-array `image_sample_l` and `image_sample_lz` through a 1D T# read as DIM 1D too, ignoring v and the slice: measured on gfx1036 with the hardware oracle (template assembled by the AMD Windows driver's `amd_comgr_2.dll` and launched through its `amdhip64_6.dll`) against the same sample with DIM 1D on a 16-texel 8_8_8_8 UNORM T# with 1 or 5 levels, HEIGHT or DEPTH 3, for u inside, on and past the edges, v of 0, 0.5, 1, -0.25 and 3, slices 0, 1, 2.7, -1 and 5, LOD -1 to 6, and the five filter and address cases above: all 48000 rows matched. A 2D-array `image_load` through a 1D T# is not the same: on gfx1036 it returns the DIM 1D texel only at slice 0, and zeros at slices 1, 2, 5 and -1 even with DEPTH 3, so it, the plain `image_sample` and the other 2D-array accesses still throw. Other 2D accesses through a 1D T# (storage, depth, packed, BY loads, mips other than explicit LOD, explicit LOD with comparison, bias, offsets, derivatives) still throw.
- [3D image accesses bound to a 1D T#](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp) follow a [hardware oracle](../HW_ORACLE.md) measurement on RDNA1 (RX 5700 XT, gfx1010; wave32 and wave64, IEEE off, DX10 clamp on, denormals preserved, round to nearest even), not the PS5's gfx1013, on 8x1 R8G8B8A8_UNORM linear 1D T#s with HEIGHT and DEPTH 0 or 3 and point filters. `image_sample_lz` reads the texel u selects for v and r in {0, 0.5, 1, 1.5, -0.25, 3}, with wrap, clamp and border addressing, and v and r never produce the border; u wraps, clamps and borders as with DIM 1D. Mip-less `image_load` reads the x texel for any y in {0, 1, 2, 3, -1} when z is 0, returns zero for z in {1, 2, 3, -1}, whatever DEPTH says, and returns zero for x past the width. A plain `image_sample` (implicit LOD) on the quads of a compute kernel takes its texel and LOD from u alone: on a 64-texel, 7-mip 1D T# steps of 1, 4 and 16 texels per lane pick mips 0, 2 and 4, as DIM 1D `image_sample_l` does, and steps in v or r alone stay at mip 0. Outside the pixel stage a plain `image_sample` is emitted with LOD 0, as for 2D accesses through a 1D T#. Every other 3D access of such an image (`image_load` with mips, storage, explicit LOD, bias, derivatives, offsets, comparison, gathers, queries) keeps throwing. SONIC SUPERSTARS (PPSA06888) samples a 1x1 1D T# this way with plain `image_sample` and `image_load`.
- [Emulated comparison of a 2D-array access through a 2D T#](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp) samples the 2D image and ignores the slice, as the native modes of a 2D-array access through a 2D T# already do. This follows the T# type deciding the addressing; it is not measured on hardware for comparison samples
- [Flat aperture bases](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvMemoryInstructions.cpp) (core/shader/recompiler) - the layout follows the gfx9+ one in the AMD KFD driver's `kfd_flat_memory.c` (`MAKE_LDS_APP_BASE_V9`, `MAKE_LDS_APP_LIMIT`): each aperture is a 4 GB window whose base is the high dword, so a flat address is routed by its whole high dword, as measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035: `src_shared_base` 0x00010000_00000000, `src_shared_limit` 0x00010000_ffffffff). The PS5's base values, high dwords 0x80000000 (shared, `SharedApertureHigh`) and 0x70000000 (private, `PrivateApertureHigh`), are inferred from GTA V's traversal addresses, not measured on a console or read from SH_MEM_BASES. The scalar aperture sources read the same two values
- [Flat accesses outside the aperture routing](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvMemoryInstructions.cpp) (core/shader/recompiler) - only compute stages route flat accesses, so graphics-stage flat accesses to the shared or private aperture still go to BDA. In compute, a 64-bit flat atomic whose address is in the shared aperture takes the workgroup lock (`ldsLock`) for its two-dword update, and every such atomic requests that lock, so a shader with one pays for it even on global addresses (the path is exercised by `agc_driver_global_atomics_float`). Inferred, not measured on LDS or scratch: an aperture access that is not inside one dword is done byte by byte, an unaligned aperture atomic records the BDA unaligned fault, and LDS or scratch accesses past their end are dropped or read zero.
- [GFX10 scratch address operands](../../core/shader/recompiler/RdnaDecoder/src/RdnaMemoryOpDecoder.cpp) (shader recompiler) use the SV/SS/ST scratch operand widths described by LLVM FLATInstructions.td at 3eb867f9b63ae2881bbe3c25d3c12842f2bdc55e (operand pseudos L557-L630, GFX10 encoding L2973-L3038), also in `MemoryResourceHelpers.cpp`. Decoding and IR tests cover the one-dword VGPR offset; PS5 hardware address-result validation remains outstanding. Unsupported scalar registers still throw.
- On a host subgroup narrower than 32 lanes, [`threadBit`](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/TranslationContext/ExecMask.cpp) reads an EXEC written as a value (`s_mov_b32`/`s_mov_b64`) at bit (subgroup invocation id) of EXEC_LO, so lanes at or above the subgroup size read a lower lane's bit.
- [EXEC read as a value](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/TranslationContext/ExecMask.cpp) (`s_mov_b64 sN, exec`, the `s_*_saveexec` copy, ALU sources) holds only lanes with a host invocation; the console's also holds the wave's other lanes, e.g. 32-63 of a wave64 on 32 host lanes.
- [`v_readlane_b32`](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAlu/SpirvAluEmitterMath.cpp) and fetch-inactive `v_permlane16_b32`/`v_permlanex16_b32` read undefined data from a lane that the host subgroup does not hold or whose host invocation is inactive; the console reads the lane's VGPR.
- Without fetch-inactive, [`v_permlane16_b32` and `v_permlanex16_b32`](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAlu/SpirvAluEmitterMath.cpp) take a source lane that the host subgroup does not hold as inactive and keep the old value (0 with bound_ctrl); the console reads that lane's VGPR.
- On a host subgroup narrower than the wave, [`v_readlane_b32`](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAlu/SpirvAluEmitterMath.cpp) of a constant lane outside it builds only as lane 63 of a half-wave scan (DPP row_shr 1/2/4/8, `v_permlanex16_b32`, EXEC -1); other such reads throw.
- [NGG mesh entry](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/InstructionTranslator.cpp) uses the guest wave size (32 or 64) for wave indices, per-wave vertex and primitive counts, and the workgroup wave count in `s3`. The four-bit wave fields follow Mesa's [get_wave_id_in_tg, get_tgsize and get_thread_id_in_tg](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromiumos/third_party/mesa/+/refs/heads/stabilize-13982.70.B-chromeos-amd/src/gallium/drivers/radeonsi/gfx10_shader_ngg.c); partial waves and groups exceeding 15 waves throw. Indexed and non-indexed wave32 mesh rendering with 32, 64 and 128 threads was checked by pixel readback on Windows with an NVIDIA RTX 4070 Laptop GPU. The guest register values have not been compared with console hardware.
- [Shader recompilation](../../core/shader/recompiler/Recompiler.cpp) currently occurs right before it was transferred to Vulkan with caching, but should be moved to the [relinker](../../core/relinker/main.cpp) stage. For this purpose, [shader/recompiler](../../core/shader/recompiler) was written completely independently from [libs/prx](../../core/libs/prx).
- [v_interp_p1ll_f16, v_interp_p1lv_f16 and v_interp_p2_f16](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/AttributeInstructions.cpp) read the 16-bit halves of each vertex's parameter through `VK_KHR_fragment_shader_barycentric` and throw without it, as fixed-function interpolation cannot split a 32-bit attribute. P10 and P20 are the vertex differences rounded to f16 to nearest even, which is not confirmed on hardware. v_interp_p1ll_f16 and v_interp_p1lv_f16 round the exact `P10 * I + P0` once to f32 and v_interp_p2_f16 rounds the exact `P20 * J + S2` once to f16, as measured on RDNA2 (#697); the exact sums are computed in f64, so these instructions need the Float64 capability. They follow the shader's float mode, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) in all eight combinations of `IEEE_MODE` and kept/flushed f32 and f16 denormals over 768 special and 1,024 random rows. With IEEE=1 a NaN operand is returned quieted; with IEEE=0 it is returned unquieted, except that v_interp_p2_f16's f16 result is always quiet. Flushed f32 denormals flush I/J, v_interp_p2_f16's f32 S2 and the f32 result; flushed f16 denormals flush P0, P10/P20, v_interp_p1lv_f16's f16 S2 and the f16 result. Denormal modes 1 and 2 (flush only inputs or only outputs) and rounding other than to nearest even are not measured and throw. An unknown mode follows IEEE=0 with denormals kept. Lavapipe has no barycentrics, so the draw test is skipped in CI; `agc_driver_interpolation_modes` checks the arithmetic in every mode against the hardware rows on any device with Float64.
- Without `VK_KHR_fragment_shader_barycentric`, a pixel shader whose I/J VGPRs [only feed `v_interp_p1_f32`/`v_interp_p2_f32` of their own pair](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/HostInterpolationChecker.cpp) reads the attribute Vulkan interpolated. Any other read of them or of a raw vertex parameter takes the weights and the three vertex values from a [generated geometry stage](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/BarycentricGeometryShader.cpp), which needs `geometryShader` and a vertex shader before it: tessellation, mesh and rect-list pipelines, primitives other than triangles, more than 32 fragment input locations and interfaces beyond the device's geometry limits throw.
- [v_fma_f64 and v_div_fmas_f64](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAlu/SpirvAluEmitterFloat64.cpp) with a subnormal operand or result round once, as IEEE 754 fusedMultiplyAdd does with f64 denormals kept, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1, `v_div_fmas_f64` with VCC clear and set. The f16/f64 denormal field of `FLOAT_MODE` (bits 7:6) is not read: when it flushes, the hardware flushes the operands and the result of `v_fma_f64` (tininess before rounding) and only the result of `v_div_fmas_f64`, while these keep them. Shaders with f64 instructions declare `DenormPreserve` for 64-bit floats only when `shaderDenormPreserveFloat64` is true and Vulkan reports `denormBehaviorIndependence` as `ALL`; other independence modes are not handled by the current backend because they require coupled execution modes. See [VkShaderFloatControlsIndependence](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VkShaderFloatControlsIndependence.html). Elsewhere (including lavapipe), the driver may flush f64 subnormals, and `agc_driver_f64_fma_subnormals` is skipped.
- [SINT storage images](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) of 8- and 16-bit components saturate each stored 32-bit value to the component range, as adriadam10 measured for image stores on RDNA2 (gfx1035) in #944 (448 rows match); the PS5's gfx1013 is not measured.
- [`StoreTexel`](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) writes channel 1 of a two-channel format from the data slot whose DST_SEL is Y, for SINT and UINT alike. With DST_SEL X,Y,Z,W, gfx1035 writes it from the W slot, as adriadam10 measured in #944.
- [Formatted buffer stores](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvRuntimeBuffer.cpp) (`buffer_store_format_*`, D16 included) apply the V# DST_SEL only in the cases [`SettledFormattedStoreSwizzle`](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvBufferFormat.cpp) and `IsSettledFormattedStoreDstSel` accept: the identity on the format's components, where each component comes from its own data slot as on `main` and the selectors of components the format lacks are ignored; Z,Y,X,W on 8_8_8_8_UNORM and 16_16_16_16_UINT; and 0,0,0,X on 8_UNORM and 8_UINT. There each format component takes the data slot whose selector names it, a slot the instruction does not provide counts as zero, and a slot that selects 0 writes nothing. Any other DST_SEL [throws](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/DescriptorBindingBuilder.cpp), and a GPU-selected V# with one records an invalid-descriptor fault and writes nothing. simonbbby measured on RDNA2 gfx1036 (Ryzen 9 9950X iGPU) with the project's HSA hardware oracle, in wave32 and wave64, that Z,Y,X,W on 8_8_8_8_UNORM (`_xyzw` and `_d16_xyzw`) and the identity and Z,Y,X,W on 16_16_16_16_UINT store this way, and the 8_UINT 0,0,0,X words of his full UINT probe (`_xyzw`, then `_x` to the next byte) equal the predicted ones ([#5172 comment](https://github.com/boykopovar/AnyPS5/pull/5172#issuecomment-6147814879)). Other permutations do not follow the rule: in his sweep of all 24 permutations of X,Y,Z,W on 16_16_16_16_UINT, storing (0x1234, 0x5678, 0x9abc, 0xdef0) and reading it back, 12 match the rule and 12 do not ([#5172 comment](https://github.com/boykopovar/AnyPS5/pull/5172#issuecomment-6148978796)). Being its own inverse is not enough: Y,X,Z,W wrote the raw dwords `56781234 9abcdef0` and X,W,Z,Y wrote `56789abc def01234`, where the rule gives `12345678 def09abc` and `def01234 56789abc`. The rotation Y,Z,X,W wrote `def01234 9abc5678`, memory (x, w, y, z), and `buffer_load_format_xyzw` through it read (0xdef0, 0x5678, 0x1234, 0x9abc), the load mapping applied to those bytes. 0,0,0,X on 8_UNORM is the V# RADV builds for `dEQP-VK.image.store.without_format.buffer.a8_unorm`, which Mesa's CI runs on Navi 21 with no failure listed (sources in #5172). That case stores the components x, 63 - x, x and 63 - x, scaled alike, at texel x of its 64-texel buffer, so it shows only that the component comes from the Y or the W slot; the 8_UINT measurement shows W.
