The `b8g8r8a8_*` and `a2r10g10b10_*_pack32` cases of the same group store equal X and Z components at every texel, so they pass whether or not the store swaps X and Z and settle nothing about Z,Y,X,W. No rule that explains these results is known, so other permutations and formats stay refused, Z,Y,X,W on the other 8_8_8_8 formats and on the 2_10_10_10 formats included. Narrower stores (`_x`, `_xy`, `_xyz`) through Z,Y,X,W and through 0,0,0,X on 8_UNORM, and D16 stores through Z,Y,X,W on 16_16_16_16_UINT, are not measured. For image stores, #944 measured gfx1035 writing channel 1 of a two-channel format from the W slot with X,Y,Z,W (entry above); that is not measured for buffer stores. The PS5's gfx1013 is not measured.
- [WAIT_REG_MEM](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Synchronization/WaitMemory.cpp) reports a diagnostic after `APS5_GPU_WAIT_TIMEOUT_MS` without treating the timeout as completion. An unsatisfied wait keeps polling and checking driver failures; a missing producer can therefore leave its queue waiting indefinitely. Existing recorded-label shortcuts remain in use.
- [COND_EXEC](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Packets/PacketExecution.cpp) reads its condition after the earlier packets of its queue complete. Without PFP_SYNC_ME the console's graphics PFP reads it ahead of the ME, so a title that races the read sees a later value here.
- A [flip inside a COND_EXEC range](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Packets/CommandValidation.cpp) throws: the flip is reserved with the video output when the buffer is submitted, and how the console withdraws a skipped one is unknown.
- A [COND_EXEC range](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Queues/Submission.cpp) must hold whole packets and end inside its command buffer. A chained INDIRECT_BUFFER or a REWIND inside a range throws, as what the CP does with the skipped dwords then is unknown.
- A [COND_INDIRECT_BUFFER](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Queues/Submission.cpp) (`sceAgcCbBranch`) runs only with the always-pass compare function; a comparison or a predicated packet throws. Modes 1 and 2 run the first buffer, assumed to be the if-then and if-then-else modes of AMD's PM4 packet. [sceAgcCbBranch](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/Cb/src/Branch.cpp) does not check the compare address of an always-pass branch or the address of an empty buffer, as PPSA14632 passes an uninitialised compare address and a null else buffer of 0 dwords.
- A [label or store](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp) (`RELEASE_MEM`, `WRITE_DATA`, `DMA_DATA`) into guest memory the device has no host import of waits for all recorded work and is then written by the CPU, so the queue worker stalls once per such label. The console's CP stores it in stream order without that stall.
- [WRITE_DATA with DST_SEL 3](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Pm4.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) writes guest memory like DST_SEL 5. AMD's GFX10 header names 3 the GDS, but Killzone: Liberation (PPSA11142) writes its frame fence with `sceAgcDcbWriteData(dst 6, cache policy 3, write confirm)`, which the SPRX-confirmed encoding ([OpenAGC](https://github.com/OpenAGC/OpenAGC/blob/main/src/cb_builders.c)) turns into DST_SEL 3 with a 40-bit label address in the title's own memory that `RELEASE_MEM` and `WAIT_REG_MEM` then use; the console must write that label or the title would wait on it forever. Whether the console routes it through the GDS or a cache is unknown
- [SET_*_REG_INDIRECT](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Queues/Submission.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) reads its register list when the command buffer is submitted, with the packets. The console's graphics PFP reads it when it reaches the packet, so a list written after the submission (by the CPU, or by GPU work ahead of the packet behind PFP_SYNC_ME) is read here before that write.
- The [shader float mode](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Shaders/ShaderRegistry.cpp) (`FLOAT_MODE`, `DX10_CLAMP`, `IEEE_MODE` and `FP16_OVFL` of `COMPUTE_PGM_RSRC1` and `SPI_SHADER_PGM_RSRC1_PS/GS/HS`, bit positions from Mesa's `gfx10.json` and `gfx103.json`) is decoded into `GuestContext::floatMode` and is part of the shader cache keys and the serialized request (#981). `IEEE_MODE` is read by `v_min_f32`, `v_max_f32`, `v_min3_f32`, `v_max3_f32` and `v_med3_f32`, their f16 forms, `v_min_f64` and `v_max_f64`, `IEEE_MODE` and the `FLOAT_MODE` output denormal bits by the output modifier, `DX10_CLAMP` by the clamp modifier, `FP16_OVFL` by every instruction with an f16 result (see its entry), and the whole mode by the 16-bit interpolation instructions (see their entries). With IEEE=1 a signaling NaN operand returns that NaN quieted (the first operand checked first, min3/max3 as two nested pairs, med3 as min3 when any operand is NaN), measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) over every combination of ±0, ±1, ±inf, denormals, quiet and signaling NaNs with f32 denormals kept and flushed. IEEE=0 and unknown modes keep the previous f32 lowering, which matches IEEE=0. The f16 forms choose their NaN results on the bits: a NaN operand returns the other operand unchanged (src0 when both are NaN), and with IEEE=1 a signaling NaN operand returns that NaN quieted, in the same order as f32. `v_min_f64` and `v_max_f64` quiet a signaling NaN operand the same way with IEEE=1; with IEEE=0 they return src0 unchanged when src1 is NaN, otherwise src1 when src0 is NaN. Both were measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1 with f32 denormals kept and flushed and f16/f64 denormals kept; RDNA2 has no f64 min3, max3 or med3. Unknown modes follow IEEE=0. `v_min_f16` and `v_max_f16` flush each denormal input to its signed zero when bit 0 of the f16/f64 denormal field is clear (0 and 2); fields 1 and 3 preserve inputs, even when output flushing is enabled. Measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1, including signed zeros, NaNs and neg/abs modifiers. Unknown modes keep f16 denormals. With f16/f64 denormals flushed the hardware also flushes denormal inputs of f64 min/max, which those lowerings still do not. NaN quieting in f16 arithmetic (the unary instructions and the f32 arithmetic follow it, see below) and `FLOAT_MODE` (f32 denormals flushed before the min/max compare in Astro's Playroom's 0xc0) still follow a single mode, as the entries below record. The mode comes from the registered shader header's `sh_registers`, the same source at registration and at draw/dispatch so the prepared artifact keys match. A title that rewrites RSRC1 at run time, or a raw compute program with no header, compiles with that mode or none.
- [v_ldexp_f16](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/FloatInstructions.cpp) follows the `FLOAT_MODE` f16 denormal field: bit 0 preserves the half input and bit 1 the half result. Otherwise a denormal input is flushed to its signed zero, and a result whose exact scaled value is below the minimum normal (2^-14) is flushed to its signed zero before rounding, so `v_ldexp_f16 0x07ff, -1` gives +0 instead of 0x0400. Measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) with round to nearest, IEEE 0 and 1 and denorm16 0..3, over every half magnitude 0x0000..0x0fff of both signs scaled by 2^-1 and 2^0 (16384 rows per mode): no mismatch. NaN and infinity handling and destination high-half preservation are unchanged. Shaders without a known float mode keep the previous lowering.
- A [WAIT_REG_MEM](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Synchronization/WaitMemory.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) that a compute queue passes on another queue's recorded label store, before the GPU has executed it, is landed before the queue's next dispatch, draw, COND_EXEC or predicated packet reads guest memory: the worker polls until memory satisfies the wait or no unfinished batch writes the label. The graphics queue (queue 0) is exempt, as landing its waits costs PPSA01341 about a quarter of its frame rate; a title that fills memory a graphics command buffer reads after it submits the buffer behind such a wait reads it early there. `APS5_LAND_WAITS_ALL_QUEUES=1` lands the graphics queue's waits too.
- The [dispatches that follow a compute queue's wait](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Dispatch/DispatchAhead.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver), up to the next wait that memory does not satisfy, a packet that writes guest memory other than a label, or a conditional packet, are resolved (memory captured, shader recompiled) when the wait passes, as the CP reads them right after it; the graphics queue is exempt (`APS5_GROUP_CAPTURE_ALL_QUEUES=1` includes it). The device still reads guest memory when the dispatch is issued; when that memory no longer matches the capture, the dispatch is resolved again from current memory.
- [v_mullit_f32](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/FloatInstructions.cpp) follows the hardware measured with the MODE register at IEEE=1 and denormals kept (a signaling NaN src0 is quieted only with IEEE=1, as measured at IEEE=0 too; a positive denormal src2 counts as positive); with f32 denormals flushed it follows the f32 denormals entry below, and denormal modes 1 and 2 throw.
- [v_mad_legacy_f32 and v_mac_legacy_f32](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/FloatInstructions.cpp) (VOP3 0x140, VOP2 0x06) round the product to f32 before the add, give a +0 product for a zero factor, and flush their f32 denormal sources, their product and their result to a zero of the same sign in every f32 denormal mode, an exception to the `FLOAT_MODE` model in [f32DenormalFlushFor](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/VectorInstructions.cpp). Measured on gfx1010 (RX 5700 XT) with `tools/hw-oracle/template.s`, assembled by the AMD Windows driver's `amd_comgr_2.dll` and launched through its `amdhip64_6.dll` instead of `oracle.c` and ROCr, over the 32 rows of the five `FmaLegacy.cpp` and the four `MacLegacy.cpp` encodings: identical in wave32 and wave64 at IEEE 0 and 1 with f32 denormal modes 0 to 3, except that the two signaling NaN rows are quieted only with IEEE=1 (#1281, #1290). A sum of two zeros takes its sign on the bits (+0 unless both are -0), as lavapipe returned -0 for +0 + -0. The PS5's gfx1013 is not measured; LLVM decodes the encoding as `v_mad_legacy_f32` there, as on gfx1010. RDNA2 differs: on gfx1031 (RX 6700 XT, measured by pandather in #1281) the encoding is `v_fma_legacy_f32` and fused, with the same zero rule and denormal-source flush, so an RDNA2 measurement is not evidence for the console.
- [v_fma_mixlo_f16 and v_fma_mixhi_f16](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/FloatInstructions.cpp) round the exact product-sum once to f16 with the f64 arithmetic of v_interp_p2_f16, so they need the Float64 capability. On RDNA2 (gfx1036) they follow the `FLOAT_MODE` f16 round and denormal fields, not the f32 round field: -1.5 * 2^-24 + 2^-126 from f32 sources (0xbfc00000, 0x33800000, 0x00800000) gives 0x8001 with f16 denormals kept and round to nearest even, 0x8000 with f16 denormals flushed and 0x8002 with f16 round toward -infinity. Only f16 denormals kept with round to nearest even is translated; another f16 mode throws, as for packed f16. `FP16_OVFL` set is not measured.
- [Signaling NaN inputs](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/FloatInstructions.cpp) of the f32 and f16 unary instructions (`v_sqrt/rcp/rsq/log/exp/sin/cos/fract/floor/ceil/trunc/rndne/rcp_iflag/frexp_mant`), `v_ldexp_f32/f16`, `v_frexp_mant_f16`, `v_div_fixup_f32/f16` and `v_mullit_f32` are returned quieted only with IEEE=1 and unchanged with IEEE=0, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) in both modes, and so are those of the f64 forms (`v_sqrt/rcp/rsq/fract/floor/ceil/trunc/rndne/frexp_mant/ldexp/div_fixup_f64`) and `v_cvt_f64_f32`, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) in both modes; shaders without a known float mode follow IEEE=0. `v_pack_b32_f16` quiets each signaling NaN half by the same rule, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) in both modes. A signaling NaN result of `v_cubesc/cubetc/cubema_f32` is quieted by the same rule and keeps the sign the instruction gives it, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) in both modes. `v_add/sub/subrev/mul/fma/fmac/fmamk/fmaak/mul_legacy_f32` and `v_fma_mix_f32` with f32 sources choose their NaN result on the bits, measured over 1000 operand triples in both modes: the first NaN source in operand order after its modifiers (the subtrahend negated, as the hardware adds its negation), quieted only with IEEE=1, or the default NaN 0xffc00000 for an invalid operation; in the fma forms an invalid product (inf * 0) gives the default NaN even when src2 is a NaN, and in the legacy forms a zero factor makes the product 0 even when the other factor is a NaN (a NaN src2 still wins). `v_mad_legacy_f32` and `v_mac_legacy_f32` follow the same rule with their separately rounded product, as measured on gfx1010 (#1281, #1290): a NaN source quieted only with IEEE=1, inf - inf giving 0xffc00000, the zero rule hiding a NaN factor and a NaN src2 winning. The f16 and f64 arithmetic, `v_fma_mix_f32` with f16 sources and the separately rounded mad/mac forms (not measurable on RDNA2) follow the same rule on hardware but their NaN bits are left to the driver. `v_cvt_f16_f32`, `v_cvt_f32_f64`, `v_dot2_f32_f16`, `v_fma_mixlo_f16` and `v_fma_mixhi_f16` always quiet and `v_div_scale_f32` never does: the f32 signaling NaN 0x7f812345 as src0 or src2 gives 0x7e09 from mixlo and mixhi at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1 on RDNA2 (gfx1036), where `v_fma_mix_f32` gives 0x7f812345 at IEEE=0 and 0x7fc12345 at IEEE=1. The payload of an f16 NaN source of mixlo and mixhi is left to the driver's f16 to f32 conversion, which NVIDIA replaces.
- [Scalar f16 add, sub, subrev and mul denormals](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/FloatInstructions.cpp) follow the `FLOAT_MODE` f16 denormal field at nearest-even: bit 0 preserves inputs and bit 1 preserves the result; otherwise a denormal is flushed to its signed zero. Measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) with IEEE=0/1 and FP16_OVFL=0/1, over 32 operand pairs in all four denormal modes, including signed zeros, denormals, minimum normals, infinities and NaNs. Multiply detects tininess after rounding to 11 significant bits with an unbounded exponent, before gradual half packing: `0x3bff * 0x0400` flushes, while `0x3c01 * 0x03ff` rounds to `0x0400` and is kept. Directed rounding and ternary arithmetic keep their previous denormal lowering. Shaders without a known float mode keep the previous lowering. Scalar `v_sub_f16`/`v_subrev_f16` share `float16Binary` with add/mul and were measured on gfx1036 over the same operand set as add (1156 non-NaN-input rows per mode); NaN result bits still depend on the driver as recorded above.
- [Packed f16 NaN results](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/FloatInstructions.cpp) (`v_pk_add/mul/fma/min/max_f16`) follow the hardware measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1 with f16 round-to-nearest-even and f16 denormals kept: with IEEE=1 a signaling NaN input is quieted and min/max return the other operand only for a quiet NaN; with IEEE=0 NaN inputs pass through unquieted and min/max ignore signaling. In `v_pk_fma_f16` an invalid product (inf * 0) gives the default NaN even when src2 is a NaN. A shader without a known float mode is translated as IEEE=0, the mode titles use; a mode with another f16 rounding or that flushes f16 denormals throws.
- [f32 denormals](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/TranslationContext/OperandAccess.cpp) follow the shader's `FLOAT_MODE` f32 denormal field (bits 5:4) when it flushes them (0, and shaders without a known float mode, as in the 0xc0 most titles use), measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) over 1444 rows per instruction at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1. `v_add/sub/subrev/mul/mul_legacy/mullit/fma/fmac/fmamk/fmaak/min/max/min3/max3/med3/fract/floor/ceil/frexp_mant/sin/cos_f32` flush their f32 inputs and their result to a zero of the same sign (`v_mullit_f32` measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) over 6907 rows per mode at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1, so a positive denormal src2 counts as zero); the f32 compares, `v_cvt_i32/u32/rpi_i32/flr_i32_f32`, `v_frexp_exp_i32_f32`, `v_cvt_f64_f32`, `v_cvt_f16_f32`, `v_cvt_pkrtz_f16_f32`, `v_cvt_pknorm_i16/u16_f32` and `v_cvt_pk_u8_f32` flush their f32 inputs. `v_cvt_f32_f64` flushes its f32 result to a zero of the same sign, as the ISA's single-precision denormal field describes and LLVM (`SITargetLowering::isCanonicalized` counts `FP_ROUND` as flushing) and ACO (`gather_canonicalized`) assume; not measured. A source below 2^-126 that rounds to 2^-126 gives 2^-126; where this conversion detects tininess is not measured. `v_div_scale_f32` flushes all three inputs to signed zeros, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) over 2744 operand triples per mode at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1 with f32 denormals flushed and kept; its scale-down behaviour and host denormal preservation remain incomplete. `v_div_fixup_f32` flushes its src1 and src2 and keeps a denormal src0 and result, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) over 6859 rows per mode at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1. Tininess is detected after rounding, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) for `v_mul_f32` and `v_mullit_f32` at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1: a product is flushed when its value rounded to 24 bits with an unbounded exponent is below 2^-126, so `0x3f7ffffe * 0x00800001` (2^-126 * (1 - 2^-46)) gives `0x00800000` and `0x3f7fffff * 0x00800000` (2^-126 * (1 - 2^-24)) gives zero. The check rounds in the f32 round mode of `FLOAT_MODE` (bits 1:0), measured in all four modes, while the products themselves are still rounded to nearest even. This applies to `v_mul_f32`, `v_mul_legacy_f32`, `v_mullit_f32`, and to `v_fma_f32`, `v_fmac_f32`, `v_fmamk_f32`, `v_fmaak_f32` and `v_fma_mix_f32` when the addend is zero; with a nonzero addend the fused result is kept, so an exact result just below 2^-126 that rounds to it is not flushed. In the three directed modes the check is computed exactly from the significands. To nearest even (0, the mode of shaders without a known float mode) one factor is multiplied by 2^64, which is exact below 2^64, and the product with the other factor is compared with 2^-62. That multiply is a plain `OpFMul` like the product, and no f32 rounding mode is requested in the SPIR-V, so the check relies on the driver rounding f32 to nearest even by default, as the products do, and a product that rounds up to 2^-126 is the emitted product. `v_fma_mix_f32` follows this rule with mixed f16/f32 sources too, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) with f16 in either multiplicand slot, low and high half selection, and an f32 or f16 zero addend: f32 `0x00801002` times f16 `0x3bff` gives `0x00800000` to nearest at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1, while the neighbours `0x00801001` and `0x00801003` give zero and `0x00800001`. The tiny-product boundary was also measured for both signs in all four f32 round modes at IEEE=0; f16 inputs retain their previous conversion and denormal handling. `v_fma_mixlo_f16` and `v_fma_mixhi_f16` flush their f32 sources too and keep a product below 2^-126, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036). `v_mad_legacy_f32` and `v_mac_legacy_f32` flush their inputs, their product and their result in every mode, see its entry; their product is still checked for tininess on its exact value, so one below 2^-126 gives zero even when it rounds to 2^-126, as the console's separately rounded encoding is not measured at this boundary. Denormal modes 1 and 2 throw for the other instructions. Not covered, and not measurable on RDNA2 (no such opcodes) or not measured: `v_mac_f32`, `v_mad_f32`, `v_madak_f32` and `v_madmk_f32` (rounded separately), `v_dot2_f32_f16` and `v_div_fmas_f32` (on hardware `v_div_fmas_f32` flushes only its result), f32 interpolation and `ds_add_f32` keep the previous lowering; `ds_min/max/cmpst_f32` flush both operands by the field, see the LDS float atomics entry. With f32 denormals kept (3) the results depend on the driver's default denormal behaviour, as no `DenormPreserve` execution mode is requested; `v_ldexp_f32` and the f32 compares are the exceptions. `v_cmp_X_f32` and `v_cmpx_X_f32` compare the operands as sign-magnitude integers with the NaN cases handled apart, so a denormal is not read as zero, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) over 256 operand pairs per opcode at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1 with f32 denormals kept and flushed. `v_ldexp_f32` preserves subnormal inputs and rounds a subnormal result in the f32 round mode of `FLOAT_MODE`, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) over 2163 rows in each of the four round modes at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1. An overflow rounds like any other result (infinity, or the largest finite value when the mode rounds that sign toward zero). With f32 denormals flushed it flushes a subnormal input and result, measured over the same rows in each round mode at IEEE=0 only. A device whose driver splits `Fma` into a multiply and an add flushes a product below 2^-126 before adding a non-zero addend in `v_fma_f32` and `v_fmac_f32`: `0x1fffffff * 0x1fffffff + 0x00800000` gives `0x00800000` instead of the fused `0x00ffffff` on RADV RENOIR (Mesa 26.2.4), whose compiled shader has `v_mad_f32` and no `v_fma_f32`. [F32DenormalFlush](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/tests/execution/F32DenormalFlush.cpp) accepts that sum on such a device.
- The executable file that [relinker](../../core/relinker/elfpatcher/src/windows/WindowsPeWriter.cpp) generates opens the console when launched, which is inconvenient for playability.
- [Overlay output bus](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVideoOut/src/VideoOutDriver.cpp) (libSceVideoOut) - flips on a port opened with `SCE_VIDEO_OUT_BUS_TYPE_OVERLAY` complete and signal their events like main-bus flips, but their buffers are not shown: the overlay is not composited over the main output. Presenting them as whole frames made the window alternate between the two buses
- [PA_CL_CLIP_CNTL](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - `DX_LINEAR_ATTR_CLIP_ENA` (bit 24) clear is drawn like bit 24 set, with Vulkan's clipping of `NoPerspective` outputs; what a clear bit changes on the console is undocumented.
- [10_11_11 unorm color targets](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) render into R32_UINT attachments: the pixel shader packs each export, rounded to nearest even like format-30 image stores, which is assumed to match the CB's conversion.
- [PA_SU_SC_MODE_CNTL.PROVOKING_VTX_LAST](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Pipeline.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) uses Vulkan's last-vertex mode for native primitives. Bit 19 follows [opengnm's `flatshade_first` mapping](https://github.com/blackbearreloaded/ps5-opengl/blob/db752da4c89380af228497b5b6852163a4e61069/src/gallium/ps5/ps5_screen.c#L2244); vertex selection follows [Vulkan](https://docs.vulkan.org/spec/latest/chapters/drawing.html#drawing-primitive-topologies), without console measurements. Last mode throws without `VK_EXT_provoking_vertex`, for generated rectangle, mesh or tessellation primitives, explicit per-vertex flat inputs, and triangle strips whose pixel shader reads a vertex slot itself (`v_interp_mov_f32`, or 16-bit interpolation, which is not checked yet), since the vertex order of odd strip triangles [depends on the device](https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VkPhysicalDeviceFragmentShaderBarycentricPropertiesKHR.html). `v_interp_p1_f32` and `v_interp_p2_f32` read P10 and P20 against the guest's I and J, which come from `gl_BaryCoordKHR` in the same vertex order as the per-vertex values, so the order cancels out and strips with only those reads draw. A FLAT_SHADE input without pass-through that is read through `v_interp_mov_f32` P10 and not P20 [returns the provoking vertex's value](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ShaderInfoCollector.cpp), like P0. ps5-opengl reads every flat input that way, and its driver passes the GL 4.6 CTS on a PS5 (#1413). The lane itself was not measured on the console, so with the first provoking vertex such a shader throws.
- [v_rcp, v_rsq, v_sqrt, v_log, v_exp, v_sin and v_cos](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/FloatInstructions.cpp) (f32 and f16) use the driver's approximation for finite results, not the console's: the low bits can differ. Only NaN, invalid, zero and infinite results and the exact sin/cos zeros are chosen on the bits. Measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) over every f16 input and 32,832 f32 inputs: RADV returns the hardware's bits for every input of rcp, rsq, sqrt and log and all but one of exp (f16 0x11c5, 1 ULP); NVIDIA (615.71.09) stays within 2 ULP except `v_log_f32` near 1.0 (next entry). [v_sin and v_cos](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAlu/SpirvAluEmitterMath.cpp) take the driver's sine or cosine of the radians of the exact cycle, except within 2^-12 of a turn from a zero of the function, where the result is the linear term of the exact distance to the zero: the hardware's constant 6.2831826 (0x40c90fd5) at the integers, which the hardware applies exactly to inputs below 2^-12 of a turn, and the f32 2π at the half and quarter turns, where the hardware is within 7 ULP of it. RADV returns the hardware's bits for every f16 input and for 32,422 sine and 32,486 cosine results of the 32,832 f32 inputs; the rest are 1 ULP off, except up to 14 ULP on either side of non-zero integers and up to 15 ULP beyond ±256 cycles, where the hardware's own reduction differs from the exact fraction, and up to 6 ULP inside the half- and quarter-turn bands. NVIDIA's sine is within 1 ULP on 93% of the f32 inputs and up to 1,166 ULP off just outside the bands, its cosine within 163 ULP. A denormal f32 input with f32 denormals kept gives the hardware the input times 6.2831826; both hosts flush it.
- [v_log_f32 and v_log_f16](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAlu/SpirvAluEmitterMath.cpp) keep the driver's log2 unless, for inputs within 1/8 of 1.0, it is more than 4 ULP from an eight-term series in x - 1, which then replaces it. `v_log_f16` goes through the same f32 lowering and is rounded to half. The series is evaluated for every `v_log` and chosen with `OpSelect` rather than a branch, so its seven fused multiply-adds and two multiplies are an unconditional cost. Measured on RDNA2 over all 3,145,728 f32 inputs within 1/8 of 1.0 (gfx1035, IEEE 0, f32 denormals flushed), the hardware is within 1 ULP of the correctly rounded log2 and the series within 2 ULP of it and within 3 ULP of the hardware (2 except at 0x3f752e47, 0x3f7baab0 and 0x3f7bc4fb), so a kept driver value is within 7 ULP of the hardware and a driver that returns the hardware's bits never triggers the gate; RADV (Mesa 26.2.4) returns the hardware's bits on the 9,280 such inputs measured on gfx1036; NVIDIA (615.71.09) was up to 23,947,836 ULP off at 1 - 2^-24 and is within 6 ULP inside the band after the change; outside it its own log2 stays up to 9 ULP off (measured up to 1.375). Over every f16 input, the change moves one `v_log_f16` result on NVIDIA (0x3bed, 1 ULP, onto the hardware's bits) and none on RADV.
- [Dual-source blending](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) takes the MRT1 export as the second source of the MRT0 blend, as RADV and radeonsi program it; what the CB does with other export or target layouts is unknown, so they throw.
- [SDK memory-management syscall traps](../../core/relinker/relinker/src/analysis/SyscallScanner.cpp) under `--trap-sdk-syscall-stubs` recognize three exact instruction sequences from [ps5-payload-dev SDK libc/mman.c](https://github.com/ps5-payload-dev/sdk/blob/12cf3dda40/libc/mman.c), observed in Nuvio Maxd 0.5.15 (PPSA99288): syscall 74 and the two syscall 477 paths, with their argument preparation and carry-flag error handling. Matching requires a decoded instruction boundary and the complete sequence inside the scanned code range. Other SDK/compiler output remains rejected. The matched syscall becomes `UD2`; this does not implement mmap or mprotect and a call still faults.
- [ANDN lowering](../../core/relinker/codegen/src/x86/AndnLowering.cpp) uses scalar NOT and AND for 32- and 64-bit register and memory operands under `--to-rosetta`, following the ANDN entry in [Intel SDM volume 2A](https://cdrdv2-public.intel.com/812383/253666-sdm-vol-2a.pdf). SF and ZF follow the result, CF and OF clear, and AF and PF are undefined. The scratch register is saved below the 128-byte red zone. A 64-bit stack-relative displacement that exceeds signed disp32 after reserving the spill frame throws; other BMI instructions are not lowered. The M1 test covers register overlap, RSP operands, memory addressing, RIP relocation, flags and red-zone preservation; precise fault delivery inside a spill sequence has not been verified.
- The [instruction decoder](../../core/relinker/codegen/src/x86/X64InstructionDecoder.cpp) decodes a near CALL, JMP or Jcc with an operand-size prefix (`66`) as Intel does, with a 32-bit or 8-bit displacement and a 64-bit target. AMD processors, the console included, read a 16-bit displacement for `66 E8`, `66 E9` and `66 0F 80`-`8F` and truncate the target of every near branch with that prefix, rel8 forms included, to 16 bits. Compilers do not emit these forms; the relinker's length and target for them do not match the console.
- `--to-intel` does not lower RDPRU/MCOMMIT; the [matcher](../../core/relinker/codegen/src/x86/Amd64OnlyInstructionMatcher.cpp) fails the relink instead.
- In executables and guest modules with a `PT_SCE_DYNLIBDATA` segment, the [code analysis](../../core/relinker/relinker/src/analysis/CodeInstructionCollector.cpp) of the Windows and macOS outputs follows code only from the end of `PT_INTERP` to the end of the PLT after the last function of the unwind table, the end the pipeline's scans above use; a guest module with PLT relocations and no PLT after its last unwind function fails as the executable does. Code after that PLT is not analyzed: a TLS access in it is not rewritten, an entry point or pointer into it is ignored, and a function symbol or init/fini array entry pointing into it fails the conversion.
- [Ordered not-equal compares](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAlu/SpirvAluEmitterMath.cpp) (shader recompiler) - `FPOrdNotEqual32` is emitted as `OpFUnordNotEqual` with both operands checked for NaN on their bits instead of `OpFOrdNotEqual`; the two agree for every input. The AMD Windows driver 32.0.13058.8001 (Radeon 860M, RDNA 3.5) returns 1 for a NaN operand when any float form of the compare feeds a ballot on a 32-wide subgroup (#825)
- [sce::Job::JobManager](../../core/libs/prx/libSceJobManager/Export.cpp) (libSceJobManager) is written from the disassembly of the copies PPSA01474 and PPSA36082 ship in `sce_module`, and `guest_job_manager_tests` passes against both of those modules as well: the memory size formula, the error codes, sync point ordering, the per-sequence limits and the priority order are the original's. Only CPU sequences exist: `initialize` and `calculateRequiredMemorySize` with compute sequences, and both compute dispatches, are not implemented, and `createCompute` and `destroyCompute` return the errors the original returns when no compute sequence was configured. The manager's state lives on the host heap, with only the block header written to the title's memory; the original keeps everything in that block, and a block smaller than 0x2d8 bytes throws here. The worker sleep policy, the spin count and the persistent thread count are validated and stored but change nothing, a factory priority above 1 throws, `shutdown` throws while a thread is blocked in a sequence wait, and using a sequence after its `destroy` throws. `initialize` returns 0 where the original returns the result of its last import (`BG26hBGiNlw`, not resolved to a name), the `sceRazorCpuJobManager*` profiler calls are not made, and a worker thread that cannot be created or configured throws where the original does not check. The order of the sequence and factory virtual functions is read from the modules' vtables only, since no title that calls them has been run
- The length-changing path of the [instruction rewriter](../../core/relinker/codegen/src/x86/X64InstructionRewriter.cpp) is not used by the [converter](../../core/relinker/codegen/src/Amd64OnlyConverter.cpp): it does not adjust data-to-code references (relocations, FDEs, jump tables) or segment sizes, so every substitution keeps the instruction length.
- A `PT_SCE_RELRO` segment that no `PT_LOAD` covers, the PS4 layout, is [loaded](../../core/relinker/relinker/src/parsing/ElfReader.cpp) as a read-write `PT_LOAD` and stays writable after relocation; FreeBSD rtld makes `PT_GNU_RELRO` read-only once relocation is done, and what the console does with `PT_SCE_RELRO` is not checked.
- In executables with a `PT_SCE_DYNLIBDATA` segment, the PS4 layout, the executable segment also holds read-only data after the code (CUSA00900: 37 MiB after its last function). The [relinker pipeline](../../core/relinker/relinker/src/pipeline/RelinkerPipeline.cpp) therefore scans for syscalls and import call sites only up to the end of the PLT that follows the last function of the unwind table; code after the PLT without an unwind entry is not scanned. `--to-intel` and the `unused-filter=1` and `unused-filter=2` analyses are not limited this way.
- A relocation against a dynamic symbol entry that is all zero, like the null symbol, takes the value 0 plus its addend, as the gABI defines for `STN_UNDEF` ([pipeline](../../core/relinker/relinker/src/pipeline/RelinkerPipeline.cpp)). The Linux output imports such an entry as an unnamed weak symbol, which the host loader resolves to 0; the Windows and macOS outputs write the value into the slot ([relocation builder](../../core/relinker/elfpatcher/src/windows/WindowsRelocationBuilder.cpp)). What the console's loader does with such an entry is not measured. OliSe Player (PPSA01153) has seven of them, and its code calls through those slots only when they are not 0 ([#4437](https://github.com/boykopovar/AnyPS5/issues/4437)).
- [DB_SHADER_CONTROL.CONSERVATIVE_Z_EXPORT](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderInputState.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) becomes `DepthLess`/`DepthGreater`. For a pixel shader that breaks the promise, the host driver decides whether its interpolated Z is depth-tested before shading; what the console does then was not measured.
- [DB_SHADER_CONTROL.PRIMITIVE_ORDERED_PIXEL_SHADER](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvModuleEmitter.cpp) (shader recompiler) - the host pixel interlock spans the whole shader, not only up to `s_sendmsg` ORDERED_PS_DONE.
- [POPS collision SGPRs](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvModuleEmitter.cpp) (shader recompiler) - the collision wave ID and intrawave collision mask read 0, so a title skips its POPS wait and runs its per-collision loop once.
- [Image loads](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceTracker.cpp) (shader recompiler) - an image_load reads a sampled binding, also of an image the shader stores to, so in a primitive-ordered pixel shader it misses an earlier overlapping primitive's image_store in the same draw.
- [PA_SC_CONSERVATIVE_RASTERIZATION_CNTL](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - 0x6001 uses Vulkan overestimation on devices that add at most 1/256 pixel and keep degenerate triangles; the console's uncertainty region and degenerate handling are unmeasured. A value with `OVER_RAST_ENABLE` and `UNDER_RAST_ENABLE` clear is decoded as disabled even when `UNCERTAINTY_REGION_MODE` or the `OUTER_`/`INNER_UNCERTAINTY_EDGERULE_OVERRIDE` bits (19:16) are set, as PPSA01474 writes 0xe6000; that these fields have no effect without over- or underestimation is assumed from their names, not measured.
- A [Metal depth surface](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/Metal/MetalDepthSurface.mm) (libSceAgcDriver) is retired from the surfaces a draw samples when a color target or another depth target is bound over its depth or stencil plane; sampling then reads the memory like any texture. Bound again, it keeps its own depth instead of the bytes written over it. A storage image, a CPU write or a driver store over its planes does not retire it, so a view of another layout at its address still throws.
- A [Metal depth surface](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/Metal/MetalDraw.mm) (libSceAgcDriver) is served to a texture only as a single-level 2D view or, through `GatherDepthSlices`, a single-level 2D array of its extent. There is no copy of a depth plane into a texture of another layout, so a cube, a mip chain or rows padded to the swizzle block at its address fail the draw (`resident depth view is incompatible`), and a dispatch reads guest memory, which holds no depth. In a dispatch, a storage image over a depth plane is bound to guest memory: its writes do not reach the surface, and a surface created later at that address starts cleared instead of from those writes.
- The Linux placement of the `--to-intel` stubs in the [ELF patcher](../../core/relinker/elfpatcher/src/linux/LinuxElfPatcher.cpp) is covered only by a synthetic test.
- The libc [SSE4a trap emulation](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/specifics/windows/Sse4aEmulation.hpp) on Windows is superseded by `--to-intel` and remains only until the relinked title has been verified without it.
- [getifaddrs](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Interfaces.cpp) (libc) lists the host's IPv4 and IPv6 addresses in FreeBSD's `ifaddrs` and sockaddr layouts. FreeBSD's per-interface AF_LINK entries (MAC, index, MTU) and their `if_data` are not produced, so interfaces without an address are omitted. Names are the host's (Linux `lo`, `eth0`; Windows NDIS names such as `ethernet_32769`, or `if<index>` when one does not fit 15 bytes), not the console's. A Linux point-to-point address without a peer loses `IFF_POINTOPOINT`, and an IPv4 broadcast address the host omits is computed from the netmask. On Windows the flags are only `IFF_UP`, `IFF_RUNNING`, `IFF_LOOPBACK`, `IFF_BROADCAST` and `IFF_MULTICAST`, derived from the adapter: PPP and tunnel adapters get neither `IFF_BROADCAST` nor `IFF_POINTOPOINT` because their peer address is not reported, and an address whose prefix length the host reports as unspecified gets a host mask.
- [gethostname](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Hostname.cpp) (libc) reports the fixed guest name `AnyPS5` without querying the host machine name. Buffer copying, null queries and `ENAMETOOLONG` follow [FreeBSD gethostname](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/main/lib/libc/gen/gethostname.c); the console's configurable hostname and its integration with `sysctl` and name resolution are unverified and not implemented.
- [Descriptor pool chain](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderResources.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - each pool holds two storage buffer descriptors more than a set can take, as a workaround: RADV (Mesa 26.0 and later) lets a `FREE_DESCRIPTOR_SET` pool place a set up to 32 bytes past the pool's buffer ([`util_vma_heap_init`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/main/src/amd/vulkan/radv_descriptor_pool.c) is given `bo_size + RADV_POOL_HEAP_OFFSET` as a size), and with a page-multiple pool size those bytes are an unmapped page
- [INDEX_ATTRIBUTES_INDIRECT](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Pm4.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver, opcode 0x91) loads INDEX_BASE, INDEX_BUFFER_SIZE (in indices) and INDEX_TYPE from the 16-byte record at `attribute_base + attribute_index * 16`, read when the packet runs. The record layout (address low, address high, size, type) is PAL's [`IndexAttribIndirect`](https://github.com/GPUOpen-Drivers/pal/blob/c5e800072a32f68b6ccc4422936d96167c6e0728/src/core/hw/gfxip/gfx9/gfx9Chip.h#L60-L67) and the packet is the one [`BuildIndexAttributesIndirect`](https://github.com/GPUOpen-Drivers/pal/blob/c5e800072a32f68b6ccc4422936d96167c6e0728/src/core/hw/gfxip/gfx9/gfx9CmdUtil.cpp#L2586-L2613) writes; neither is measured on a PS5 or seen in a title. `indirect_mode` 1 (`addr_offset`, which only PAL's ExecuteIndirect path emits) throws, and so does a record whose index type is above 3, as INDEX_TYPE does.
- [CMASK fast clears](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Draw.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - each fast-cleared CMASK tile (code 0) is stored as `CB_COLOR_CLEAR_WORD` into its 8x8 texels at the first draw into the target or at its fast-clear eliminate or DCC decompress pass, not as the color block writes the tile, and the CMASK is then stored as expanded (0xF): a texture or copy read of the target after that draw and before the title's eliminate sees the clear value in tiles no draw wrote, where the console keeps their previous bytes. Tile addresses follow the pipe-aligned SW_64KB_Z_X CMASK equation for 16 pipes; other CMASK swizzle modes, codes other than 0 and 0xF, a cleared tile of a DCC target and a fast clear of a mipmapped (base mip 0), 3D or array color target throw. When the clear runs as a compute pass (a resident target whose CMASK is host-imported), an invalid code throws at the next fast-clear materialization, not at the one that read it. Single-sample views with `viewMip > 0` skip CMASK fast-clear decoding, since CMASK metadata covers only mip 0 (Mesa `si_state.c` likewise clears `FAST_CLEAR` for level > 0)
- [HTILE depth fast clears](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/DepthSurface.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - HTILE is not modeled. Only RADV's `clear_htile_mask` compute kernel (17 GFX10 words, 64x1x1 groups) is recognized, and only when it leaves ZMASK at 0 (`user[4]` and `user[5]` low nibbles both 0); the dispatch still runs, and the host depth image is cleared to `DB_DEPTH_CLEAR` the next time the surface with that `DB_HTILE_DATA_BASE` address is bound. The address match is exact, so a clear of a different HTILE range does not hit. HTILE initialization, the stencil-only update, and fast clears made by the SDK's own paths, CP DMA fills or other kernels are not covered and leave the depth image as it was. The kernel words are the ones the PR author saw RADV submit; they are not compared with Mesa's source.
- [Host imports](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestBufferMemory.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) on Linux skip ranges that are not mapped read-write, which are copied instead. The NVIDIA driver (595.91.07) refuses `VK_EXT_external_memory_host` imports of read-only or executable mappings (and shared mappings of regular files) with `VK_ERROR_OUT_OF_DEVICE_MEMORY`, after which every `vkAllocateMemory` on the device fails the same way. Read-only ranges are never imported, even on drivers that accept them. Shared mappings of regular files are not checked for. On Windows, a run of adjacent registered ranges whose shared views are not one contiguous run of views of a section gets no read-write alias, so its ranges are imported one at a time, as before runs were imported whole, and a buffer that crosses two of them is copied. Adjacent registered ranges are joined into one import only where they fill a whole 8 MiB aligned window; ranges of a partly filled window are imported one at a time, and a buffer that crosses two imports is copied. The 8 MiB window is a choice: unbounded runs were re-imported whole on every growth of a heap mapped in small pieces, and the NVIDIA driver (610.57.04, 615.78.08) then refused imports or lost the device. Not measured on RADV.
- [Cube texture descriptors](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestTextureResource.cpp) with equal `BASE_ARRAY` and `DEPTH` expose six faces starting at `BASE_ARRAY`. Measured with `image_load`, `image_sample` and `image_get_resinfo` on RX 6600 (gfx1032), using 64x64 R32_UINT faces and base slices 0, 5 and 6. Other ranges containing fewer than six faces or incomplete cube arrays still throw: hardware load bounds and filtered-sample addressing differ. Out-of-range cube-array sampling remains unimplemented. Samplers with `DISABLE_CUBE_WRAP` are created with `VK_SAMPLER_CREATE_NON_SEAMLESS_CUBE_MAP_BIT_EXT`, the flag RADV maps to that bit (Mesa `src/amd/vulkan/radv_sampler.c`, `src/amd/common/ac_descriptors.c`); without `VK_EXT_non_seamless_cube_map` a cube texture sampled through such a sampler throws. PS5 equivalence has not been measured on console hardware.
- [DCC display buffers](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/VulkanDevice.cpp) are presented only when all keys of the console's DCC extent agree on uncompressed or a fast-clear code, padding keys included; other keys, and register-clear keys over a pending image, throw.
- [Storage image write-back](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Texture.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) stores the GPU results over a CPU-stamped range whose guest bytes equal the copy taken when the image's layers got their generation (images up to 1 MiB whose guest bytes were settled at that point, with no unit shadow, pending image results or recorded GPU store over them; other images keep the previous rule: the CPU's bytes win). A CPU store that rewrites the baseline value after the GPU changed it is not told apart from no store, and the GPU result wins there. The 1 MiB limit is a choice, not a console value.
- [16-bit float display buffers](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/DisplayBuffer.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver, libSceVideoOut) - pixel formats `0xC001000622000000` and `0xC001000600000000` are taken as R16G16B16A16 and B16G16R16A16 float, eight bytes per texel, as [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/59183dbfa81ed7efdff6069177fed50de0552725/src/graphics/host_gpu/renderer/image/imageInfo.h) maps them and [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/5dfdb4b0b166e83dc9ae275001afb695f12c7174/src/SharpProspero/Interop/VideoOut/VideoOutTypes.cs) names them (`Rgba16Float`, `Bgra16Float`); PPSA11386 registers 3840x2160 tiled buffers of the first. The tiled layout is assumed to be the 8-byte SW_64KB_R_X equation of `TextureSwizzleEquations.hpp` applied to full coordinates, as addrlib applies it, by analogy with the 32-bit scanout; `ColorTargetLayout` applies the equations to in-block coordinates, which for 8-byte texels differs in every odd 64-row block row (address bit 11 takes y bit 6) and for 16-byte texels also differs, so a float display buffer written through `WriteColorTarget` and presented from guest memory would show those rows misplaced. The values are assumed to be linear light and are encoded with the sRGB curve for the 8-bit output. Bits 32-39 of these formats are `0x06`, as in the 10-bit formats SharpProspero lists without sRGB transfer, while its sRGB formats have `0x00`, its 10-bit PQ formats `0x04` (with `0x07` in bits 40-47) and its float BT.2100 PQ formats `0x07`: they show only that these formats are not the sRGB ones. NaN and negative values give 0, values of 1 and above give 255, alpha is linear. Neither the layout nor the transfer is verified against console or title frames, and HDR output is not modelled. DCC metadata on a float buffer throws.
- [DCC key stores](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/DccMetadata.cpp) cover the console's DCC extent only for pipe-aligned DCC of single-level, single-layer 4-byte SW_64KB_R_X surfaces; other surfaces, and every texture and color target key read, cover one byte per 256 bytes.
- [SW_64KB_Z_X color targets](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ColorTargetLayout.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) use the texture equations and 64 KiB block geometry of `SW_64KB_Z_X`, whose addresses are checked against [AMD addrlib GFX10](https://github.com/GPUOpen-Drivers/pal/blob/c5e800072a32f68b6ccc4422936d96167c6e0728/src/core/imported/addrlib/src/gfx10/gfx10addrlib.cpp) in the texture tiling tests. Any 256-byte pipe/bank XOR below the block size is accepted, as for `SW_64KB_R_X` targets and Z_X textures; which of those bits the console honours, and the render results, are not measured.
- [DCC key fills over other images](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Texture.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - a uniform fill at a live image's `dccAddress`, at least one key per 256 bytes long, is a key fill of that image even when other live images overlap it, but only while it is no longer than the image's key extent (`DccKeyCount`, the pipe-aligned count); a longer one is classified by its overlaps as before. The bound is inferred, not measured on a title: a longer fill also writes the bytes past the keys, which the overlapping image may own. Whether the title's bloom-target key fills stay within the extent was not checked (`APS5_TRACE_DCC_KEYS` prints the length of the fills classified as keys, not of the refused ones).
- [Comp-to-single DCC keys](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Draw.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - key 0x10 (`GFX9_DCC_CLEAR_SINGLE` in Mesa `ac_descriptors.h`) is expanded only by a CB DCC decompress pass over uniform keys of a non-linear target: every texel takes the element at the start of its 256-byte block, the top-left texel of the block where radv's `build_clear_dcc_comp_to_single_shader` stores the clear color. A fast-clear eliminate over such keys and mixed keys throw; textures and color targets that read them use their stored texels. "Uniform" is judged over the key bytes the driver reads, one per 256 surface bytes (see DCC key stores above), so a pipe-aligned surface whose keys differ past that range is expanded as if all were comp-to-single, as for the other clear codes. Not verified on hardware
- [8_SRGB color targets](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Draw.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) on a device that cannot attach and blend R8_SRGB render into an RGBA8_SRGB copy of the target, so the sRGB encode, decode and blend rounding are the device's; the console's are not measured.
- [8_SRGB color targets](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Texture.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) on such a device throw unless they are single-mip 2D surfaces with a resident image; linear targets have none.
- [1_5_5_5 color targets](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) render into A1R5G5B5. AMD formats keep the first component in the least significant bits (X in bits 0-4, Y in 5-9, Z in 10-14, W in bit 15, as in the [fpPS4](https://github.com/red-prig/fpPS4) `kSurfaceFormat1_5_5_5` doc comment and as measured for textures in the packed 16-bit textures entry), while A1R5G5B5 keeps R in bits 10-14 and B in bits 0-4, so the pixel exports are written with X and Z exchanged, which makes the stored bits follow the guest layout. The decoded format, component mapping and write mask are tested, and an execution test draws 32_ABGR exports into tiled and linear targets and compares every stored texel with the guest layout on the host device; the export direction is not measured on a PS5. Only UNORM with the standard component swap is accepted. Constant blend colors go through the same component-swap handling as other targets with that export mapping (`0xc6`), which is not tested for this format. PPSA06699 renders into this format.
- [COPY_DATA from the GPU clock](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Pm4.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver, `SRC_SEL` 9) writes the host steady clock in 10 ns ticks when the packet runs, the same counter as the `RELEASE_MEM` timestamp, not the console's GPU clock. The `COPY_DATA` cache policy fields are accepted and have no effect.
- [Blending into reversed-order color targets](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - 8_8_8_8 and 16_16_16_16 targets with `COMP_SWAP` 2 or 3 keep the exported alpha in their first channel and red or blue in their fourth, which Vulkan would blend with the color and the alpha equation respectively. The color block is taken to blend in shader export order and apply `COMP_SWAP` afterwards: the alpha equation and every source-alpha factor use the exported alpha, and the destination alpha is the first channel. This follows the `CB_TARGET_MASK` text of the AMD [SI](https://xorg.freedesktop.org/docs/AMD/old/SI_3D_registers.pdf) (p. 229) and [CIK](https://xorg.freedesktop.org/docs/AMD/old/CIK_3D_registers_v2.pdf) (p. 238) register references ("the order of components after blending and before COMP_SWAP is applied"). RADV builds `CB_BLENDn_CONTROL` without regard to the swap ([radv_pipeline_graphics.c](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/mesa-26.2.2/src/amd/vulkan/radv_pipeline_graphics.c#L650-L709)) and renders R4G4B4A4, B4G4R4A4, R5G5B5A1 and B5G5R5A1 with `SWAP_STD_REV` and `SWAP_ALT_REV` ([vk_format.c](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/mesa-26.2.2/src/vulkan/util/vk_format.c#L43-L48), [ac_formats.c](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/mesa-26.2.2/src/amd/common/ac_formats.c#L609-L624)). Mesa CI's Navi10 (gfx1010) CTS run ([job 112381553](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/jobs/112381553), [112381554](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/jobs/112381554)) passes all 400 `blend.format.<format>.states` cases of each of those formats, including separate alpha and destination-alpha factors. It is not measured on gfx1013 or on 8_8_8_8 and 16_16_16_16 targets. Factor pairs Vulkan applies per channel are used directly. Otherwise the pixel shader only copies values: it writes the exported alpha, each channel's own export or the constants 0 and 1 into a second color at location 0 index 1 (and 1 into a channel of the first color whose source factor is ONE, moving its export into the factor), and the blend forms every factor, `1 -` and product from those copies through `SRC1_COLOR`/`SRC1_ALPHA` factors. The blend unit therefore rounds them as it rounds a plain Vulkan blend of the same export; Vulkan does not require a second-source color to be converted like the first, but on an RTX 5080 and lavapipe every reversed-order case of the execution test is bit-identical to the same draw into a `SWAP_STD` target, which the test checks. Factor combinations that copies cannot express (for example `ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA` in the alpha equation with `SRC_COLOR` in the color equation), sRGB, SNORM and integer targets, constant and second-source factors, destination-alpha and `SRC_ALPHA_SATURATE` factors in the color equation, different color and alpha operations, MIN or MAX with factors other than ONE, other color exports when the blend needs the second color, and devices without `dualSrcBlend` throw. Gran Turismo 7 (PPSA01317) blends `CB_BLEND0_CONTROL` 0x65010504 into such a target (#4782).
- [16-bit RGBA color targets](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) support the standard and the alternate (`COMP_SWAP` 1) component order. Alternate swaps red and blue in shader exports, write masks and constant-color blend factors, following `ac_translate_colorswap` in [Mesa 26.2.2 ac_formats.c](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/mesa-26.2.2/src/amd/common/ac_formats.c). Blending into the reverse orders (`COMP_SWAP` 2 and 3) is covered by the reversed-order blending entry, and simultaneous targets requiring conflicting blend constants throw. Console equivalence has not been measured on hardware.
- [UINT16_ABGR color exports](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - export format 7 is accepted only into an unsigned integer target, and each 16-bit value reaches the target unchanged; a value beyond a narrower target's range (`R8_UINT`) is undefined in Vulkan, and whether the console clamps or truncates it is not confirmed. SINT16_ABGR (8) throws
- `--to-intel` guest module trampolines are covered only by a synthetic relinker test; no game title has been verified with them on Linux or Windows.
- `--to-rosetta` lowers what Rosetta lacks on top of `--to-intel` ([matcher](../../core/relinker/codegen/src/x86/Amd64OnlyInstructionMatcher.cpp)). Rosetta runs AVX2, FMA, BMI1/2, F16C and MOVBE but reports none of them through CPUID, so code that checks CPUID takes its baseline path. RDSEED becomes RDRAND and CLWB a NOP. RDPID returns processor 0, because Rosetta's TSC_AUX is the constant 0xAAAAAAAB; the same value reaches RDTSCP's ECX, which is not converted. ADCX/ADOX fault under Rosetta and are not converted: compilers do not emit them, and code that checks CPUID sees no ADX. The conversions are covered by the converter tests; that the converted code runs under Rosetta was checked on an M5 Max only, outside this repository's CI.
- `--to-intel` replaces the approximate reciprocal instructions (RCPPS, RSQRTPS, RCPSS and RSQRTSS: legacy and VEX encodings, 128-bit and 256-bit packed and scalar, register and memory sources) with [correctly rounded](../../core/relinker/codegen/src/x86/ReciprocalLowering.cpp) 1/sqrt(x) and 1/x, not with the AMD approximation: its tables are not public. Intel's approximation of 1/sqrt(1.0) is 0x3F7FF000, and two Newton-Raphson steps on it settle on 0.99999994 instead of 1.0; a renormalised identity quaternion then turns into a NaN axis in PPSA21564, which the console does not show. Refined results now reach the exact value; unrefined ones can still differ from the console in the low bits. Each site costs an out-of-line stub (two jumps, a spill below the red zone, SQRTPS/DIVPS). Every form gives the same exact result, so a title no longer gets different values from different encodings (under Rosetta the native instructions return Apple's approximation, for example 0x3EFFF000 for 1/2). A 256-bit site runs through a 192-byte frame below the red zone instead of a spare register. A site with no movable instruction after it for the jump keeps the native instruction (5 such 4-byte sites in PPSA21564, counted before a following RIP-relative instruction could move into the stub; not recounted).
- `--to-rosetta` runs every CMPSS/CMPSD and VCMPSS/VCMPSD whose predicate is not EQ_OQ, LT_OS or LE_OS [out of line](../../core/relinker/codegen/src/x86/ScalarCompareOperands.cpp), unchanged. Rosetta otherwise loses a full 128-bit VEX load (VMOVAPS, VMOVUPS, VMOVDQA) into the compare's destination in the same block: a following scalar read of that register (VUCOMISS, VADDSS) gets the compare's mask, so the clang `fmaxf` idiom (VCMPUNORDSS, VMAXSS, VBLENDVPS) followed by a reload and a VUCOMISS branched as if 309.0 < 309.0 and a flex layout never grew its items. Each site costs a stub and two jumps (240 in the title that showed it). `rosetta_cpuid_tests` runs every predicate of every form under Rosetta with and without the split; the load is lost in 68 of the 80 sequences as written, so a Rosetta update that fixes it shows there first.
- [sceKeyboardGetKey2Char](../../core/libs/prx/libSceKeyboard/src/keyboard_impl.cpp) (libSceKeyboard) translates the 101-key (US) arrangement and the alphanumeric layer of the 106-key (Japanese) one, which follows the JIS layout of the HID usages: the Yen (0x89) and Ro (0x87) keys return `\` (0x5C) unshifted, as JIS X 0201 places the yen sign there; whether the console returns U+00A5 instead is unverified. With the Kana LED (bit 4, the HID position) set the 106-key arrangement throws, as the kana the console returns are unknown. Ctrl and Alt do not change the character.
- [sceAudioOut2Set3DLatency](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut2System.cpp) and [sceAudioOut2MasteringInit, sceAudioOut2MasteringTerm and sceAudioOut2MasteringSetParam](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut2Mastering.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) have no effect: there is no 3D rendering or mastering stage.
- [sceImeKeyboardOpen, sceImeKeyboardClose](../../core/libs/prx/libSceIme/Export.cpp) (libSceIme) - opening a keyboard that is already open for the same user returns `0x80bc0001` (busy) and closing one that is not open returns `0x80bc0002`, as [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/main/src/libs/ime.cpp) does; it tracks one keyboard for all users, while the open state is kept per user id here, and the PS5 result is unverified
- [sceImeKeyboardGetInfo](../../core/libs/prx/libSceIme/Export.cpp) (libSceIme) returns `0x80bc0023` (no resource id) for every id while a keyboard is open, since the IME keyboard never reports a connected device. [sceImeKeyboardSetMode](../../core/libs/prx/libSceIme/Export.cpp) validates the mode bits but the mode has no effect, as no IME keyboard events are delivered.
- [AudioOut2 contexts](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut2Context.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) with an SDL device and no `sceAudioOut2ContextAdvance` calls read a grain's port buffers at the next push, at least 100 us after the grain was pushed, and play the mix behind a 40 ms cushion that the queue level leaves out. When the hardware reads the buffers is not known. The timing follows a title whose mixer fills a buffer after pushing it, reuses it two pushes later, and may still be writing it when the next push follows within microseconds. The last grain pushed before a title stops pushing is never read or played.
- [sceAudioOut2ContextAdvance and sceAudioOut2ContextPush](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut2Context.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) - with an SDL device, Advance snapshots each port's PCM and gain; Push consumes completed grains in order and a blocking Push waits for Advance when none is ready. Inferred from a title, which rewrites the same 512-frame, 12-channel float buffer on a producer thread and pushes on another thread. Console buffer ownership, snapshot timing and empty-queue errors are unverified. More than 16 completed grains awaiting Push and a nonblocking Push without a completed grain throw; the limit and errors are runtime restrictions, not console measurements. Context destruction concurrent with active calls remains unsupported. Without an SDL device the existing wall-clock path remains.
- [sceAudioOutSysGetHdmiMonitorInfo](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) reports an HDMI sink without a monitor name or audio descriptors: it zeroes the 0x180 bytes and returns 0, since audio reaches the host through SDL as PCM and there is no EDID to describe. The arguments `(type, info, size)`, the required size 0x180 and the fields titles read (monitor name at +0x0B, 13 bytes; descriptor count as a 32-bit value at +0x94; 8-byte descriptors `[coding type, channels, rate mask, 0, extra, 0, 0, 0]` from +0x98) come from [PS5-Audio-Passthrough-Research](https://github.com/sainsaji/PS5-Audio-Passthrough-Research/blob/c3be9c2afcb8f7838784ead0fe1c12a2ceea965a/docs/FINDINGS.md) (firmware 12.70, one monitor). What a console without an HDMI audio sink returns, an error or no descriptors, is unknown. The other bytes, among them the header at +0x90 (`01 09 4f 00` on that monitor), have no known meaning and are written as 0. No LPCM descriptor is reported for the host device, as the rate mask and extra byte of one are not known. A null buffer returns `0x80260004` like the other AudioOut calls; the console's code is not measured. Monitor type 0x10, which the console also accepts, other types and sizes other than 0x180 throw: their results and error codes are unknown.
- [sceAudiodecDecode](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudiodec/src/Codecs.cpp) (libSceAudiodec) - an ADTS stream with channel configuration 0 (layout in a program config element) is still parsed by FFmpeg, so `ui_enable_heaac` 0 does not remove its SBR and a mono one with Parametric Stereo comes out as stereo.
- [libSceAudiodec](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudiodec/Export.cpp) throws for the 24-bit PCM word size (`iBwPcm` 0): its sample layout is unknown. ATRAC9 decoding is covered only by configuration and error tests, as no ATRAC9 encoder is available for a fixture.
- [sceAjmDecMp3ParseFrame](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp) (libSceAjm.native) throws when the original file length is requested (`parse_ofl` non-zero) for a layer other than III, for a CRC-protected frame, or for a VBRI header outside MPEG-1 stereo: shadPS4 looks for the tag after the CRC and after the side information, while LAME and FFmpeg place it without the CRC and VBRI always at byte 36.
- [ATRAC9 gapless segments](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp) (libSceAjm.native) - a run job stops after the gapless total of output samples counted from the start of the segment, leaving the rest of its input unconsumed, and the next run starts the next segment with the decoder as it is and without a second skip. FMOD in PPSA29141 sets a total shorter than a sound's frames and reads the frames after it on later runs; with the output held at the total its stream reader waited for them forever and the last buffer repeated. The console's handling of the frames after the total is not documented; a reset (`SetGaplessDecode` with the reset flag, `ClearContext`, initialization) starts a new segment. An ATRAC9 run job without `RUN_MULTIPLE_FRAMES` decodes its whole input as one with it: FMOD in PPSA29141 puts two run jobs in one batch with the second's input right after the whole of the first's. Whether the console stops after one frame there is not verified; MP3 and Opus run jobs still stop after one frame
- [Tessellation rings](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/TessellationLowering.cpp) of local, hull and domain programs become Vulkan tessellation interfaces and are never written to guest memory; a title that reads the off-chip or factor ring back sees no data.
- [Inner tessellation factors](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvModuleEmitter.cpp) of integer, fractional-even or fractional-odd triangle patches that are NaN tessellate as 1 (fractional-even then raises them to 2, as Vulkan does), as in the D3D11 reference tessellator; the console is not measured.
- [Integer partitioning](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvModuleEmitter.cpp) (`VGT_TF_PARAM.PARTITIONING` 0) is drawn with equal spacing, which [radeonsi programs as `PART_INTEGER`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/mesa-24.0.0/src/gallium/drivers/radeonsi/si_state_shaders.cpp#L539-540), with factors bounded at 64 instead of fractional-odd's 63; how the console rounds a clamped factor up to a whole number of segments is not measured. Power-of-two (1) partitioning throws.
- [Fractional-even partitioning](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvModuleEmitter.cpp) (`VGT_TF_PARAM.PARTITIONING` 3) is drawn with fractional-even spacing, which [radeonsi programs as `PART_FRAC_EVEN`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/mesa-24.0.0/src/gallium/drivers/radeonsi/si_state_shaders.cpp#L536-537), with factors bounded at 64. Vulkan raises every factor that is not culled to at least 2 after `VGT_HOS_MIN/MAX_TESS_LEVEL`; the console's order of the HOS clamp and that minimum is not measured.
- [VGT_TF_PARAM](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - DISTRIBUTION_MODE is taken as scheduling only; the host tessellator decides the order of a patch's triangles.
- [Hull programs](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/InstructionTranslator.cpp) start from the hull launch registers (user data, ring bases, v0, v1); on the console they start with what the local program leaves, and that it leaves them unchanged is not checked.
- [Local programs](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Shaders/ShaderRegistry.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) that the hull header leaves at address 0 are prepared at their first draw, one artifact per tessellation configuration, instead of at registration.
- The [output modifier](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/TranslationContext/OperandAccess.cpp) (`mul:2`, `mul:4`, `div:2`) follows the shader's float mode as measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035): the hardware applies it only with IEEE mode off and output denormals of the result's precision flushed (bit 1 of the `FLOAT_MODE` denormal field clear), and ignores it otherwise. On f32 results it is applied as the hardware does with f32 denormals flushed; a shader without a known float mode is translated with IEEE mode off and f32 denormals flushed, the mode titles use. On f16 and f64 results it is ignored, and it throws in the modes where the hardware applies it. `v_cvt_f32_f64` has an f32 result and follows the f32 rule: `mul:2` is applied at IEEE=0 with f32 denormals flushed and f16/f64 denormals kept or flushed, and ignored at IEEE=1 or with f32 denormals kept, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) in #981. It is applied to the rounded and flushed f32 result; whether the hardware scales before rounding, which differs only near the f32 denormal and overflow boundaries, is not measured, nor are `mul:4` and `div:2` and the mode with f32 output denormals kept and f16/f64 output denormals flushed, which throws.
- The [clamp modifier](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/TranslationContext/OperandAccess.cpp) follows `DX10_CLAMP`, measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035) for f32, f16, packed f16 and f64 results at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1: with `DX10_CLAMP`=1 (and in shaders without a known float mode) a NaN result clamps to +0, including the quieted NaN that `v_pk_min_f16` and `v_pk_max_f16` return for a signaling NaN operand with IEEE=1 (measured on RDNA2 gfx1036 at both `IEEE_MODE` and both `DX10_CLAMP` settings); with `DX10_CLAMP`=0 a NaN result is written unchanged and every other value clamps to [0, 1] as before. f64 results follow the same rule on hardware, but a clamped f64 result with `DX10_CLAMP`=0 throws until it has an execution test. With `DX10_CLAMP`=0 the NaN's bits are those the translation already produces for the instruction without clamp, so where those are left to the driver (f32 and scalar f16 arithmetic) the payload is the driver's, not the hardware's input propagation. `v_dot2_f32_f16` and the integer clamps are unchanged.
- [FP16_OVFL](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/TranslationContext/OperandAccess.cpp) follows the shader's float mode as measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) at IEEE=0 and IEEE=1 with f16 denormals kept and flushed: with `FP16_OVFL`=1 an f16 result that rounds to ±infinity is written as ±0x7bff unless a source operand is infinite, so `v_rcp_f16`, `v_rsq_f16` and `v_log_f16` of ±0 and `v_exp_f16` of 16 or more are clamped while an infinite input passes through, and `v_div_fixup_f16` keeps the infinity it writes for a zero denominator or an infinite numerator and clamps any other infinite quotient, a NaN one included. `v_cvt_pkrtz_f16_f32` rounds toward zero and never overflows. The sources are checked as the f32 values the translation computes with; the rule is applied to `v_fma_mix*_f16` with f32 sources and to `v_interp_p2_f16` without a measurement of infinite f32 sources or parameters. Round modes other than nearest even are not modelled, so the overflow threshold is the nearest-even one (65520).
- [Directed f16 rounding](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/TranslationContext/SubwordPacking.cpp) for `v_cvt_f16_f32`, `v_cvt_f16_i16`, `v_cvt_f16_u16` and `v_ldexp_f16` follows `FLOAT_MODE` bits 3:2 only when f16 denormals are preserved (bits 7:6 = 3), measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036). With f16 denormals flushed (bits 7:6 = 0), or flushed on one side (modes 1 and 2, not measured), the hardware also flushes the f16 result and the directed path does not model that, so those shaders keep round-to-nearest-even. The `clamp` modifier with directed rounding is not measured.
- [Comparison sampling of a color texture](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) (`image_sample_c`/`image_sample_c_lz` on a texture that is not R32 float or R16 unorm) is emulated in the shader, since Vulkan compares only depth formats: the red channel is fetched, compared and, for bilinear filtering, blended (compare, then filter). Only 2D and 2D array views, float/unorm/snorm formats, wrap, clamp-to-edge and clamp-to-border addressing (border red 0 for the black borders, 1 for opaque white) and point or bilinear filtering at one level are implemented, with texel offsets added to the texel indices; LOD biases and clamps are accepted, as they cannot select another level of a single-level sample. Half-border addressing, the border color table, gradients, gathers, other dimensions, anisotropy, mip filtering and TRUNC_COORD with point sampling throw. The array layer is the coordinate rounded half to even and clamped to the layers, and NOT_EQUAL is unordered (true when the reference, the texel or both are NaN) while the other functions are false for NaN, both as measured by adriadam10 on RDNA2 (gfx1035). Under bilinear filtering, taps past a clamp-to-border edge compare the border value and blend with the texel taps, so results near the edge depend on the filter weights. AMD documentation does not say how a color format's reference is handled: it is clamped to [0, 1] for unorm and [-1, 1] for snorm, as Vulkan does for unorm depth, and not clamped for float. Bilinear weights are full float, not the hardware's fixed-point subtexel weights, and TRUNC_COORD, which changes those weights on hardware, is accepted under bilinear filtering without that change
- [Negative color-texture comparison wrap indices](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) use explicit `OpSRem` normalization to implement modulo; the negative-offset point-wrap regression fails with direct `OpSMod` and passes with normalization on Mesa 22.3.6 llvmpipe (LLVM 15.0.6), but negative-index wrap has not been measured on PS5 hardware.
- [libScePlayerInvitationDialog](../../core/libs/prx/libScePlayerInvitationDialog/libScePlayerInvitationDialog.cpp) simulates dialog completion without displaying UI or sending invitations, so `scePlayerInvitationDialogGetResult` reports `USER_CANCELED`; its parameter ABI remains unverified. The 40-byte result layout (error code, result, 32 reserved bytes) is taken from [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/main/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Dialog/PlayerInvitationDialog.cs) and the error codes from the other common dialogs, not confirmed on a title.
- [Converted unorm images](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) (10_11_11_UNORM, read and written through an R32_UINT view): samples, gathers, LOD queries, 16-bit data and a DST_SEL of the missing W channel throw.
- [Packed 16-bit textures](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/TextureFormat.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - `5_6_5`, `1_5_5_5` and `4_4_4_4` are viewed as R5G6B5, A1R5G5B5 and B4G4R4A4 (the 4-bit format every Vulkan device can sample; R4G4B4A4 is optional and lavapipe lacks it) with a swizzle that reads X from the low bits, and a DST_SEL of the missing W of `5_6_5` reads X. Measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035), not on the console: through `image_load` and `image_sample_lz`, X, Y, Z (and W of `1_5_5_5` and `4_4_4_4`) come from the low, middle and high fields, and W of `5_6_5` returns X (the low 5 bits), not 1.
- [Converted unorm image loads](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvImageEmitter.cpp) divide by 2^n - 1 with the buffer format path's `OpFDiv`, which Vulkan allows to be up to 2.5 ULP off, so some drivers are not bit-exact.
- [64-bit LDS atomics](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvMemoryInstructions.cpp) (`ds_*_u64`, `ds_*_b64`, `ds_*_f64`) read and write the two dwords under a workgroup lock stored after the guest LDS, as LDS is a 32-bit array without 64-bit atomics. On RDNA2 (gfx1036) the LDS serialises a 32-bit atomic on a slot's high dword and a 64-bit atomic on that slot: 128 lanes of `ds_add_u32` and 128 lanes of `ds_add_u64` on one slot sum exactly. Only that pair was measured. Every 32-bit LDS atomic of a program that contains a 64-bit LDS atomic therefore takes the same lock, including a 32-bit `flat_atomic_*` whose address lands in the LDS aperture (a 64-bit one takes it already); the flat forms were not measured against a 64-bit atomic either (the other 32-bit LDS atomics, `ds_min/max`, `ds_cmpst`, the float forms, `ds_inc/dec`, `ds_mskor` and `ds_wrxchg`, are extrapolated from `ds_add_u32` and were not measured against a 64-bit atomic); a program without a 64-bit LDS atomic keeps its native 32-bit atomics. `ds_condxchg32_rtn_b64` is two `ds_mskor` halves, each under the lock but not atomic as a pair, so a 64-bit atomic between the two halves is not excluded. Sub-dword writes (`ds_write_b8`, `ds_write_b16` and their `_d16_hi` forms) and plain `ds_read_*`, `ds_write_*`, `ds_append_u32` and `ds_consume_u32` are not taken under the lock, so a plain access racing a 64-bit atomic on the same dwords can see or overwrite one half of it; whether the hardware orders them was not measured. The lock serialises the workgroup's LDS atomics of such a program; the throughput cost was not measured. `VK_KHR_workgroup_memory_explicit_layout` with `shaderSharedInt64Atomics` would make both widths native and is not used. A 64-bit atomic at an address that is 4-byte but not 8-byte aligned faults the wave on gfx1036, as on gfx1035.
- [LDS float atomics](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvMemoryInstructions.cpp) (`ds_min/max/cmpst_f32`, `ds_min/max/cmpst_f64` and their `_rtn` forms) follow the hardware measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) over 14 x 14 operand sets (zeros, ones, infinities, denormals, quiet and signalling NaNs of both signs and with payloads) in IEEE 0 and 1 with every value 0-3 of the type's denormal field: both operands are flushed to a signed zero when bit 0 of the field is clear (0 and 2; 1 and 3 keep them; the f32 field for the f32 forms, the f16/f64 field for the f64 forms; a shader without a known float mode flushes f32 and keeps f64), then a signalling-NaN data operand is stored quieted, else a signalling-NaN old value is stored quieted, else a quiet-NaN old value is replaced by the data, else a quiet-NaN data operand loses, else the ordered compare with -0 below +0 decides, and the flushed value is what is written, also when the old value wins. `cmpst` compares the flushed bits (both zeros equal, NaN never equal) and leaves memory unchanged when they differ. The returned value is always the old value as it was in memory. `IEEE_MODE` has no effect. The same bit rule serves the buffer float atomics (`AtomicFloatBits`). Not measured: a denormal or signalling-NaN `data1` of `cmpst` (stored as given). `ds_add_f32` and `ds_add_rtn_f32` follow RDNA1 (gfx1010, RX 5700 XT), measured with the [hardware oracle](HW_ORACLE.md) template run through the Windows driver's comgr and HIP runtime over 1574 operand pairs (signed zeros, denormals, the smallest normals, infinities, quiet and signalling NaNs with payloads, the largest normals, random pairs near the denormal range and pairs that nearly cancel) in all 16 combinations of the f32 denormal field and the f32 round mode, and at IEEE 1 and DX10 clamp 0: both operands are flushed to a signed zero when bit 0 of the field is clear and the result when bit 1 is clear, the sum is rounded to nearest even whatever the round mode, a NaN data operand is stored quieted, else a NaN old value is stored quieted, `+inf + -inf` stores 0xffc00000, and `IEEE_MODE` and `DX10_CLAMP` have no effect. The sum is the host's `OpFAdd` between those flushes, so where the field keeps f32 denormals the result also depends on the host preserving them, which the module does not request.
- [V# bases off a DWORD boundary](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvMemoryInstructions.cpp) (shader recompiler) - raw accesses use the exact bytes and `s_buffer_load` reads `(base & ~3) + (offset & ~3)`, as measured on gfx1035 (Linux, alignment mode UNALIGNED; the console's mode is unknown). Formatted accesses off their element's alignment and atomics throw.
