- [32-bit bitfield masks](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/IntegerInstructions.cpp) (`S_BFM_B32`, `V_BFM_B32`) use the truncating 32-bit shift specified by the [RDNA 1.0 ISA](https://www.amd.com/content/dam/amd/en/documents/radeon-tech-docs/instruction-set-architectures/rdna-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf), sections 12.1 (opcode 36) and 12.12 (opcode 867). Width and offset use their low five bits. This matches gfx1035 (Linux) for every width/offset pair, with and without upper operand bits set, and both leave SCC unchanged; not measured on the console.
- [Vector buffer offsets that are not a multiple of 4](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvMemoryInstructions.cpp) (shader recompiler) - dword accesses count whole dwords from the V# base, where the hardware reads the exact bytes at base + offset.
- [Min/max S# reduction](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestSamplerResource.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - `FILTER_MODE` 1 and 2 use Vulkan's min and max reduction (the same `SQ_IMG_FILTER_MODE` values RADV programs). Measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035), not on the console: only texels of non-zero weight are reduced, and the weight is taken after rounding to the 8-bit subtexel grid (a sample within 1/512 texel of a centre reduces that texel alone); a driver with other `subTexelPrecisionBits` reduces a different set there. Gathers ignore the mode.
- [Min/max S#](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/GuestSamplerResource.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver, shader recompiler) - throws with anisotropy, a linear mip filter, depth or color-texture compare, or bilinear filtering of an image the recompiler point-samples (sint, converted, depth-bits).
- [FORCE_DEGAMMA](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Sampler.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - an S# that forces sRGB decoding adds nothing to an sRGB texture, as in shadPS4 (PS4). Measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035), not on the console: `8`, `8_8` and `8_8_8_8` sRGB textures sample bit for bit the same with and without the bit (point and bilinear; BC formats not measured). Other textures throw; on the same hardware their UNORM `8`, `8_8` and `8_8_8_8` forms sample bit for bit as the sRGB format (decoded before filtering).
- [Tessellation factor ring](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/State/src/Configuration.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - `sceAgcDriverSetTFRing` records the ring and `sceAgcDriverGetTFRing` reports it. Before a title sets one, it reports the console's default, 0xFF0000000 and 128 KiB, as measured in [PS5_Vulkan's hardware findings](https://github.com/mihawk-99/PS5_Vulkan/blob/fde9e3798c2ec1d8fccc90d5067cd6060490607f/docs/HARDWARE_FINDINGS.md). Nothing is written to the recorded ring: tessellated draws run as Vulkan tessellation stages, which keep their factors on the host, so a title that reads tessellation factors from the ring finds none. A null base or a zero size throws; [ps5-opengl's AGC runtime](https://github.com/blackbearreloaded/ps5-opengl/blob/67c873f985eaefe0a72f713529e5632eaa01139c/src/platform/ps5_agc_native_runtime.c) calls `sceAgcDriverSetTFRing(0, 0)` in its cleanup, and what that does on the console is unknown.
- [Raw `buffer_load_dwordx2`](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvMemoryInstructions.cpp) is one 64-bit access on the hardware (with or without glc), so it never sees half of a `buffer_atomic_swap_x2`. Only a glc load that is 8-byte aligned and in bounds, in a program that has the 64-bit storage view (it uses 64-bit buffer atomics), is read with one 64-bit atomic load; every other dwordx2 load is two dword loads and can tear against a concurrent 64-bit write: loads without glc, and loads in a program without 64-bit buffer atomics while another shader writes the pair with `swap_x2`.
- [Buffer loads without GLC or DLC](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAnalysis.cpp) (shader recompiler) are not coherent in a program that has GLC or DLC buffer accesses and no `buffer_gl0_inv`, `buffer_gl1_inv`, `s_gl1_inv` or `s_dcache_inv`: only its GLC and DLC loads, stores and atomics use the `Coherent` buffer view, so a plain load can return a value cached before another work-group's write. This follows the [LLVM AMDGPU memory model for GFX10](https://llvm.org/docs/AMDGPUUsage.html#memory-model-gfx10-gfx11), in which a load-acquire is a GLC DLC load followed by `buffer_gl1_inv` and `buffer_gl0_inv`; it is not measured on the console or on RDNA hardware.
- [DS src2 operations](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/MemoryInstructions/SharedMemoryInstructions.cpp) (`ds_*_src2_*`, GFX10.1 only) read `DS[B]`, with `B = A + 4 * sext(offset1[6:0]:offset0)`, and then update `DS[A]` with the matching atomic, so the read of `B` is not atomic with the update of `A`. The 64-bit forms take the same dword offset; a `B` that is not 8-byte aligned reads as 0 and an `A` that is not 8-byte aligned writes nothing, as measured with the alignment mode the Windows driver sets. The f32 and f64 min and max forms follow the [LDS float atomics](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvMemoryInstructions.cpp) entry with `DS[B]` as the data operand. Measured on RDNA1 (gfx1010, RX 5700 XT) with the [hardware oracle](HW_ORACLE.md) template run through the Windows driver's comgr and HIP runtime: 78 single-lane cases of the 32-bit integer forms, 624 cases of the 64-bit integer forms with random, equal and adjacent operands, and the f32 and f64 min and max forms against plain `ds_min/max_f32/f64` over 14 x 14 operand sets in IEEE 0 and 1 with denormals flushed and kept, which agreed on every row and follow that entry's rule. The active lanes of a wave run one after another in lane order and a lane's read of `B` sees the writes of lower lanes, as measured on gfx1010 with chains of eight lanes whose `B` is the previous lane's `A` (32-bit add and write, 64-bit add, the same `A` in four lanes, and in wave64 a chain across lanes 31 and 32); the translation runs the active lanes of each host subgroup one at a time with `OpGroupNonUniformElect` and a workgroup memory barrier between lanes, and a wave64 program on a 32-lane host, whose invocations each hold lanes n and n + 32 and run the two halves one after the other, runs lanes 0-31 before 32-63; a wave64 program kept at one lane per invocation on a host whose subgroups are narrower than 64 (`APS5_SINGLE_LANE` on such a host) orders lanes only within each host subgroup, as every other subgroup operation there. `ds_add_src2_f32` throws: on gfx1010 it equals `ds_add_f32`, which flushes both operands to a signed zero when the f32 denormal field flushes, and that is not modelled. The form that takes the offset from the address (`offset1[7]` set) and GDS throw: on gfx1010 that form writes nothing when `A[16]` or `A[17]` is set and otherwise updates `A[15:0]` with a `B` that no documented formula gives.
- [`ds_ordered_count`](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/MemoryInstructions/SharedMemoryInstructions.cpp) (GDS only) reads its operand fields as LLVM encodes them for `llvm.amdgcn.ds.ordered.add` ([SIISelLowering.cpp](https://github.com/llvm/llvm-project/blob/llvmorg-19.1.0/llvm/lib/Target/AMDGPU/SIISelLowering.cpp#L8844-L8894)): `offset0` is the counter's byte offset (index times 4) and the whole GDS address, `offset1` bit 0 wave release, bit 1 wave done, bits 2-3 the shader type, bit 4 swap instead of add and bits 6-7 the dword count less one. `M0` holds the ordered wave ID, not a GDS address as for `ds_append` (radeonsi passes `TG_SIZE` bits 6-17 as the intrinsic's pointer in [si_compute_prim_discard.c](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/mesa-20.3.0/src/gallium/drivers/radeonsi/si_compute_prim_discard.c) and `gs_tg_info` bits 0-11 in [gfx10_shader_ngg.c](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/mesa-21.3.0/src/gallium/drivers/radeonsi/gfx10_shader_ngg.c), with "The GDS address is always 0 with ordered append"); it only orders the waves, which is not modeled, so it is ignored. Each wave adds the value of its first active lane to the counter with one device-scope atomic and every active lane gets the counter's previous value (the `ORDERED_APPEND_ENBL` dispatch modifier is accepted for it). The hardware also serializes the waves of a dispatch in launch order, which is not modeled: the waves add in any order, so counters a title reads back as ordered offsets give the same totals but may hand out ranges in another order. Which lane's value is added is not documented; the first active lane is assumed and not measured on hardware. Swap, counts of more than one dword and LDS forms throw. Covered by [GdsAppendConsume.cpp](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/tests/execution/GdsAppendConsume.cpp).
- [LDS in pixel, vertex-path and fetch programs](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvAnalysis.cpp) (shader recompiler) - is an array private to each host invocation, in `Function` storage, as only compute and mesh programs get workgroup memory; on the console one LDS region is shared by the wave (RDNA 2 ISA sections 3.6.6 and 10.4), so a lane does not see another lane's LDS writes. Sub-dword writes (`ds_write_b8`, `ds_write_b16` and their `_d16_hi` forms), the 32-bit LDS atomics, `ds_append` and `ds_consume` load, update and store the invocation's own dword without an atomic, as Vulkan allows atomics only on `Uniform`, `Workgroup`, `Image`, `StorageBuffer`, `PhysicalStorageBuffer` and `TaskPayloadWorkgroupEXT` pointers (VUID-StandaloneSpirv-None-04686). `ds_append` and `ds_consume` update only the first active lane's copy and return its old value to every lane, so a second append after the first active lane changes, or a read of the counter by another lane, differs from the hardware. Not measured on hardware.
- [VALU lane masks written to an SGPR or VCC](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/TranslationContext/ExecMask.cpp) (shader recompiler) - the carry and borrow masks of `v_add_co_u32`, `v_sub_co_u32`, `v_subrev_co_u32` and their `_ci` forms, the carry of `v_mad_u64_u32` and `v_mad_i64_i32`, and the flag of `v_div_scale_f32` and `v_div_scale_f64` have 0 in the bits of lanes that EXEC disables, as `v_cmp` results do. The [RDNA 4 ISA guide](https://gpuopen.com/download/rdna4-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) states this for carry-outs (section 7.2.3, page 72). The RDNA 2 guide does not say it in words, and no guide says it for `v_mad_*64` or `v_div_scale`. Not measured on gfx1013 or another RDNA 2 GPU, in particular whether a disabled lane's bit that was 1 before the instruction is cleared or kept; [ValuMaskInactiveLanes.cpp](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/tests/execution/ValuMaskInactiveLanes.cpp) expects it cleared
- [32_AR color exports](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvModuleEmitter.cpp) (shader recompiler) - with `SPI_SHADER_COL_FORMAT` 3 the red value is export channel 0 and the alpha is export channel 1, the GFX10 layout of Mesa's [`emit_ps_color_export`](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/3e32b0e1f132231b7a972c1b0b5b06c7e16cf8b9/src/amd/common/nir/ac_nir_lower_ps_late.c#L400-L411), ACO and radeonsi in Mesa 22.0 ([aco_instruction_selection.cpp](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/a82920af6efa4a7a66919885c6477fbd8fdc7b9d/src/amd/compiler/aco_instruction_selection.cpp#L11087-L11096), [si_shader_llvm_ps.c](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/a82920af6efa4a7a66919885c6477fbd8fdc7b9d/src/gallium/drivers/radeonsi/si_shader_llvm_ps.c#L323-L333)) and AMD's [LLPC](https://github.com/GPUOpen-Drivers/llpc/blob/40cb8d95ad8d6f7f1652e3fd47d39667594cce08/lgc/lowering/FragmentColorExport.cpp#L157-L168); before GFX10 the alpha was channel 3. Channels 2 and 3, which none of these compilers enables, are ignored; green and blue are 0 and the alpha of an export without channel 1 is 1.0, the defaults of the other formats, which no source gives. Not measured on hardware, neither on gfx1013 nor on a PC RDNA 2 GPU; pinned in [Pixel32ArExport.cpp](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/tests/execution/Pixel32ArExport.cpp)
- [SDWA source selects on `v_cvt_f32_ubyte1`, `v_cvt_f32_ubyte2` and `v_cvt_f32_ubyte3`](../../core/shader/recompiler/RdnaDecoder/src/RdnaVectorOpDecoder.cpp) (shader recompiler) - byte N is taken from source 0 after `SRC0_SEL` and `SRC0_SEXT` select and extend it, since SDWA supplies source 0 (RDNA 2 ISA guide 6.8 and 13.3.7) and the opcode reads `S0.u[8N+7:8N]` (12.8). Not measured on hardware.
- [Scalar buffer descriptors](../../core/shader/recompiler/Optimization/src/ResourceMaterializer.cpp) use only the base address, stride and record count for resources accessed exclusively by scalar loads; their fourth word is normalized to zero and bit 30 of the second word (reserved) is cleared, following AMD RDNA2 ISA section 7.2.2. Vector and mixed accesses retain descriptor validation. Native Vulkan readbacks cover ignored fourth-word fields on Windows/NVIDIA; their console behavior has not been measured.
- [wcsrtombs_s](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Multibyte.cpp) (libc) follows the console's `libc.prx`, but a runtime-constraint violation only returns its error: the default handler there writes the message and a newline to `stderr` before returning, which is not done here. Conversion uses the C locale of `wcrtomb` (`MB_CUR_MAX` 1), so the path where a multibyte character does not fit the remaining space is not reached
- [The narrow printf family](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Formatting.cpp) (libc) converts 16-bit guest wide arguments to UTF-8 on both platforms, independently of the host locale. It does not use the guest's single-byte `wcrtomb`; the console's treatment of invalid UTF-16 and locale-specific encoding remains unverified. Linux retains host formatting for formats without wide arguments, so a narrow `%p` there still follows the host (glibc prints a null pointer as `(nil)`, where FreeBSD prints `0x0`); positional arguments, the `'` flag and `%m` throw when a format requires the guest formatter
- [collate<char> and collate<wchar_t> _Getcat](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/LocaleSupport.cpp) (libc) build the facet for the C locale only and throw for any other locale name, the wide entry point following the narrow one it sits next to. The wide facet carries its own vtable, whose `compare`, `transform` and `hash` take the guest's 16-bit characters, so the length of a range is a count of characters: `compare` orders by 16-bit value (the C locale, as Dinkumware's `char_traits<wchar_t>::compare` does), `transform` copies the characters into the result as the guest's bytes and sizes the result in bytes (two per character, a 16-bit terminator, the 7 characters that still fit next to it and `length * 2 | 15` plus two bytes of storage beyond that), and `hash` mixes one whole character per round, so wide text below 0x100 hashes like its narrow spelling and a character above 0xff hashes as one value instead of the two bytes it holds. The console's `libc.prx` exports both `_Getcat` (NIDs BSVJqITGCyI and 2wz4rthdiy8) with identical 0x19d-byte bodies, believed to build the same 0x20-byte facet; the console bodies themselves are unreadable (obfuscated dynsym, and the text of the relinked `libc.prx` is zeroed), so the wide entries follow the narrow algorithm and the C locale instead of the console's wide code, and no console run checks the two. The console's `libc.prx` stores pointers to its collation state tables at offsets 0x10 and 0x18 of the facet; ours leaves them null, as only the facet's own functions read them. `do_transform` throws for input with embedded null characters, which the console drops depending on the output buffer size. The narrow result capacity (`length | 15`) and the over-aligned storage of results of 4080 characters or more follow the console's `libc.prx` code and are not checked on hardware. No module of the PPSA59322, PPSA76677 or PPSA97358 dumps in this tree imports either `_Getcat`, so neither has been exercised by a title.
- [The scanf family](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Formatting.cpp) (libc) hands `%i` to glibc on Linux, which since 2.38 reads the C23 `0b`/`0B` binary prefix when the library is built as GNU C++: `sscanf("0b101", "%i", &value)` stores 5 where C17 stores 0 and stops at the `b`. The strtol family stops at the prefix as C17 does
- [The scanf family](../../core/libs/prx/libc/include/WindowsScanning.hpp) (libc) on Windows returns 0, as C17 and FreeBSD do, when input fails after only suppressed conversions have completed (`sscanf("12", "%*d%d", &value)`). Linux hands the format to glibc, which returns `EOF` there. On Windows the host stops a `%x`, `%X`, `%i` or `%p` after the `x` of a `0x` that no hex digit follows (or that the width cuts off), and a float conversion (`%e`, `%f`, `%g`, `%a`) after the `e` of an exponent with no digits (`1e`), where C17 and FreeBSD stop before the `x` or the `e`
- [socketpair](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Udp.cpp) (libkernel) - the pair is the host's `AF_UNIX` pair on Linux and, on Windows, a loopback TCP connection for `SOCK_STREAM` or two connected UDP sockets for `SOCK_DGRAM`; `SOCK_SEQPACKET` throws on Windows. A datagram sent to a full receiver gets `ENOBUFS` on the console, waits or gets `EAGAIN` on Linux and is dropped on Windows, and on Windows a datagram `recv` after the peer closed reports `ECONNRESET` instead of the console's `ENOTCONN`, and a stream `send` after the peer closed reports `ECONNABORTED` instead of the console's `EPIPE`. `read` and `write` on a pair end go to the host like on every guest socket. `getpeername` on a pair end succeeds after the peer closed, where the console returns `ENOTCONN`, and `connect`, `bind` and `sendto` with an address on a pair end report an address-family error instead of the console's `EISCONN`. On Windows `poll` on a stream end reports `POLLHUP` without `POLLIN` after the peer's `shutdown(SHUT_WR)` or `close`, where the console reports `POLLIN`, with `POLLHUP` only after `close`
- [UDP recv/recvfrom](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Udp.cpp) and [sceNetRecv/sceNetRecvfrom](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNet/Export.cpp) translate Windows WSAEMSGSIZE into the copied byte count for datagram sockets, including MSG_PEEK, and retain the sender address. This follows FreeBSD 11.4 recv(2) and Winsock's documented truncated-datagram behavior; console behavior and existing title impact are unverified.
- [read, write and vector I/O](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/Stdio.cpp) (libkernel) on a guest socket use `recv` and `send` without flags; `sceKernelReadv` and `sceKernelWritev` gather or scatter through one socket operation, preserving stream short reads and datagram boundaries. The positioned `pread`, `pwrite`, `preadv` and `pwritev` forms, and their `sceKernel` forms, return `ESPIPE` for an open guest socket and `EBADF` for a closed one. Guest socket descriptors start at `0x10000000` and are allocated upward, so the descriptor range selects the guest socket table or the host descriptor table. `EPIPE` from a write raises the host's `SIGPIPE` on Linux, which the guest can neither ignore nor suppress: `signal(SIGPIPE, SIG_IGN)` and `sigaction` record the disposition, as they do for every number from 1 to 128, but only the six signals with host equivalents (2, 4, 6, 8, 11 and 15) take effect on the host or are delivered, so the host `SIGPIPE` is not stopped; `SO_NOSIGPIPE` is not an option here, and raises nothing on Windows; a `write` longer than `INT_MAX` bytes is `EMSGSIZE` where the console sends it. AIO on a guest socket still goes to the host, which has no such descriptor
- [poll](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Udp.cpp) (libkernel) waits on guest sockets and pipes, through `WSAPoll` on Windows and `poll` on Linux; the `pollfd` layout and flag values are FreeBSD's. A libSceNet socket joins the same host wait through the native socket libSceNet lends to libkernel. `WSAPoll` rejects `POLLPRI`, so on Windows a socket's `POLLPRI` is asked as `POLLRDBAND` and `POLLIN` as `POLLRDNORM`; Winsock reports urgent data until it is read with `MSG_OOB`, where FreeBSD 11.4's `sopoll_generic` stops once the normal stream passes the mark. Guest `send` and `recv` refuse `MSG_OOB` with `EOPNOTSUPP`, so urgent data can only come from an outside peer and Windows keeps reporting `POLLPRI` after the stream passes the mark. A pipe follows FreeBSD's `pipe_poll`: the read end is readable when data is buffered, either end reports `POLLHUP` once the other end is closed, with the requested `POLLIN` and `POLLRDNORM` bits and without `POLLOUT`, which `pollscan` clears, and the write end is writable when `PIPE_BUF` (512) bytes are free on Windows, or when the host kernel says so on Linux. `POLLOUT` on a read end throws: FreeBSD opens both ends for reading and writing and reports it writable, and host pipes are one-way. Windows pipes cannot be waited on, so while a pipe is polled `poll` checks it with `NtQueryInformationFile` and waits on the sockets in slices of 1 ms. A closed descriptor reports `POLLNVAL`, and an open descriptor that is neither a guest socket, a libSceNet socket nor a pipe (files, stdio) throws, as do event bits other than `POLLIN`, `POLLPRI`, `POLLOUT`, `POLLRDNORM`, `POLLRDBAND` and `POLLWRBAND` (the `POLLERR`, `POLLHUP` and `POLLNVAL` bits are ignored in `events`), an infinite timeout with no socket or pipe to wait on. A closed socket descriptor reports `POLLNVAL`; the console's limit on the descriptor count is unknown and not checked
- [msync](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/PosixMemory.cpp) (libkernel) follows FreeBSD 11's `kern_msync` and `vm_map_sync`, not measured on the console: the address is rounded down to the page, a range that wraps is `EINVAL`, as is `MS_ASYNC` with `MS_INVALIDATE`, and a range whose start or any page is not mapped (as `sceKernelVirtualQuery` reports it, reservations included) is `ENOMEM`; a page unmapped from inside a larger mapping still counts as mapped there, since `sceKernelVirtualQuery` keeps reporting the whole mapping. The guest has no file mappings, so a successful call writes nothing back. `MS_INVALIDATE` on a mapped range throws: FreeBSD returns `EBUSY` when the range holds `mlock`ed pages, and locked pages are not tracked
- [sceKernelMprotect, sceKernelMtypeprotect](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/DirectMemory.cpp) (libkernel) over a range with a gap that is not registered in the allocation tracker protect the registered parts and skip the gap, and return 0, as FreeBSD 11.4's [`vm_map_protect`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/release/11.4.0/sys/vm/vm_map.c) does (it has no check for holes); a range whose start is unmapped (also when later pages are mapped) returns `SCE_KERNEL_ERROR_EINVAL` (`mprotect`: `EINVAL`) because an existing test pins that for an unmapped start; FreeBSD answers 0 there too (`vm_map_protect` takes the next entry), so this errno is not taken from the source and was not changed. `sceKernelMprotect` threw for a gap before, which ended the title. Every part is checked for a GPU or image pin before the first one is protected; the parts are then protected one after the other, so a host protection failure in a later part leaves the earlier parts changed (FreeBSD's `vm_map_protect` cannot fail in its second pass). The behaviour on the console is not known. None of this was measured on a console.
- [sceKernelMprotect protection bits](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/Export.cpp) (libkernel) - bits outside `0x3F7` are dropped instead of refused, as FreeBSD 11's [`sys_mprotect`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/34bd34750187bae02a0086402c78c031c59dd8b3/sys/vm/vm_mmap.c#L606) does with `VM_PROT_ALL`. Onimusha: Way of the Sword (PPSA27836 01.000.001) maps direct memory with `0xf2` and then protects it with `0xfffffff2`. Not measured on a console; the map calls and `sceKernelMtypeprotect` still refuse undefined bits
- [Thread exit destructors](../../core/libs/prx/libSceLibcInternal/Export.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal) run when a libkernel thread finishes; on the main or a host thread only `_sceLibcInternalThreadDtors` runs them, as `exit` does, else they are dropped
- [sceFontOpenFontSet](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFont/src/SystemFont.cpp) (libSceFont) opens only the primary file of a system font set. Characters the console takes from the set's other files (Hangul, Thai and Arabic in Japanese and Chinese sets, Arabic in European sets) are reported as unsupported glyphs. The console file names are those of the PS4 firmware; the names of the PS5-only JG2 sets (`0x1A......`) are unknown, so they use the SSTJpPro files. When a console file is missing, an openly licensed substitute (Noto Sans, Noto Sans Mono, Noto Sans Thai, Noto Sans CJK) is loaded instead. It is not the system font: its metrics differ, so text width, line height and wrapping differ from the console. System font faces are loaded by FreeType in host memory, not in the font memory the title passes to the library: FreeType needs about 1 MiB per CJK face, which the title's font memory is not sized for.
- [sceFontGetCharGlyphMetrics, sceFontGetRenderCharGlyphMetrics](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFont/src/Layout.cpp) (libSceFont) report the vertical metrics the way the [FreeType driver](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFontFt/src/Driver.cpp) reports them for a rendered glyph, not confirmed on a title: FreeType's vertical advance, and for a face without vertical metrics a bearing of half the horizontal advance left of the horizontal bearing and of half the advance left over by the glyph height.
- [Font writing lines](../../core/libs/prx/libSceFont/src/WritingLine.cpp) (libSceFont) - `sceFontWritingLineWritesOrder`, `sceFontWritingLineRefersRenderStep`, `sceFontWritingLineGetRenderMetrics`, `sceFontWritingLineGetOrderingSpace` and `sceFontWritingLineClear` follow the PS4 11.00 `libSceFont.sprx` (firmware module, read from its disassembly and compared call for call on random sequences): runs are linked in visual order by embedding level, the space at each spaced join is summed for the line, steps walk the visual order from the left (form `0x12`) or from the right (form `0x11`) and the render metrics grow as steps are consumed. The PS5 library was not measured. Not from that module: form `0x10` is accepted and treated as left to right and a detail with id `0x0FD5` is accepted (the PS4 library rejects both); the memory argument of `sceFontCreateWritingLine` and the rest of the detail are not read (the PS4 library validates its library object there); an unknown writing form, another detail id, a null line pointer on creation, unknown and null handles (except in `sceFontWritingLineRefersRenderStep`, which returns null for a null line) and a null output of `sceFontWritingLineGetRenderMetrics` throw instead of returning the PS4 error codes, which are not confirmed for the PS5; `sceFontDestroyWritingLine` was not compared. Lines are allocated on the host, not in the font memory the title passes to the library: FreeType needs about 1 MiB per CJK face, which the title's font memory is not sized for.
- [s_swappc_b64](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/ControlFlowInstructions.cpp) writes the link pair with the same model as `s_getpc_b64`: the guest return address `pc + 4` as a byte offset over `GetShaderBase`. Any other use of the link pair (arithmetic, hash, store) throws, apart from the overwrites, SOPC compares and shared links that [static scalar calls](../../core/shader/recompiler/ControlFlow/src/GraphBuilder.cpp) allow; constant-offset folding over the link is not modeled. The special encoding `s_swappc_b64` with SDST 125 still decodes as `s_setpc_b64`, which matches the hardware: measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) in wave32 and wave64, it branches to the source pair and leaves a marker SGPR pair untouched, and with an SGPR destination the link is `PC + 4`.
- The [s_swappc_b64 fetch-shader call at vertex/local/tessellation/mesh entry](../../core/shader/recompiler/ControlFlow/src/GraphBuilder.cpp) assumes the called fetch-shader blob ends in `s_setpc_b64` to the link pair (the GNM fetch-shader shape shadPS4's `EmitFetch` copies); the blob is not part of the shader program, so its return closes the pairing by assumption and any in-program reader of the link pair throws. Attribute fetches are inlined from the request's parsed fetch-shader plan through the same `GetAttribute` path as the embedded fetch analyzer, honoring the buffer resource `dst_sel`; an attribute writing more than four registers, or an entry call without a parsed plan, throws. The vertex/instance offset accumulator inside the fetch blob is not replayed for external blobs.
- The scalar aperture sources [src_shared_base, src_shared_limit, src_private_base, src_private_limit](../../core/shader/recompiler/RdnaDecoder/src/RdnaInstructionDecoder.cpp) (core/shader/recompiler, scalar source codes 235 to 238) read as the 64-bit apertures the flat routing uses: a base is the aperture's high dword (`SharedApertureHigh`, `PrivateApertureHigh`) over a low dword of 0, a limit is the same high dword over 0xffffffff. A 64-bit read (`s_mov_b64`, a 64-bit VALU source, a wave64 lane mask) gets the whole value and a 32-bit read gets the low dword. This follows LLVM's `SIRegisterInfo.td` (aperture registers are 64-bit, their low 32 bits are always 0 for a base and -1 for a limit) and `getSegmentAperture` (`AMDGPULegalizerInfo.cpp`, `SIISelLowering.cpp`: a 64-bit read whose high half is the aperture), the KFD's `MAKE_LDS_APP_LIMIT` (limit = base | 0xffffffff) and the gfx1030 and gfx1035 readouts on [#1190](https://github.com/boykopovar/AnyPS5/pull/1190) and [#3803](https://github.com/boykopovar/AnyPS5/issues/3803). Not measured on a console: the PS5's high dwords are the inferred values of the flat aperture bases entry, and the wave is taken to be in 64-bit address mode (PTR32 clear)
- [VertexBufferReadSize](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/include/VertexInput.hpp) and the [vertex staging copy](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/Draw.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - a vertex fetch whose index is at or past the descriptor's `num_records` no longer throws for `OOB_SELECT` 0 and 1. The copy still spans the whole fetch, but the bytes from `num_records * stride` onward read as zeros, so the out-of-range vertex is `(0, 0, 0, 0)`. Measured with `buffer_load_format_xyzw` (`idxen`) through the [hardware oracle](HW_ORACLE.md) on RDNA2 gfx1036 for PPSA01325's word 3 `0x0004dfac` (`OOB_SELECT` 0) in #2241: indices 3, 4 and 5 over 3 records each return `00000000 00000000 00000000 00000000`, W included, unlike Vulkan `robustBufferAccess`, which returns `(0, 0, 0, 1)`. The zeros come from the record check, not from memory: stored values placed past the records are ignored, and a `num_records` 6 control returns them. `RESOURCE_LEVEL` 1, `OOB_SELECT` 1 and FORMAT 74 `32_32_32_FLOAT` give the same zeros, in wave32 and wave64; gfx1013 (the console) is not measured. `OOB_SELECT` 2 returns the stored values past the records and `OOB_SELECT` 3 compares `num_records` against the byte offset, both measured on the same setup in #2241; the two keep throwing past `num_records` here, since each needs its own rule. Zero-stride descriptors (`stride` 0) keep their separate byte-count check and are not covered here. Each out-of-range fetch is copied alone and outside the draw-snapshot cache, which is neither read nor written for it: a reused snapshot would carry another draw's bytes into the zero region, and a stored zero-padded copy would hand zeros to a later in-range draw at the same address. The valid prefix is still flushed for GPU writes before it is read.
- [sceImeKeyboardGetResourceId](../../core/libs/prx/libSceIme/Export.cpp) (libSceIme) reports that no USB keyboard is connected (`0x80bc0004`, as [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4) does) because IME keyboard events are not delivered to the handler, although [libSceKeyboard](../../core/libs/prx/libSceKeyboard/src/keyboard_impl.cpp) exposes the host keyboard
- [Player review dialog](../../core/libs/prx/libSceCdlgPlayerReview/Export.cpp) (libSceCdlgPlayerReview) shows nothing: `scePlayerReviewDialogOpen` finishes at once and the result reports that the user canceled, as `sceWebBrowserDialogOpen` does
- [NP reachability callbacks](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNpManager/Export.cpp) retain one callback and its user data; duplicate registration and unregistering an absent callback return the errors used by [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/945dbc3cc3eee80ac3e053b438502ed936fa6bb2/src/core/libraries/np/np_manager.cpp). No callback is delivered because the existing NP model remains signed out with reachability unavailable.
- [Running application ID](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSystemService/Export.cpp) (libSceSystemService) - `sceSystemServiceGetAppIdOfRunningBigApp` reports 1 as the running title's app ID. The value is AnyPS5's own: the console's shell assigns app IDs and their values are not known here, so other calls that take an app ID must use the same one. `sceSystemServiceKillApp` with that ID and the arguments -1, 0 and 0, which [SwanStationPS5](https://github.com/darkxex/SwanStationPS5/blob/4cdc981d1a64ffb7f2af73f7a27a9e91a3cb5eb4/src/main.c#L1050-L1058) passes to close itself and [ps5-payload-dev's hbldr](https://github.com/ps5-payload-dev/websrv/blob/46374edee6627885abac717de24da7a1d73af6f1/src/ps5/hbldr.c#L425-L435) passes to close the running game, ends the title as its own `exit(0)` would: the guest's shutdown and `atexit` handlers run and the process exits with status 0. On the console the call returns and the shell then ends the title, and whether those handlers run there is unknown; here there is no shell, so the call does not return. Another app ID or other arguments throw.
- [sceNpCheckNpAvailability](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNpManager/Export.cpp) (libSceNpManager) - the `(req_id, online_id)` signature and the errors follow [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/0fe263a4760dfbfa973366890061749b4af0de97/src/core/libraries/np/np_manager.cpp) with no user signed in to PSN: a null online id is `SCE_NP_ERROR_INVALID_ARGUMENT`, and any other online id matches no user, `SCE_NP_ERROR_USER_NOT_FOUND`; the request id is not checked, as in the other NpManager requests
- [Temporary app-content storage](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAppContent/Export.cpp) tracks one `/temp0` mount, preserves its files on unmount, and rejects new path resolutions until remount. Mount, format, space-query and unmount errors follow [fpPS4](https://github.com/red-prig/fpPS4/blob/04cefd43e6fddd1ab033e7980cd356d14c964905/sys/sys_path.pas); the pointer ABI is also declared by [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/main/src/SharpProspero/Interop/AppContent/AppContent.cs). Existing open host descriptors remain usable after unmount; console behaviour with open files is unverified. Host filesystem failures still throw.
- [Guest path resolution](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/General.cpp) falls back to ASCII case-insensitive component lookup, following [shadPS4's host filesystem](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/945dbc3cc3eee80ac3e053b438502ed936fa6bb2/src/core/file_sys/backends/host_fs.cpp). PPSA03747 requests `Skyrim.INI`, `Data/Video` and lowercase archive/plugin names that differ from the dump's filenames. Exact host names take precedence; multiple fallback matches throw instead of selecting an arbitrary file. Existing parent names are resolved when creating a new file, and guest mount aliases use the same ASCII comparison. Results are not cached, so renames and newly created files remain visible. Unicode case folding and PS5 behaviour for case-colliding host entries are unverified; host roots are used as supplied.
- [localtime, mktime](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Time.cpp) (libc) on Windows convert through the CRT's `localtime_s` and `mktime`, which accept about 1970 to January 3001 UTC and apply the CRT's daylight-saving rule; outside that range `localtime` returns null and `mktime` returns -1, where the console converts. `gmtime` converts every time whose year fits an `int` on both hosts.
- [strftime](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Time.cpp) (libc) expands FreeBSD's `%k`, `%l`, `%s`, `%v` and `%+` itself, ignoring a leading `E` or `O` as FreeBSD does, and passes every other conversion to the host's `strftime`. On Windows the conversions the CRT lacks (`%P` and the `%-`, `%_` and `%0` padding flags) still produce nothing, and the padding flags are not supported on the five new conversions at all (`%-k`, `%_k` and `%0k` go to the host), an unknown conversion, a doubled `E` or `O` modifier (`%EE`) or a trailing lone `%` returns 0 where FreeBSD prints `%` and the character, and a standalone `%z` or `%Z` uses the host's zone instead of the broken-down time's `tm_zone`. `%s` follows FreeBSD 9 and 11 and is the local-time `mktime` of the broken-down time (current FreeBSD uses `tm_gmtoff`), which is -1 on Windows outside the range above. `%+` is the C locale's `%a %b %e %H:%M:%S %Z %Y` with `%Z` taken from `tm_zone`, or from `tzname[tm_isdst != 0]` when it is null.
- [LINE_STIPPLE_TEX](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/InstructionTranslator.cpp) (recompiler) reads as 0: line stipple is not emulated. RADV enables the input only as the required pixel input of a shader that reads no interpolants, because it initializes the fewest VGPRs ([Mesa](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/main/src/amd/vulkan/radv_shader.c) `radv_compute_spi_ps_input`), and never reads it
- [Draw snapshot buffer descriptors](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderResources.cpp) rewrite each affected array from element zero, preserving unchanged descriptors. MoltenVK 1.4.2 writes auxiliary buffer lengths at index `i` instead of `start + i` for partial array updates ([MVKDescriptorSet.mm, BufferAuxSize](https://github.com/KhronosGroup/MoltenVK/blob/v1.4.2/MoltenVK/MoltenVK/GPUObjects/MVKDescriptorSet.mm#L1051)). Per-element writes therefore let one buffer length replace another even when Vulkan buffer handles and ranges are correct.
- [Static scalar calls](../../core/shader/recompiler/ControlFlow/src/GraphBuilder.cpp) (`s_call_b64` and `s_swappc_b64`) require an ordinary even-aligned SGPR link pair (s0:s1 through s104:s105) and a later `s_setpc_b64` return reading that exact pair. A pair serves one call, or several calls whose regions are disjoint, each closed by the first return reading the pair after its target. Outside every region of its calls the pair is free to use wherever no return address can reach: a dataflow over the decoded instructions follows each dword of the pair from the calls that write it until a scalar load, SALU or VALU result is known to overwrite it (a VALU mask counts as one SGPR unless the request says wave64), and an instruction there that may read a dword still holding a return address throws; SOPC compares are exempt, as they only set SCC. Regions must nest or be disjoint; recursion, other shared links, escaping link values (including overlapping tuples and resource descriptors), invalid targets, interleaved regions, cross-region branches or fall-through, relative SGPR reads and dynamic `s_setpc_b64` in a program with calls throw. The analysis checks all decoded instructions, including unreachable code. Arbitrary indirect calls and general call frames are not implemented.
- [Relative vector addressing](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/ControlFlowInstructions.cpp) (`v_movrels_b32`, `v_movreld_b32`, `v_movrelsd_b32`, `v_movrelsd_2_b32`, `v_swaprel_b32`) keeps every vector register from the lowest base register the program addresses relatively up to the last register the program names in zero-initialised function-scope storage per invocation (`LoadVectorWindow`/`StoreVectorWindow`, one element per register), so an indexed access is one load or store instead of a compare-and-select chain over the window. [`IndexedWindowSlots`](../../core/shader/recompiler/SpirvBackend/src/SpirvMemory/SpirvModuleSetup.cpp) bounds each relative index through the IR (bit-field extracts, masks, shifts, adds, selects, phis, `ReadFirstLane` and the window stores that reach a load); the storage is an array of the slots below the largest bound that is under half the window, and one function variable per slot after it. A constant index uses the array element or the slot's variable; a relative access whose index has no such bound, or lands past the array at run time, indexes the array below its size and otherwise branches through an `OpSwitch` over the slot variables, so the bound only decides speed. When no index has a bound under half the window, the whole window is the array, as with `APS5_NO_WINDOW_NARROWING=1` (a recompiler switch in the shader cache key). Measured on an NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3060 (driver 615.78.08, Linux) with a compute program whose 167-register window an ALU loop reads and writes in full and whose relative accesses take `m0 = s8 & 3`: a 2048-group dispatch took 70.2 ms in wave32 and 123.3 ms in wave64 with the whole window in the array (as before narrowing) and 1.9-2.1 ms and 7.8 ms narrowed; with a second relative access whose index has no bound and lands past the array at run time (index 50 or 166), 113 ms and 5.8-6.0 ms in wave32. In the PPSA08427 (Killing Floor 3) hub it made no measurable difference to presents or to the GPU time of the eight most expensive programs. Whether the host compiler keeps the array and the slot variables in registers or spills them to scratch is not measured. A register in the window the program never wrote reads 0, as an unwritten SSA register does; the console's value is undefined. M0 is unsigned: an index at or past the window reads `v0` and drops its write, and `v_swaprel_b32` writes neither side when either is out of range, as measured on RDNA2 (gfx1035, `agc_driver_relative_indexing`). The window ends at the last register the program names rather than at the shader's VGPR allocation, which the recompiler does not receive, so a relative write past the named registers but inside the allocation is dropped where the hardware keeps it for a later relative read. Killzone: Liberation's PSP vertex program (PPSA11142, 3176 ISA words, 104 relative accesses over 154 registers) went from 649,515 to 392,333 SPIR-V words with this. Pixel programs whose relative span contains the packed ancillary input instead keep that span in SSA and implement indexed operations with compare/select chains. This keeps ancillary definitions visible to field lowering and masked-write proofs; storing the unmodelled whole word in a private array would require composing unknown bits. A fold after masked-select elimination lowers fields whose consumers become visible only after redundant copies are removed. A whole-word use that the masked-select pass keeps gets the composed word described under the packed pixel ancillary word. Both representations include implicit entry inputs in the inferred register limit and retain unsigned M0, inactive-write and DPP update rules; wave32/wave64 native Vulkan readbacks cover relative reads, writes, split-offset swaps, inactive writes, DPP masks and out-of-range accesses, but console comparison and the performance cost of selecting over this span are unverified.
- [User-data callees](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Execution/src/Driver/Shaders/ShaderRegistry.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - a compute shader's `s_swappc_b64` whose source pair is a user-data pair, or a copy of one made with `s_mov_b32` or `s_mov_b64` in the same block, calls the code at the address that user data holds. At dispatch the callee is read from guest memory up to its `s_setpc_b64` on the link pair, one copy of it is appended per call after the caller's last instruction, and the call is rewritten to `s_call_b64` to that copy. The words after the caller's last instruction are dropped; in the shaders seen they are metadata, and a caller that reads its program counter, which could address them, throws. A copy runs at another program counter than the callee, so a callee that reads its program counter, makes a call, ends the program, jumps through another pair, returns before code it branches to, spans more than 64 KiB or is not readable throws. Callees are read again whenever a dispatch misses the dispatch cache; a dispatch that hits it reuses the linked program, so a title that rewrote a callee in place at the same address would keep running the old code.
- [DB_RENDER_CONTROL resummarize draws](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/State.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - HTILE is not modeled, so a RESUMMARIZE_ENABLE draw without depth or stencil tests and without color writes changes no modeled state and draws nothing. A pure resummarize with pass-through D16/D32 depth, no depth writes or bounds test, no stencil plane, no color writes and no running pixel stage also draws nothing; other resummarize, copy or decompress draws throw.
- [s_call_b64](../../core/shader/recompiler/Translation/src/ScalarInstructions.cpp) saves `PC + 4` over `GetShaderBase` and branches to `PC + 4 + signext(SIMM16) * 4`, following [AMD RDNA2 ISA](https://gpuopen.com/download/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) sections 3.6.3, 12.2 and 12.3. Forward callees after `s_endpgm` are decoded. Measured on RDNA2 (gfx1036) in wave32 and wave64 with SIMM16 = 3: the link pair receives `PC + 4`, the branch goes to `PC + 4 + signext(SIMM16) * 4`, and `s_setpc_b64` of the link returns to the instruction after the call. Execution on the PS5 (gfx1013) and title compatibility for these bounded scalar calls remain unverified; `s_rfe_b64` still throws in translation.
- [AVC parameter-set units](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVideodec2/Export.cpp) (libSceVideodec2) - an H.264 access unit with no slice NAL (types 1 to 5), such as the sequence and picture parameter sets Twispero (PPSA99474) submits on their own before a stream's first slices, is kept and put in front of the next unit that has a slice, and `sceVideodec2Decode` returns 0 with no picture for it, since libavcodec refuses a packet without a slice. What the console returns for such a unit is not known. HEVC units of parameter sets alone are still passed to libavcodec as they are
- [sceVideodec2CreateDecoder](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVideodec2/Export.cpp) (libSceVideodec2) decodes HEVC (codec type 974921) and VP9 (2382845) through FFmpeg, besides H.264. The tags come from SharpProspero's `Videodec2CodecType` and EVO-PLAYER-PS5's `sce_videodec2.h`, which records them from hardware runs. HEVC input is converted from length-prefixed NAL units to Annex B as for H.264, and VP9 frames are passed through. Output is the same 8-bit NV12 for every codec; a decoder for HEVC Main10 or VP9 Profile 2 is created, but a frame deeper than 8 bits throws, since the console's 10-bit output layout is unknown, and `QueryDecoderMemoryInfo` reports the 8-bit frame size for it. `sceVideodec2GetPictureInfo` and `sceVideodec2GetAvcPictureInfo` throw for HEVC and VP9 pictures, which use other layouts (`SceVideodec2HevcPictureInfo`, `SceVideodec2Vp9PictureInfo`), and `sceVideodec2GetHevcPictureInfo` is not exported; `sceVideodec2GetVp9PictureInfo` has its own VP9 layout
- [syslog](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Syslog.cpp) (libc) writes each message to `stderr` as `[syslog:<priority>] <message>`, with a newline added when missing, instead of sending it to the console's system log. Priority and facility handling follow FreeBSD's `vsyslog`: `%m` expands to the guest errno's message, unknown bits are reported (as priority 0x23, since FreeBSD passes `LOG_ERR|LOG_CONS|LOG_PERROR|LOG_PID` as the priority) and masked, a missing facility becomes `LOG_USER`, and errno is preserved. `openlog`, `setlogmask` and `closelog` are not implemented, so the identity, options and mask they set are not applied
- [daemon](../../core/libs/prx/libc/src/Process.cpp) (libc) throws: forking into the background is not supported. PPSA85300 imports it and fails to load without it. VLC 3.0 calls it only when its `daemon` option is set ([libvlc.c](https://github.com/videolan/vlc/blob/6de05adcbaf2e8b85fe86aad4169393098628119/src/libvlc.c#L190-L197)), and the title starts libVLC with `--ignore-config` and without `--daemon` ([player.cc](https://github.com/ZiZc3/VLC/blob/f3253020a64368153233ede29183014c0b6c8979/src/player.cc#L960-L981))
- [sigtimedwait](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Signals.cpp) (libkernel) throws: guest signals are delivered when raised and never left pending, so there is nothing to wait for or take. PPSA85300 imports it and fails to load without it. Its only call site is VLC's `vlc_writev` ([filesystem.c](https://github.com/videolan/vlc/blob/6de05adcbaf2e8b85fe86aad4169393098628119/src/posix/filesystem.c#L243-L278)), which polls with a zero timeout to drain a `SIGPIPE` only after `writev` fails with `EPIPE`
- [sigsuspend](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Signals.cpp) (libkernel) swaps the process-wide mask of `_sigprocmask` for its argument, waits as `pause` does through the same helper, then restores the mask through `_sigprocmask`, and returns -1 with `EINTR`; a null set throws, as the console faults in libthr instead of returning `EFAULT`. All four words of the argument replace the mask and are restored afterwards, as in `_sigprocmask`. A caught `SA_SIGINFO` handler still throws, and a default-action signal with no host equivalent stays pending. The wait is not a cancellation point
- [sigaction](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Signals.cpp) (libkernel) records the handler, flags and mask of every signal from 1 to 128 and reports them back, following FreeBSD's `sigaction` (`EINVAL` for other signals, and for a handler on `SIGKILL` or `SIGSTOP`); `signal` records its handler with `SA_RESTART`, as FreeBSD's libc does. Only handlers without `SA_SIGINFO` on the signals `signal` already delivers (`SIGINT`, `SIGILL`, `SIGABRT`, `SIGFPE`, `SIGSEGV`, `SIGTERM`) run, the same way `signal` handlers do; their mask and flags are not applied. `SA_SIGINFO` handlers are recorded but never called, since that needs FreeBSD's `siginfo_t` and `ucontext_t`, and neither are the handlers of other signals (`SIGBUS`, `SIGHUP`, ...), whether they are set by `sigaction` or by `signal`, which records them too (every number from 1 to 128, with `SA_RESTART` and an empty mask replacing what `sigaction` stored; `SIGKILL` and `SIGSTOP` accept only the default handler in both calls, as in FreeBSD's `kern_sigaction`). `raise` of an `SA_SIGINFO` handler on a signal `signal` delivers throws instead of terminating, and `raise` and `kill` of the other signals still fail with `EINVAL`, recorded handler or not
- [kill](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Signals.cpp) (libkernel) follows FreeBSD's `kern_kill` and `killpg1` for the only guest process: `EINVAL` for signals outside 0 to 128, signal 0 only checks that the target exists, and its own pid, 0 and minus its own pid (the guest is taken to lead its own process group) signal it through `raise`, so they share `raise`'s limits above. Any other pid, and -1, which skips the caller, report `ESRCH`, so `EPERM` is never reported
- [sceKernelFcntl](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/Open.cpp) (libkernel) runs the POSIX `fcntl` below and returns its guest errno as `SCE_KERNEL_ERROR_*`, the form the other `sceKernel` file exports use. The return values and error codes are unverified on a console
- [fcntl](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/DescriptorControl.cpp) (libkernel) follows FreeBSD 11.4's [`kern_fcntl`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/kern/kern_descrip.c#L479-L799) and [`kern_dup`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/kern/kern_descrip.c#L812) on file, directory, pipe and character device descriptors; a socket keeps the libSceNet path (`F_GETFL`, `F_SETFL` with `O_NONBLOCK`, `F_GETFD` and `F_SETFD`, `EINVAL` for every other command, see the next entry). The close-on-exec flag and the status flags live in a guest table, the status flags shared by `dup`, `dup2` and `F_DUPFD` as in an open file description; a descriptor the table has not seen, such as 0 to 2, starts from the host's flags, since the console's are not known. `F_GETFL` takes the access mode from the host (the granted access of the handle on Windows), so pipe ends report `O_RDONLY` and `O_WRONLY` where FreeBSD's bidirectional pipes report `O_RDWR`. `F_DUPFD` and `F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC` return the lowest free host descriptor at or above the start, so the bound is the host table (`EMFILE` on Windows, `EINVAL` past `RLIMIT_NOFILE` on Linux), not the console's unknown `RLIMIT_NOFILE` and `kern.maxfilesperproc`. `O_NONBLOCK` switches a host pipe (`PIPE_NOWAIT` on Windows, where an empty read and a full write then report `EAGAIN`) and is only recorded on files, directories and the random device, as their reads never block; on a Windows console or other character device it throws. Changing `O_APPEND` on a Windows file, changing `O_FSYNC` on a file, `O_ASYNC` on a pipe or character device (`SIGIO` is not delivered), `F_GETOWN`, `F_SETOWN` and `F_SETLK_REMOTE` throw; `O_DIRECT`, `F_RDAHEAD` and `F_READAHEAD` are recorded as the hints they are. Record locks (`F_GETLK`, `F_SETLK`, `F_SETLKW` and their forms without `l_sysid`) use the host's POSIX locks on Linux, where `F_GETLK` with `F_UNLCK` throws. On Windows they follow [`kern_fcntl`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/kern/kern_descrip.c#L607-L752) and [`lf_advlockasync`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/kern/kern_lockf.c#L433-L487) for the one guest process, which owns every lock: `EOVERFLOW` for an `SEEK_CUR` start past `OFF_MAX`, then `EBADF` for `F_RDLCK` without read access or `F_WRLCK` without write access and `EINVAL` for another type, then `EINVAL` and `EOVERFLOW` for the range; as [`lf_blocks`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/kern/kern_lockf.c#L866) never lets locks of one owner conflict, whichever descriptor or thread asks, `F_SETLK` and `F_SETLKW` then succeed without waiting and `F_GETLK` reports `F_UNLCK`, so no lock list is kept and merging, splitting and the release on close have nothing to change. Nothing is held on the host, since Windows byte-range locks are mandatory and would fail the guest's own reads and writes through other descriptors, so other host processes neither see these locks nor are seen; a lock from the end of a directory throws. The return values and error codes are unverified on a console
- [fcntl on a guest socket](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Udp.cpp) (libkernel) answers `F_GETFD`, `F_SETFD`, `F_GETFL` and `F_SETFL` and fails every other command with `EINVAL`; FreeBSD 11.4's `kern_fcntl` also duplicates the descriptor (`F_DUPFD`, `F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC`) and handles `F_GETOWN` and `F_SETOWN` on a socket. `FD_CLOEXEC` is only recorded (there is no `exec`): `dup`, `dup2` and an accepted socket start without it, `SOCK_CLOEXEC` and `F_SETFD` set it. None of this was measured on a console.
- [ioctl, _ioctl](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Udp.cpp) (libkernel) handle only `FIONBIO` and `FIONREAD` on a socket, and any other request there fails with `ENOTTY`. On the other descriptors they follow FreeBSD's [`kern_ioctl`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/kern/sys_generic.c#L758), [`vn_ioctl`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/kern/vfs_vnops.c#L1488) and [`pipe_ioctl`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/sys/kern/sys_pipe.c#L1346): `FIONBIO` sets `O_NONBLOCK` as `fcntl` does, `FIOCLEX` and `FIONCLEX` set and clear the close-on-exec flag `F_GETFD` reports, and `FIONREAD` reports the size past the offset of a file, the queued bytes of a pipe's read end, 0 on its write end and `ENOTTY` on the random device, while it throws on a directory or another character device. Every termios request (group `t`, `TIOCGWINSZ` included) fails with `ENOTTY`, since those descriptors are taken as not a terminal, as in `tcgetattr`, and any other request throws. Stremio Plus imports `_ioctl` for its statically linked `tc*` functions, which send `TIOCGETA`, `TIOCSETA`, `TIOCSPGRP` and the other termios requests
- [MSG_DONTWAIT](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Udp.cpp) (libkernel) in `send`, `sendto`, `sendmsg`, `recv`, `recvfrom` and `recvmsg` makes that one call non-blocking through the host's `MSG_DONTWAIT`, as FreeBSD 11.4's `soreceive_generic` returns `EWOULDBLOCK` for it (`sys/kern/uipc_socket.c`). Winsock has no per-call non-blocking flag, so on Windows these calls still fail with `EOPNOTSUPP` for it
- [Regular file mappings](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/PosixMemory.cpp) (libkernel) - on Linux `mmap` maps a regular file's descriptor shared or private like a shared memory object, with the same access checks; SQLite's WAL index (`-shm`) needs it. FreeBSD 11 `mmap(2)` and shadPS4's `MemoryManager::MapFile` map regular files; the console's behaviour is not measured. Other descriptors (pipes, sockets, directories, devices) still fail with `EOPNOTSUPP`, and Windows maps no files.
- [mincore](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/PosixMemory.cpp) (libkernel) follows FreeBSD 11's `sys_mincore` (`sys/vm/vm_mmap.c`), not measured on the console: the address is rounded down to the page, length 0 returns 0, a null vector over a mapped range returns `EFAULT`, an overflowing range or any unmapped page in the range (as reported by `sceKernelVirtualQuery`) returns `ENOMEM`. For valid ranges, a page reports `MINCORE_INCORE` (0x1) when `sceKernelVirtualQuery` reports it committed and 0 when it is only reserved; actual residency, referenced and modified bits are not modelled. Length 0 returns 0 when the start page is mapped and `ENOMEM` otherwise, and the range and mapping checks come before the null vector `EFAULT`, as in `kern_mincore`
- [mlockall, munlockall](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/PosixMemory.cpp) (libkernel) - `mlockall` returns `EINVAL` for zero or unknown flags. Valid `MCL_CURRENT` throws because it would lock host stacks, heaps, modules and driver mappings along with guest memory; `MCL_FUTURE` throws because later mappings are not tracked for locking. FreeBSD 11.4's `sys_munlockall` (`sys/vm/vm_mmap.c`:1133-1165) checks `priv_check(td, PRIV_VM_MUNLOCK)` first (returning `EPERM` without it) and unwires the user map (`vm_map_unwire` over `vm_map_min`...`vm_map_max` with `VM_MAP_WIRE_HOLESOK`). Returning 0 unconditionally assumes the calling process is privileged. `munlockall` unlocks memory locked by `mlock` (`VirtualUnlock` on Windows, tolerating `ERROR_NOT_LOCKED` for pages never locked, and `munlockall` on Linux). On main, XPSemu (PPSA97358) fails to load at these imports (mlockall #4386, munlockall); their calls in QEMU `os-posix.c` execute only when `-overcommit mem-lock=on` is enabled. The call has not been observed in a run with this implementation.
- [Host CPU check](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/HostCpu.cpp) (libkernel) - when libkernel loads on a Linux or Windows x86-64 host, it logs which of the PS5 CPU's user-mode extensions CPUID reports and throws, naming the host CPU, if any extension is missing that LLVM selects from plain C/C++ for the PS5's Zen 2 (`-march=znver2`, [feature list](https://github.com/llvm/llvm-project/blob/llvmorg-19.1.7/llvm/lib/Target/X86/X86.td#L1459-L1513)): SSE to SSE4.2, SSSE3, POPCNT, LZCNT, BMI1, BMI2, MOVBE, CMPXCHG16B, LAHF/SAHF, AVX, AVX2, FMA, F16C, and the operating system's AVX state in XCR0 (#3719). SSE4A, CLZERO and SHA, which `--to-intel` converts, and AES, PCLMULQDQ, RDRAND, RDSEED, ADX, CLFLUSHOPT, CLWB, RDPID, XSAVEOPT, XSAVEC and PREFETCHW, which compilers emit only for intrinsics or explicit prefetches, are logged but not required: a title that executes one of them on a host without it still stops with an illegal instruction. macOS hosts are not checked, because Rosetta runs AVX2, FMA, BMI1/2, F16C and MOVBE without reporting them through CPUID
- [hw.model](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/SystemConfiguration.cpp) (libkernel) reports the [host CPU's name](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/HostCpu.cpp), built as FreeBSD 11 builds `hw.model`: the CPUID brand string (leaves 0x80000002 to 0x80000004) without leading spaces ([identcpu.c](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/release/11.0.0/sys/x86/x86/identcpu.c#L683-L691)). Trailing padding stays, as it does there, so an AMD host's name ends in spaces. The name a PS5 reports is unknown. A write returns -1 with `EPERM`. A host without an x86 CPUID brand string, such as an Apple Silicon Mac, throws. Another title only prints it (`[console] hw.model: %s`)
- [pthread_kill](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/System/src/Signals.cpp) (libkernel) returns the error number like FreeBSD's `pthread_kill`: `EINVAL` for signals outside 0 to 128 and `ESRCH` for a null thread. Signal 0 reports any other thread handle as existing, without checking that the thread is still alive. A signal to the calling thread goes through `raise`, so it shares `raise`'s limits; a signal to another thread throws, since guest signals are not delivered to a chosen thread
- [select](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Socket/src/Socket.cpp) (libkernel) validates `tv_usec` but saturates a valid timeout beyond the remaining `steady_clock` range at `time_point::max()`, so the wait can continue effectively indefinitely. The console's behavior for a valid timeout outside the host clock's range is unknown.
- [AJM](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp), [APR](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Apr/src/Apr.cpp) and [NGS2](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/System.cpp) state is allocated with `new` and never freed on purpose: title threads still call into these libraries while `std::exit` runs static destructors, so ordinary statics would be destroyed under them. Keep them leaked; `guest_ajm_teardown` covers the AJM case on Unix.
- [Comparison-sampled R32 float and R16 unorm textures](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderResources.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) whose surface a storage image holds are sampled from a D32_SFLOAT or D16_UNORM image that the GPU refills from the storage image whenever its version moves, because Vulkan compares only depth formats. Only surfaces of at least 1 MiB (`APS5_DEPTH_COPY_MIN_KIB`) that are not 3D take the copy; smaller ones stay CPU snapshots compared against guest memory. With copies of small surfaces, one title hit an intermittent boot-time crash that was not explained.
- [Null vertex buffer V#](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/include/VertexInput.hpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - a V# whose four dwords are all zero is bound to a zeroed vertex buffer of the attribute's size with stride 0, so every component of its attribute reads 0. This follows the V# fields in the [AMD RDNA2 ISA](https://gpuopen.com/download/rdna2-shader-instruction-set-architecture.pdf) (`NUM_RECORDS` 0, `DST_SEL` 0 selects 0), not measured on hardware.
- PPSA07411 PS VR2 imports declared without parameters, signatures unknown, added without an implementation because the title fails to load without them: [sceHmd2GetDeviceInformationByHandle, sceHmd2GetFieldOfView and sceHmd2Terminate](../../core/libs/prx/libSceHmd2/Export.cpp) (libSceHmd2), [scePadVrControllerSetVibration, scePadVrControllerSetVibrationMode and scePadVrControllerReadCombinedState](../../core/libs/prx/libScePad/Export.cpp) (libScePad) and [sceVrTracker2ResetLocalCoordinate](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVrTracker2/Export.cpp) (libSceVrTracker2)
- [Descriptor pool recycling](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Graphics/src/ShaderResources.cpp) avoids individually freed sets: [RADV 26.2.2](https://gitlab.freedesktop.org/mesa/mesa/-/blob/mesa-26.2.2/src/amd/vulkan/radv_descriptor_pool.c) initializes its free-set heap with 32 bytes beyond the backing allocation. An allocation-only RX 6600 test reproduces acceptance of a 4,128-byte set in a 4,096-byte pool; the path without individual frees rejects it. Pools reset after their last set is released. A cached set can retain unused slots in an exhausted pool until eviction; peak gameplay pool memory remains unmeasured.
- [_fstatfs](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/File/src/Stdio.cpp) (libkernel) fills the FreeBSD 11 `struct statfs` of the PS5 payload SDK with the host volume that holds the file: block size, transfer size, total, free and available blocks, maximum name length and the read-only, `nosuid` and `noexec` flags (on Windows only read-only, and `f_files` and `f_ffree` are 0, as NTFS has no fixed number of files). The filesystem type number and name, mount paths, owner, filesystem id and I/O counters are left 0 or empty, since they would describe a host mount, not a console one. Sockets and pipes return `EINVAL`, as FreeBSD's `getvnode` does; a Windows descriptor that is neither a file nor a pipe throws. Stremio Plus (PPSA74126) reads the free space before a download and only logs the other fields
- [sceKernelGetPageTableStats](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/Export.cpp) (libkernel) reports page-table entries over the recorded protection ranges (committed mappings; reservations are not recorded): each range charges one entry per 2 MiB granule it spans to the cpu pool when it has CPU access bits (0x7) and to the gpu pool when it has GPU access bits (0x30), so a range with both counts in both and two ranges sharing one granule charge it once each, as KytyPS5 sums per committed range. Mappings with no access bits, such as PROT_NONE or ACP-only protections, charge neither pool. The 2048-entry budget per side, its constants, and whether the console counts reservations or guest image segments are unverified. A title reaches this call after the other missing exports of its start-up are provided
- [fstatvfs](../../core/libs/prx/libSceLibcInternal/FilesystemStatistics.cpp) (libSceLibcInternal) converts `_fstatfs` to the 88-byte `struct statvfs` as FreeBSD 9.2's `sfs2svfs` does (`f_bsize` from the transfer size, `f_frsize` from the block size, `f_favail` equal to `f_ffree`, `f_fsid` 0, `ST_RDONLY` and `ST_NOSUID` only). Differences: `f_namemax` is the `statfs` value, not `fpathconf(_PC_NAME_MAX)`; negative `f_bavail` and `f_ffree` wrap to unsigned without `EOVERFLOW`, as the same-width copy in FreeBSD does; inode counts are 0 on Windows, inherited from `_fstatfs`; a null output returns `EFAULT` after the descriptor is validated, where FreeBSD would fault. Layout and conversion are unverified against the PS5 library (PS5MC imports `fstatvfs` but was not run)
- [sceVoiceQoSEnd](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVoiceQoS/Export.cpp) (libSceVoiceQoS) - `0x804E0901` (not initialized) before any initialization was measured on a PS5 (firmware 13.60, #1407); ending an initialized library was not, because a valid `sceVoiceQoSInit` never returns inside a homebrew payload. PPSA14632 calls it at shutdown and PPSA26344 after a nonzero `sceVoiceQoSInit`. After an initialization it returns 0 and clears the init flag, so the library can be initialized again. Whether the console also ends the libSceVoice instance that its `sceVoiceQoSInit` starts ([prosper](https://github.com/mattias800/prosper)'s reading of the module), and what it answers while endpoints exist, is unknown; no endpoint is implemented
- [sceSharePlayGetCurrentConnectionInfoA](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSharePlay/Export.cpp) (libSceSharePlay) - reports no Share Play session, as the library keeps none: status 0 (DORMANT), mode 0, empty online IDs, account IDs 0 and both user IDs -1 (`SCE_USER_SERVICE_USER_ID_INVALID`). A null pointer returns `SCE_SHARE_PLAY_ERROR_INVALID_ARGS` (0x810E0001). The 72-byte `SceSharePlayConnectionInfoA` layout and the error code follow [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/main/src/libs/libNet.cpp) and [fpPS4](https://github.com/red-prig/fpPS4/blob/trunk/src/ps4_libsceshareplay.pas), which agree on both; KytyPS5 sets the user IDs to -1 and fpPS4 leaves them 0, and which one the console returns is not verified. KytyPS5 also returns `SCE_SHARE_PLAY_ERROR_NOT_INITIALIZED` before `sceSharePlayInitialize`; this library keeps no initialization state and does not.
- [sceAgcDcbSetWorkloadsActive and sceAgcDcbSetWorkloadComplete](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgc/DcbState/src/Workload.cpp) (libSceAgc) and [sceAgcDriverRegisterWorkloadStream](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAgcDriver/Resource/src/Registration.cpp) (libSceAgcDriver) - the driver call records only the stream id (1 to 31; it returns 0 for any argument and keeps no stream record), and the DCB calls write the packets [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/main/src/libs/agc.cpp) writes: an 18-dword (active) or 12-dword (complete) `IT_NOP` packet with the stream id, and for active the 64-bit mask of the workload ids, for complete the workload id and the 64-bit clear mask of that id. Both return null for a null command buffer, an unregistered or out-of-range stream, a workload id above 63, and for active a duplicate id or a count of 0 or above 63. The layouts and checks come from KytyPS5 alone; the console oracle in [prosper](https://github.com/mattias800/prosper/blob/main/prosper/tests/data/console_oracle/agcbuild.golden.tsv) measures the null return only for unregistered streams, not the packet contents of a registered one. The error codes the console driver returns for an invalid or repeated registration are unknown, so registration never fails here. PPSA29866 calls sceAgcDcbSetWorkloadsActive while building its command buffers (sceAgcDcbSetWorkloadComplete was not observed); nothing else was observed.
- One tested title's imports declared without parameters, signatures unknown, added without an implementation because the title fails to load without them: [sceSslCreateConnection, sceSslDeleteConnection, sceSslEnableVerifyOption, sceSslConnect, sceSslRead, sceSslWrite](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSsl/Export.cpp) (libSceSsl), [four sce::Json::Value members (setElementAccessFailureHandler, setSpecialFloatHandler, setTypeMismatchHandler, operator bool)](../../core/libs/prx/libSceJson2/Export.cpp) (libSceJson2), [sceConvertKeycodeGetCharacter](../../core/libs/prx/libSceConvertKeycode/Export.cpp) (libSceConvertKeycode) and [sceNpSessionSignalingGetGroupFromSessionId](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNpSessionSignaling/Export.cpp) (libSceNpSessionSignaling)
- [sceSaveDataMount3](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSaveData.native/Export.cpp) (libSceSaveData.native) - a failed mount (BUSY, EXISTS, NOT_FOUND, MOUNT_FULL) leaves the result as it was, as shadPS4 does; not checked on hardware. A title can check for its save with a mount it never unmounts, then save with a second mount of the same directory, ignore the BUSY and keep using the first mount point
- [sceSaveDataMount3](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSaveData.native/Export.cpp) (libSceSaveData.native) - an unterminated or empty directory name, `.`, `..`, or a name containing `/`, `\` or `:` returns `SAVE_DATA_ERROR_PARAMETER`, which the [SDK 12.000 reference](https://github.com/peterpuppy/bm/blob/3f9d1ba6b896a53080460c903f5c15af8affb80d/features/ps5/document/output/psn_12/System/Save_Data/SaveData-Reference/sce-save-data-mount3.md#L196-L204) lists for an invalid parameter, and leaves the result as it was, as [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/569ec04d1a77127c601eed522ad5bba187173f9d/src/libs/libSaveData.cpp#L652-L658) does. The [SDK](https://github.com/peterpuppy/bm/blob/3f9d1ba6b896a53080460c903f5c15af8affb80d/features/ps5/document/output/psn_12/System/Save_Data/SaveData-Reference/sce-save-data-dir-name.md#L32-L36) lets an application use only letters, digits, `-`, `.` and `@` in a name, and KytyPS5 also accepts `_` ([`valid_path_component`](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/569ec04d1a77127c601eed522ad5bba187173f9d/src/libs/libSaveData.cpp#L276-L287)); which other names the console's mount rejects is not checked on hardware
- [sceSaveDataDirNameSearch, sceSaveDataDirNameSearch2](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSaveData.native/Export.cpp) (libSceSaveData.native) - only the running title's saves are kept, so a search with another title's `title_id` reports zero hits, as [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/ec334904604ba41f5d4753f4a2498ec82540e5d6/src/core/libraries/save_data/savedata.cpp#L791-L802) and [KytyPS5](https://github.com/KytyPS5/KytyPS5/blob/569ec04d1a77127c601eed522ad5bba187173f9d/src/libs/libSaveData.cpp#L592-L596) do when that title has no save directory; what a console returns for another title's saves is not verified. The running title's ID is the `titleId` in its `param.json`
- [getservbyname](../../core/libs/prx/libScePosixForWebKit/Resolver.cpp) (libScePosixForWebKit) searches a built-in copy of 37 lines of FreeBSD 11.4's [etc/services](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/etc/services) (ftp-data, ftp, ssh, telnet, smtp, domain, tftp, http, pop3, ntp, imap, https, rtsp, submission, imaps, pop3s with their aliases and protocols, in file order) the way [getservent.c](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/8c639945a44588f6cfdddfb057bb5aad88735da0/lib/libc/net/getservent.c) searches the file: case-sensitive name or alias, a null protocol takes the first match, the port in network byte order, the result in per-thread storage. The console's `/etc/services` is unknown, so any other service returns null without setting `errno`, as for a name the file lacks. `getaddrinfo` still resolves service names through the host's service database
- [sceCesUcsProfileInitSJis1997Cp932](../../core/libs/prx/libSceCesCs/Export.cpp) (libSceCesCs) - returns the sheet argument without writing profile tables to it, and returns null for a null pointer; Shift-JIS conversion tables are unimplemented. The signature `union* sceCesUcsProfileInitSJis1997Cp932(SceCesUcsProfileSheet* sheet)` follows the [Orbital IDA type database](https://github.com/AlexAltea/orbital/blob/master/tools/ida/db_types.json)
- [sceOpusDecDecode](../../core/libs/prx/libSceOpusDec/Export.cpp) (libSceOpusDec) returns exactly the packet's duration, as libopus does. FFmpeg resamples narrowband SILK from 8 kHz and holds back 24 samples of the first narrowband packet after the decoder starts or after a packet of another bandwidth, releasing them with the next packet that is not narrowband. The decoder keeps FFmpeg's output in order and pads the start with silence, so such a packet begins with 24 samples (0.5 ms) of silence and the output after it is 24 samples late; libopus has no such gap
- NGS2 custom sampler rack `0x4001` (libSceNgs2.native) accepts built-in module `0x13` (Custom Voice Filter) and routes `0x40001300` Biquad parameters (identified from title logs and shadPS4's mapping), but throws for any filter type other than 1.
- [sceVideodec2GetVp9PictureInfo](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVideodec2/Export.cpp) uses the 88-byte layout and two-pointer signature from [SharpProspero Videodec2.cs](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/5dfdb4b0b166e83dc9ae275001afb695f12c7174/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Video/Videodec2.cs). Frame metadata follows the [VP9 specification](https://storage.googleapis.com/downloads.webmproject.org/docs/vp9/vp9-bitstream-specification-v0.7-20170222-draft.pdf), sections 5.26 and 6.2: profile, key/intra-only flags, bit depth, color space/range, coded/render dimensions and superframe count are retained per emitted buffer. Level is 0 because the elementary stream does not signal a level; the console value is unverified. Null pointers, undersized structures and querying another codec throw; console error codes and support for other structure sizes are unknown. Existing NV12 output rejects pictures deeper than 8 bits. Superframes are submitted as individual coded frames to keep each output associated with its input metadata.
- [sceVideodec2Decode](../../core/libs/prx/libSceVideodec2/Export.cpp) (libSceVideodec2) and `sceVideodec2Flush` output the coded picture, with the chroma plane after its luma rows, as the console does for 1080p H.264 and HEVC streams coded at 1920x1088. An HEVC or VP9 picture whose size is not a multiple of 16, such as HEVC coded at 1920x1080, is output at that size; whether the console pads it is unverified. `sceVideodec2GetPictureInfo` reports no frame cropping
- [sceConvertKeycodeGetCharacter](../../core/libs/prx/libSceConvertKeycode/Export.cpp) (libSceConvertKeycode) - the signature `(uint16_t keycode, uint32_t, uint32_t keyboardType, char16_t* character)` is inferred from PPSA12361, which passes a 16-bit keycode, 0, the type from `sceConvertKeycodeGetImeKeyboardType` and a pointer it reads one UTF-16 unit from when the result is 0. The meaning of the second argument is unknown. PRAGMATA (PPSA02530, #5726) and Onimusha: Way of the Sword (PPSA27836) pass `0x4000000` as a constant: Onimusha calls it for every keycode from 0 to 0x8F to build a table of key names, upper-cases `a` to `z` itself and skips keycodes that fail or return 0, so that bit is ignored and the unshifted character is returned as for 0. Any other bit throws, and so does a keyboard type other than 0.
- One tested title's imports declared without parameters, signatures unknown, added without an implementation because the title fails to load without them: [sceSslEnableVerifyOption](../../core/libs/prx/libSceSsl/Export.cpp) (libSceSsl), [four sce::Json::Value members (setElementAccessFailureHandler, setSpecialFloatHandler, setTypeMismatchHandler, operator bool)](../../core/libs/prx/libSceJson2/Export.cpp) (libSceJson2), [sceConvertKeycodeGetCharacter](../../core/libs/prx/libSceConvertKeycode/Export.cpp) (libSceConvertKeycode) and [sceNpSessionSignalingGetGroupFromSessionId](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNpSessionSignaling/Export.cpp) (libSceNpSessionSignaling)
- [sceAudioOut2EnableChat](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut2System.cpp) (libSceAudioOut) takes no arguments and returns 0, as declared by [SharpProspero](https://github.com/SvenGDK/SharpProspero/blob/5dfdb4b0b166e83dc9ae275001afb695f12c7174/src/SharpProspero/Interop/Audio/AudioOut2.cs#L460-L464) ("Allows chat audio alongside the application's own"). The host mixes no chat audio and AudioOut2 has no chat port, so the call only records that chat audio is allowed. Whether the console fails it without a chat session, when repeated or before `sceAudioOut2Initialize` is unknown. [sceAudioOut2DisableChat](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAudioOut/src/AudioOut2System.cpp) is the counterpart, imported by one tested title: it takes no arguments, returns 0 and records that chat audio is no longer allowed; its signature is not confirmed by a console measurement, and whether the console fails it without a chat session or when repeated is unknown.
- [AJM M4AAC decoder](../../core/libs/prx/libSceAjm.native/src/Ajm.cpp) (libSceAjm.native, codec 2) - one tested title initializes it through `sceAjmBatchJobControl`. The initialize parameters (`u32` config type, 1 ADTS or 2 raw, then `u32` sampling frequency index), instance flags (channels in bits 3-6, `ENABLE_SBR_DECODE` bit 32, `ENABLE_NONDELAY_OUTPUT` bit 33, surround interleave order bits 34-35), `{heaac, reserved}` codec info and decoder error result (`CODEC_ERROR | FATAL`) follow [shadPS4](https://github.com/shadps4-emu/shadPS4/blob/443f022450f6857256e780daa3d5a14029a748f7/src/core/libraries/ajm/ajm_aac.cpp) (PS4, fdk-aac) and are unverified on PS5; decoding uses FFmpeg's AAC decoder.
