Without `ENABLE_NONDELAY_OUTPUT`, the first two frames after initialization or reset are consumed without output. Raw input takes its channel configuration from the instance (0 means stereo) and is decoded as one access unit per run job. The format reports the last frame's bitrate. Invalid config type or raw sampling frequency index above 11 returns invalid-parameter result 4. The implementation throws for ADTS without a frame header, profiles other than AAC LC, multiple raw data blocks per ADTS frame, unsupported channel configurations, more channels than the instance supports, HE-AAC without `ENABLE_SBR_DECODE`, raw input with `RUN_MULTIPLE_FRAMES`, output room smaller than the previous frame length, and unknown codec flags.
- Controller haptic PCM drives Xbox-style motor rumble from its per-channel RMS (large/small motor, scale 2x, below 0.01 ignored, 80 ms hold); the mapping approximates waveform energy and is not measured against console hardware
- [rtprio_thread, pthread_setconcurrency, pthread_getconcurrency](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Pthread/Posix/Thread.cpp) (libkernel) - `rtprio_thread` follows FreeBSD 11.4's `rtprio_thread(2)`. Every guest thread here runs under `SCHED_FIFO` or `SCHED_RR` (priority 256-767), whose rtprio class is real-time; the console's mapping from that priority to the rtprio `prio` range (0-31) is unmeasured, so `RTP_LOOKUP` and `RTP_SET` throw for such a thread rather than report an impossible priority. A thread that is not real-time reports `RTP_PRIO_NORMAL` (3) with 0, and `RTP_SET` accepts only that value. Another function fails with `EINVAL`, a null `rtprio` with `EFAULT`, a thread other than the calling one throws, and an id that is not a live guest thread with `ESRCH`. `pthread_setconcurrency` stores 0 or more and returns `EINVAL` for a negative level (the error itself, as FreeBSD's libthr does, with errno untouched); `pthread_getconcurrency` returns the level, which is a hint and changes nothing about the host's concurrency. Morrowind (`mshivam019/morrowind-ps5`) imports all three at start-up
- [sceKernelVirtualQuery](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/DirectMemory/Export.cpp) returns [VirtualQueryInfo](../../core/libs/SceTypes.hpp) with a one-byte flag allocation unit so Windows and SysV hosts return the same 72-byte representation, with the name at byte 33 and GPU mask at byte 65. This preserves the existing SysV representation; the console offsets beyond the address, protection and size fields observed in Astro Bot's AGC calls are not hardware-measured. Size and offset assertions plus a raw-byte named-mapping regression guard the host ABI.
- [kqueue and kevent](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Equeue/src/Kqueue.cpp) (libkernel) follow FreeBSD 11's [`kern_event.c`](https://github.com/freebsd/freebsd-src/blob/34bd34750187bae02a0086402c78c031c59dd8b3/sys/kern/kern_event.c) (`kqueue_kevent`, `kqueue_register`, `kqueue_scan`, the timer and user filters) over the event queue; nothing is measured on a console. The descriptor is a host null device registered as the kqueue: `close` and `sceKernelClose` close the queue and `dup`/`dup2` of it throw, but `read`, `write` and `fstat` act on the null device instead of failing with `EOPNOTSUPP` or reporting `S_IFIFO`. Read and write events are supported on guest sockets only, plus the timer, user and [vnode](../../core/libs/prx/libkernel/Equeue/src/FileEvent.cpp) filters; other filters, `NOTE_LOWAT`, `EV_CLEAR` on a socket, `EVFILT_READ` on another kqueue, a `NOTE_NSECONDS` period above one second with a fraction (11.0 converts the fraction with the microsecond macro) and any change to an existing timer (11.0 keeps the armed expiry and applies the new period after it, 11.4's `filt_timertouch` restarts it) throw. `EV_DISABLE`, `EV_DISPATCH` and `EV_FORCEONESHOT` follow kern_event.c: a disabled knote stays registered, stops polling or firing and is not reported, a trigger raised while it is disabled is kept and reported after `EV_ENABLE`, where `knote` would drop it, `EV_DISPATCH` reports once and then disables the knote, and `EV_FORCEONESHOT` is ignored on a new knote while on an existing one it sets `EV_ONESHOT` and fires the knote; a negative `nevents` fails with `EINVAL`. A new user event keeps its `fflags` unmasked and is not triggered by `NOTE_TRIGGER`, because `filt_userattach` reads the already cleared `kn_fflags`. The timer limit `kq_calloutmax` (`ENOMEM`) is not enforced, and closing a kqueue wakes a thread waiting on it in `kevent` with `EBADF`, where `kqueue_drain` makes the close wait for that thread. A level-triggered event is reported once per call and moved behind the other events, as in `kqueue_scan`; the `sceKernel*Equeue` calls keep their own collection loop
- [NGS2 render buffer capacity](../../core/libs/prx/libSceNgs2.native/src/Render.cpp) is treated as available storage for one configured grain. PPSA01880 passes 303104 bytes for 256 stereo float frames (2048 bytes); rendering writes or clears only those frames and preserves the remaining capacity. Non-null silent outputs now undergo the same supported-format and minimum-size checks as audible outputs, with channel counts limited to 1 through 8. Unused null outputs remain ignored. The console's handling of surplus capacity and invalid silent output descriptors has not been measured; invalid descriptors throw rather than return guessed SDK errors.
